Examples: "This request isn't open any more.", "This offer has already been answered.", "There isn't enough space left on this journey.", "Your identity check needs attention before you can do this.", "Your reserved space expired.", "This delivery is closed."
- Buttons everywhere: "Try again", "Go back", "Contact support".

## How does a traveler verify identity?
- Profile shows a Verification card with status: **Not started**, **Being reviewed**, **Verified**, **Not approved**. Button "Start identity check" (or "Try again" after a rejection). Home also links to it for travelers.
- Screen "Identity check": choose Document type (**National ID card**, **Passport**, **Driving licence**), add Front of document, Back of document (not needed for a passport: "A passport only needs its photo page."), and a Selfie. Photos: JPEG or PNG, under 8 MB each. Button "Submit for review".
- Messages: "Documents received. We'll review them shortly."; pending "We're reviewing your documents. This usually takes less than a day."; approved "You're verified. You can publish journeys and carry parcels."; rejected "We couldn't verify your documents. You can submit again."; unavailable "Verification is temporarily unavailable. Please try again shortly."
- Verification is needed to publish a journey (error "You need to be verified before you can publish a journey."). Saving a draft journey does not require it. Senders are never asked for an identity check: the server checks identity only for travelers (publishing a journey, and again when an offer on that journey is made or accepted).

## How does a sender post a parcel request?
Home > "Send a parcel" (or Deliveries > Sending section). Four steps: **Route**, **Parcel**, **Timing**, **Review**. Next is blocked until the step is valid; a refused server field is marked on its step ("The server refused this request. The problem is on this step, marked below.").
1. **Route**: "Pickup from" and "Deliver to" (search localities and airports, or choose on map; countries offered: Algeria, France, Spain, Germany). Pickup and delivery must differ. A preferred meeting point is optional ("Decide later"). Only the city is shared until the delivery is paid.
2. **Parcel**: item photo (required; JPEG, PNG or WebP, up to 10 MB; "From gallery" / "Take a photo"), title "What are you sending?" (max 160 characters), Description (max 2000), Category (Documents, Small box, Electronics, Clothing, Something else), Weight (0.01 to 100 kg), Size length/width/height in cm (optional, but all three or none; "We charge on whichever is greater" weight or size), Declared value (EUR, what it would cost to replace), Handling notes, Fragile switch ("Handle with care").
3. **Timing**: Ready from / Ready until (a window), "Must arrive by" (deadline, must be in the future and not before the window closes; the date picker allows up to 365 days ahead).
4. **Review** ("Check this over"): the proposed reward ("What you propose to pay", a starting point; shown against a recommended and a minimum price, errors like "Offer must be at least X"), live totals (Traveler receives, Wassla fee, Total sender cost), and five required tick boxes: description accurate, item legal, no prohibited goods, declared value accurate, customs responsibility understood ("Confirm all five before you post."). Link "See what can't be sent". Button "Post this request".
- A request is created with no Boost. After posting, if a posting deposit is due the sender goes to "Publish your request" (deposit screen); otherwise to "Your request".
- Prices, deposit size and fee come from the pricing file; the app only displays what the server quotes ("Updating the price" while loading).

## What is the posting deposit and how does the sender pay it?
- Request status `awaiting_deposit` shows as **Deposit needed**; travelers cannot see it ("Travelers can't see this yet. Pay the deposit to publish it.").
- Screen "Publish your request": explainer "Paid now to list your request. 100% credited toward your final delivery payment." Presets: "Minimum (amount)", "Recommended (amount)", "Pay in full (amount)", "Custom". Limits: "The smallest deposit is X" / "That's more than the whole amount of X". Button "Pay deposit and publish".
- Refund: "If nobody takes it, or you cancel before accepting an offer, you get it back in full." Some requests need no deposit: "No deposit needed".
- After payment: "Your request is now published" and "Find a traveler going your way and send them an offer."
- Request statuses in the app: Deposit needed, **Finding a traveler** (`open`), Matched, On the way, Delivered, Completed, Cancelled, Expired.
- Cancel a request: "Cancel request" > "Cancel this request? It stops being visible to travelers. Any deposit you paid comes back to you." Not possible once a traveler is matched ("A traveler is already matched with this request, so it can't be cancelled here."); then cancel from the delivery.

## How does a sender find a traveler and make an offer?
- On "Your request" tap "Find travelers" (screen "Travelers for this parcel"). Each card shows route fit ("Excellent route match", "Good route match", "Compatible route"), timing ("Arrives with time to spare", "Fits your delivery window"), room ("Has room for X kg"), badges ("Identity verified", "Flight ticket verified", "Ferry ticket verified"), rating or "New", deliveries count, detour ("Barely a detour", "Small detour", "Noticeable detour"), the traveler's listed price if any, and transfers. Sort: **Best match**, **Soonest trip**, **Lowest price**. "Show more travelers" pages more.
- Empty: "No travelers going your way yet / Your request stays active. We'll tell you as soon as somebody posts a trip along your route." If the request cannot be matched the reason is shown: pay the deposit, already has a traveler, closed, already on its way.
- A traveler can also be found from Browse: "Trips leaving soon" with filters From / To / From date and "Clear filters". Tapping a trip shows "You propose first. Nothing is booked from here." and "Choose one of your parcels".
- The sender always proposes first. Screen "Make an offer": "You choose what the traveler earns. They can accept, decline, or come back with a different amount." Field "Traveler's reward", "Use suggested", button "Send offer" (or "Offer {amount}" when the traveler listed a price; "Use their price" / "Offer a different amount"; below their price: "That is below their price, so they may decline or counter."). Below the minimum: "Offer at least X". Sent: "Offer sent".
- If the trip no longer fits the app says why: "There isn't enough space left on every leg.", "It would arrive after the deadline.", "The dates don't line up.", "The detour from the route is too large.", "One of them is no longer open.", "The traveler's verification isn't complete.", "This item can't be carried on this trip.", "The traveler has paused this trip.", "The traveler doesn't take handovers at the places you need.", "The traveler doesn't take this kind of item.", "There isn't enough room left for this parcel's size.", "The traveler has no parcel places left.", "A ferry line on this trip isn't available right now."
- A traveler browsing parcels can tap "I can carry this", pick a trip, and the sender gets one notification. It is not an offer: no price, nothing reserved. Daily limit message: "You have reached today's limit for "I can carry this". Try again tomorrow." Default limit 10 per 24 hours (setting).

## How do propose, accept, counter and decline work?
- Offers live in Deliveries under "Open offers" and on the negotiation screen (history under "Offer history").
- Who moves: "Your move" / "Waiting for the traveler" / "Waiting for the sender". Statuses: **Waiting**, **Accepted**, **Declined**, **Withdrawn**, **Expired**.
- Buttons: **Accept**, **Decline** (confirm "Decline this offer? The other side will be told. You can still negotiate afterwards."), **Counter** ("Send counter-offer"; traveler: "Choose how much you receive for this delivery."), **Withdraw**.
- Accept confirmation: sender "Pay {amount} for this delivery?" (then asked to pay); traveler "Receive {amount} for this delivery?" ("Space on your journey is reserved as soon as you accept. The sender then has to pay.").
- Deadline: each proposal has an answer-by time. Default 6 hours (marketplace > offers > response_hours, 1 to 168 hours, can be switched off) with a reminder 1 hour before. The app shows "Answer by {time}", then in the last hour a minutes countdown, then "This proposal expired / No answer came in time. Nothing was reserved or charged." with "Propose to another traveler" or "Browse parcels".
- Money breakdown ("How this adds up"): Base delivery reward, Boost bonus (if any), Traveler receives, Wassla fee, Boost fee, total "You pay". "The traveler receives this full amount. The Wassla fee is added on top, not taken out of it." If amounts changed: "The amounts on this offer changed. Check the new figures before accepting."
- Boost: on a live request the sender can "Add Boost" (presets none, +EUR 5, +EUR 10, custom). "Travelers receive 100% of the Boost bonus. Boosted requests appear higher in traveler search." Boost never overrides compatibility. Cannot change once an offer is accepted or the request expired.

## How does the sender pay for a delivery?
- When an offer is accepted the deal is created as `payment_required` ("Payment needed"). The delivery screen shows "Pay before {time} or the space is released" and "Pay now". The space is held for 1 hour by default (3,600 s, Settings: reservation payment grace).
- Payment screen: "How would you like to pay?" **Stripe** (Visa, Mastercard and other cards) or **Chargily** (Algerian cards, CIB and Edahabia). Unavailable options say why: "Not available yet", "Temporarily switched off", "Not ready yet", "Below this method's minimum", "Not taking new payments right now".
- Chargily shows "Equivalent to {amount}", "Rate: EUR 1 = {rate} DA"; "The rate is locked when you start the payment. The delivery price stays in euros." The DZD figure is calculated by the server from EUR. Button "Pay {amount} with {provider}".
- The user is sent to the provider's secure page. Coming back is not proof: "We confirm every payment with the provider before marking it paid." The app shows "Checking your payment" and re-checks on a back-off schedule (3, 3, 5, 5, 10, 10, 20, 20, 20 seconds, about two minutes), then "We're still confirming your payment ... you won't be charged twice" with "Check again".
- If a checkout is open: "A checkout is already open / Continue your payment". Failed: "Payment didn't go through / Nothing was charged." Cancelled: "Payment cancelled". None available: "No payment method available / Payment is temporarily unavailable."
- A posting deposit already paid is shown as "Deposit already paid" and subtracted: "Remaining to pay". Paid in full: "Paid in full", "Payment protected". If more is owed: "Pay remaining {amount}"; "The traveler can't collect the parcel until it's paid."
- Paid statuses: deal **Paid and protected** (`funded`). Payment states: Payment needed, Paid, Refund on the way, Partly refunded, Refunded. A failed payment leaves the deal in `payment_required` so the sender can try again (no code sets `payment_failed`).
- Someone else can pay: "Someone else can pay" > "Create payment link" > "Share link" / "Copy link". The payer needs no account, enters an email for the receipt, and pays. The link only pays that amount. Link states: ready, expires {when}, "Someone is paying now", expired, revoked ("Revoke link"), "Payment received". Creating a new link stops the earlier one.
- Chat opens only after payment is confirmed ("Chat opens once a delivery is paid for.").

## What does the sender do after paying (recipient, tracking)?
- Delivery screen steps: Terms agreed, Payment held, Recipient added, Parcel collected, Delivered, Protected payment, Completed.
- "Add recipient" ("Who's receiving this?"): Recipient's name, email (the delivery code is emailed here), language, optional phone and note. "Save recipient". Required before pickup ("Add the recipient before pickup so we can send them the delivery code.").
- Exact addresses and handover notes appear only once the deal is paid: before that "Exact address available after payment". Route section: "The travel route appears here once the delivery is funded." Shown as "Frozen at booking" or "Live journey".
- Tracking number: label "Tracking number" with a copy button.
- Traveler progress shown to sender (marked "Reported by the traveler"): "Your traveler is at the port", "Your traveler has departed", "Your traveler has arrived". These never move money.
- Button "Message" opens the chat. Deal statuses: Offer accepted, Payment needed, Paid and protected, Ready for pickup, Picked up, On the way, Ready to deliver, Delivered, Payment protected, Completed, Cancelled, Under dispute, Refunded, Partly refunded, Payment failed, Expired.

## How do pickup and delivery codes work in the app?
- **Pickup**: the sender taps "Pickup" > "Show pickup code" and says it in person ("Don't send this code in a message."). The traveler taps "Pickup", enters the "Pickup code" and "Confirm pickup". Result: "Pickup confirmed / You're carrying this parcel now."
- **Delivery code**: locked for 30 minutes after pickup ("Available in {countdown}", "For safety, the delivery code stays locked for 30 minutes after pickup. The recipient hasn't been emailed yet either."). After that Wassla emails the code to the recipient and the sender can "Show delivery code". The traveler is never shown it: "Only the recipient has this code. Ask them for it when you arrive." The traveler taps "Delivery", enters "Delivery code", "Confirm delivery". Result: "Delivery confirmed / Thank you. Your payout is being prepared."
- Code length is 8 characters (Crockford base32) by default. The app names no number in its wording, because the length is an admin setting. 5 wrong attempts then a 15 minute lockout, up to 3 lockouts, 12 attempts per hour (seeded settings, handover section).
- Wrong code: "That code isn't right", "{n} attempts left". Locked: "Too many wrong attempts / Try again {when}." "Get a new code" (confirm "Issue a new code? The code you already shared will stop working immediately.") is for the sender only: the pickup code can be replaced before pickup (deal funded or ready for pickup), the delivery code only after the 30-minute lock and before delivery is confirmed.
- If the sender has not added the recipient: "The sender hasn't added the recipient yet. Ask them to do that, then try the code again."
- Chat shows "Never send a pickup or delivery code in chat."
- "I arrived early" (traveler): "Report early arrival" notifies the sender, who can "Confirm arrival" or "Decline". It does not confirm delivery or release payout earlier: "Arriving early does not make the payout available earlier than the protected payout date."

## What does the sender see during the protection window?
- After delivery: status Delivered then "Payment protected": "Your payment is held until {when}. If something's wrong with the delivery, open a dispute before then." Protection is 48 hours after delivery confirmation (setting, default 172800 seconds). Traveler: "Your payout is released after {when}, once the protection window closes."
- "Wassla releases them only after that period ends and no dispute is open."

## How does a traveler add a journey, legs, tickets and a price?
Home > "Add a journey" (or Deliveries > "Post a trip"). Screen "Add a journey".
- **Your stops**: add where you start, where you end, and stops between ("Add a stop here"; move earlier/later, remove). Wassla works out the legs between stops. A stop cannot repeat the stop before it. Max 20 legs.
- Per leg: mode **Flight**, **Drive**, or **Ferry** (ferry only if staff switched sea legs on; off by default: "Ferry legs are switched off for now."). Algeria to Europe cannot be driven, so it must be a flight and the app asks "Which airport?". Ferry needs port cities and an existing line.
- Per leg: Departs, Arrives (arrival time is required to publish; the sender deadline is checked against it), Space you can carry in kg (0.01 to 20,000, two decimals; the top limit only catches typos), optional volume in litres (1 to 200,000) and number of parcels (1 to 10,000), optional flight number. Leg times must be in order and each leg must depart after the previous arrives.
- Per stop "Handover options": takes pickups and/or drop-offs, optional time window, note (only the traveler sees it until a delivery using the stop is paid, then that sender sees it).
- "What you carry": switch off item kinds you won't take; fragile can be refused; at least one kind must stay on.
- Notes: "Anything senders should know". Button "Save journey" saves a **Draft** ("Nothing is visible to senders yet").
- **Tickets**: every flight and ferry leg needs an approved ticket. On the journey page "Tickets to check" > "Upload proof" (Ticket, Boarding pass or Booking; JPEG, PNG, WebP up to 10 MB). Proof status: Not uploaded, Being reviewed, Approved, Not accepted (with "Reason: ..."). Driving legs need none.
- **Publish**: button "Publish journey" needs identity verified and all tickets approved ("Every flight and ferry leg needs an approved ticket before this can go live."). Then "Your journey is live." Journey statuses: Draft, Being checked, **Live**, Under way, Completed, Cancelled, Expired.
- **Your price** (optional, if enabled): "Price per kg" and "Minimum per parcel" (fill both or none). "Senders see your price plus Wassla's fee. Wassla's minimum for each parcel still applies." Defaults for limits: max EUR 100.00 per kg and EUR 500.00 minimum (setting marketplace > traveler_pricing). A new price applies to offers made from then on. Without a price: "No price set. Senders propose their own amount."
- **Edit**: only while the journey is a Draft (or Being checked), never once published ("This journey can no longer be edited because it is already published. Cancel it and create a new one if the route has changed."), and not if a sender is already proposing on it or a delivery is attached. Editing a flight leg's places, number or times sends its approved ticket back to review.
- **Pause / Resume**: "Pause journey": senders stop finding it, pending offers cannot be accepted, paid deliveries carry on. If staff paused it: "Wassla paused this journey. Contact support to have it resumed." with a "Contact support" button (just "Wassla paused this journey." while the server has no support address).
- **Cancel journey**: "Senders stop seeing it and any space they were holding is released. This can't be undone." Refused if a paid delivery depends on it ("A paid delivery is riding on this journey.").
- **Change times** on a live trip: "Change times", review, "Send new times". Senders who must agree (new arrival after their deadline, arrival moves more than the allowed margin, pickup moves) answer; default margin 360 minutes (setting marketplace > route_change). Senders see "Accept the new times" / "Decline"; a decline lets them cancel and "that cancellation is not counted as yours". Traveler can "Withdraw the new times". Others are just told.
- **Progress reports**: "I'm at the port", "I've departed", "I've arrived", opens 24 hours before the planned time, undo within 10 minutes; needs a connection ("Nothing is saved for later."). Reminders: 30 min after planned departure, 60 min after arrival, repeat every 180 min up to 2 times (settings, marketplace > reminders).

## How does a traveler answer proposals and carry the parcel?
- Offers appear in Deliveries > "Open offers" and as "An offer is waiting for your answer". Accept / Decline / Counter as above. Travelers cannot offer on parcels ("Senders make the first move"), only signal with "I can carry this".
- After the sender pays: meet, ask for the pickup code, "Confirm pickup", travel, ask the recipient for the delivery code after the 30 minute lock, "Confirm delivery".
- Browse parcels: "Parcels looking for a traveler" with filters; empty "No open parcels right now" with "Add a journey".
- Route alerts (Profile > Route alerts): "Add alert" for a city pair, watch "New trips" or "New parcels"; "at most one notification per alert every few hours" (the server waits 6 hours between notifications for one alert); at most 10 active alerts per user ("You have reached the maximum number of active alerts."). Travelers may also get "A parcel fits your trip".

## What does the Deliveries tab show?
- Filter chips: **Active**, **Needs you**, **History**. Sections: Open offers, "Sending" (requests, sender role) or "My journeys" (traveler role), and deals as cards ("Sending" / "Carrying", "with {name}").
- Empty states: "Nothing active / Deliveries you're sending or carrying will appear here."; "You're all caught up / Nothing is waiting on you."; "No history yet / Completed and cancelled deliveries stay here." History loads more pages as you scroll.
- A deal not visible to the account: "Delivery unavailable".

## How does a user cancel a delivery?
- Delivery screen > "Cancel this delivery" > "Cancel delivery" (or "Keep it"). The button shows only if the server says cancellation is allowed. After pickup: "The parcel has already been picked up. If something's wrong, open a dispute instead."
- Outcome text: "You'll be refunded in full." or, close to pickup, "Because it's close to pickup, the traveler is compensated for holding the space." Seeded rule: sender cancels more than 24 hours before agreed pickup = full refund; inside 24 hours = traveler gets 10% of the reward, capped at EUR 15 (settings, cancellation section). Then "Your refund is on the way."
- If the traveler changed the times and the sender declined, the cancellation is counted against the traveler, not the sender.

## How does a user report a problem or a no-show?
- On the delivery screen: "Report a problem" (sheet "What went wrong with this delivery?"). Kinds: "The sender did not come", "The traveler did not come", "The recipient is not available", "The parcel is not what was described", "The parcel is damaged", "The trip is cancelled or delayed", "Held up at customs", "Something else". Optional note up to 1,000 characters. Button "Send report". "The other person is told. Wassla staff look at damage and mismatches right away. Reporting does not cancel or refund anything."
- Available while the deal is `funded`, `pickup_ready`, or in carriage (`picked_up`, `in_transit`, `delivery_ready`).
- No-show buttons ("The sender did not come" shown to the traveler, "The traveler did not come" to the sender) only appear from the agreed pickup time until pickup is confirmed, on a funded deal without a staff decision. Before that time: "This can't be reported right now. The pickup time may not have come yet, or the parcel may already be collected." The other side sees "The traveler says you did not come" with "Reply in chat". "Wassla staff will look at it. Nothing is cancelled or refunded yet."
- Report statuses: Sent, With Wassla staff (escalated: damaged, mismatch, sender unavailable, traveler no-show), Being looked at, Closed. Offline: "No connection. Nothing was sent."

## How does a user open a dispute?
- Delivery screen > "Open a dispute" (only if the server allows it). Either party can open one from pickup until protection ends (48 hours after delivery). Staff cannot open one. Before pickup: "You can't open a dispute yet ... cancel the delivery instead." After: "The dispute window has closed ... Contact support if you still need help." The screen offers "Contact support" there when the server has an address.
- Form: "What happened?" (Never arrived, Arrived damaged, Wrong item, Arrived too late, The other person didn't show up, Something is wrong with the money, Something else), "Describe the problem". Warning: "The traveler's payout goes on hold / Nothing is paid out while we look into this." Button "Open dispute".
- Dispute screen: status Open, Waiting for evidence, Being reviewed, Resolved, Closed; timeline; Evidence (photo, video, note; JPEG, PNG, WebP, MP4, MOV; max 20 items, 25 MB each by default; "Add photo", "Add video", "Add a note"). Outcome: "Refunded to the sender", "Paid to the traveler", "Split between both sides". Amounts are Wassla's decision. If the payout already went out: "The payout had already gone out before this dispute was opened."
- One open dispute per delivery ("You already have a dispute open on this delivery. We've taken you to it.").

## How do ratings and reviews work?
- After a delivery: "Leave a rating" ("How did it go?"): "Rate the traveler" / "Rate the sender", score 1 to 5, tags (from a server list: communication, punctual, careful handling, clear instructions, friendly, late, poor communication, damaged), optional comment (up to 1,000 characters). "Submit rating".
- Blind: "Neither of you sees the other's rating until you've both left one, or the window closes." Window default 14 days after delivery confirmation ("The rating window has closed.").
- Reviews appear on public profiles and trips ("Reviews"). Users can "Report" a review (offensive, shares personal information, not about the delivery, not true, other). Staff can remove a review or hide its comment ("Removed by Wassla, not counted", "Wassla hid the comment from others. The score still counts."). Reviews can be switched off by staff ("Reviews are not available").

## How does a user change profile, language, appearance, notifications?
- Profile tab: passport card (name, Verified status, "Email verified"/"Email not verified", role, Completed count, Rating (the server's figure), "With Wassla since"). Rows: "Verify email" (only while the email is not confirmed), Payouts, Payout methods, Ratings, Public profile, Route alerts, Notifications, Language, Appearance, Terms of service, Privacy policy (open in browser), Contact support (only when the server has an address), Sign out, app version.
- **Language**: "App language" (stored on the phone; device language, English, French, Arabic with right-to-left) and "Email language" (saved to the account; used for payments, verification, deliveries, disputes, security emails).
- **Appearance**: Match device, Light, Dark.
- **Notifications**: "Enable notifications" asks for system permission. Toggles: Messages, Marketplace activity (offers, matches, journeys, requests). "Essential updates" (payment, delivery, dispute, verification, security) always stay on. The in-app bell keeps working without push.
- **Public profile**: off by default. Switch "Show my public profile" shows first name, verification, rating, completed deliveries, join month and up to three usual routes. Never shown: last name, email, phone, exact addresses.
- Profile has no support-contact row and no edit-name screen. Its "Help and support" section holds only Terms of service and Privacy policy.

## How does a traveler set up a payout method and get paid?
- Profile > "Payout methods". Preference: **EUR only**, **DZD only**, **Both** (Both: "Stripe-funded deliveries are paid in EUR; Chargily-funded deliveries are paid in DZD."). "Preferences apply to future payouts only."
- **EUR (Stripe)**: "Set up EUR payouts" > pick the legal country (supported list from server; France shown by name; "If your country is not listed, use DZD payouts.") > Stripe onboarding in the browser. States: not set up, setup required ("Resume setup"), "Stripe is verifying your account details", ready ("Manage with Stripe"), needs attention. "Refresh status" rechecks.
- **DZD (CCP / BaridiMob)**: "Set up DZD payouts": First name, Last name, CCP account number (1 to 20 digits), CCP key (2 digits), RIP (20 digits), photo of the full crossed cheque. "Submit payout information". Staff review it: "being reviewed by our team"; ready; "not accepted ... submit a different account in your own name"; correction needed.
- Payout screens: "Payout" with history, detail ("Payout reference", "Associated delivery", "Eligible at", "Sent at", "Paid at", "Protection ends at", "Payout rail": Stripe EUR or CCP Transfer (DZD), and for DZD "Frozen rate: 1 EUR = {rate} DZD").
- Payout statuses: Not yet, Ready, Scheduled, On the way, Paid, Failed, Cancelled, On hold. Reasons: Payout setup required, Profile under review, Profile needs attention, Payout on hold, Dispute open on this delivery, Payout attempt failed, Bank payout was returned, Country not supported for Stripe EUR payouts. Delivery-side labels: Awaiting delivery, Protection period, Awaiting release, Payout ready, Payout sent.
- Timeline: delivery confirmed, 48 hour protection, then payout is released if no dispute is open. A dispute shows "A dispute is open on this delivery, so the payout is on hold until it's resolved." The payout rail follows how the sender paid (Stripe = EUR, Chargily = DZD) subject to the preference. Fee and amount rules: see payments file.
- Empty: "No payouts yet / Complete a delivery and your earnings appear here."

## How does a user contact support?
- Support is one email action, "Contact support", that opens a new email to the address from `GET /api/support` (the tracking number is in the subject when asked from a delivery). It shows in Profile, on the dispute screen, with the problem list on a delivery, in the dispute-window-closed state and on a journey paused by staff, and nowhere while the server has no address. There is no support chat or phone button. Terms and Privacy open the website.
- Outside the app, the website's support page lists four mailboxes (general support, safety and disputes, privacy, payments). In the repo these are placeholders that must be replaced before launch. See messages-and-notices.md.

## Not covered here
- Exact fee, deposit and recommended price formulas (see pricing file).
- The server rules behind each "available action" (cancel allowed, dispute allowed, payout rail choice).
- Admin console pages (see console and helper files).
- Push notification delivery details and the full list of notification types.


==== NOTES: knowledge/architecture-and-diagrams.md ====

# Architecture and diagrams

Covers: How Wassla is built and how it works end to end, with ASCII and mermaid diagrams of the system, the money, the deal states, matching, verification, disputes and the staff console with the AI helper, plus where data lives, scheduled jobs and hosting.
Related files: stakeholder-questions.md (CI checks, reliability), overview-and-glossary.md (status words), deliveries.md, money.md, pricing.md, trips-and-matching.md, accounts-and-verification.md, problems-and-disputes.md, messages-and-notices.md (live updates, Go services), admin-console.md, settings-and-helper.md

## How do I present a diagram to someone?
- Answer in two or three plain sentences first, then show the diagram.
- Print the ASCII version in a code block (works in any chat or email). Then print the mermaid version in a second code block marked `mermaid` (draws a picture on GitHub or a docs page).
- The diagrams use the exact status words staff see in the console (for example `funded`, `protection_window`) and show the normal path. For exact numbers point to the named file, for example "details: money.md". Do not invent boxes that are not drawn here.

## What is Wassla on one page?
Wassla is a peer-to-peer fast-delivery marketplace. A sender posts a parcel, a traveler already going that way carries it, Wassla handles matching, payment, handover codes, a 48-hour protection period and the traveler's payout. EUR is the canonical currency; Algerian cards pay a server-converted DZD amount (details: money.md).

The parts:
| Part | What it does | Talks to |
|---|---|---|
| Flutter app (iOS, Android; `mobile/`) | What senders, travelers and recipients' links use. Tabs: Home, Deliveries, Chat, Profile; bell for notices. English, French, Arabic (right-to-left). Never decides money or status; it only shows what the server says. | Django over HTTPS; Go services over WebSocket |
| Django monolith (`backend/monolith`) | The brain. All rules, money, deals, matching, verification, disputes, the admin console and the AI helper API. One codebase, many "apps" (next section). | PostgreSQL, Redis, object storage, Stripe, Chargily, email, push |
| PostgreSQL 16 and Redis 7 | PostgreSQL is the one source of truth. Redis is a fast message bus (live events, push and email queues), not the truth: Django also saves every obligation in the database. | Django, Go |
| Go services (`backend/services`) | `notification` (live updates and push), `chat` (live chat relay), `kyc` (ID photo upload), `email` (sends queued mail). If they are down Django still works. | Redis, PostgreSQL, object storage, Firebase, Sender.net |
| Object storage (S3-compatible) | Private buckets for parcel photos, ticket proofs, dispute evidence, ID photos, payout evidence. | Django, Go kyc |
| Stripe and Chargily | Stripe: EUR cards, refunds, EUR payouts (connected accounts). Chargily: Algerian card / Edahabia, shown in DZD converted by the server from EUR. | Django (calls and webhooks) |
| Firebase push and email (Sender.net API) | Phone notices when the app is closed; verification, reset, invitation, delivery-code and status emails. Both ship switched off (server flags `FCM_ENABLED` and `EMAIL_ENABLED` default to false) until the operator turns them on. | Go notification and email; Django |
| Landing site (`web/`) | Static pages in en, fr, ar plus terms, privacy, prohibited items, support. No build step. | Served by a small web server inside the app unit, reached through the edge |
| Admin console (`/admin/`, part of Django) | Staff pages: verification, deals, disputes, payments, payouts, settings, audit. Also the AI helper pages. | Django, only from approved office addresses |
| Caddy edge | The only public door (ports 80 and 443). Adds HTTPS, hides internal routes. | App container |
ASCII diagram of the system (show this one in a code block):
```
 +----------------+   HTTPS        +-------------------+
 | Flutter app    |--------------->|   Caddy edge      |
 | (iOS/Android)  |   WebSocket    |  ports 80 / 443   |
 +----------------+<------+        +---------+---------+
 +----------------+       |                  | private
 | Staff browser  |-------+--(office IPs     |  network
 | (admin console)|          only for /admin)|
 +----------------+                          v
 +----------------+        +-----------------------------+
 | Landing site   |<-------|        ONE APP UNIT         |
 | (static pages) |        |  Django monolith (API,      |
 +----------------+        |   console, AI helper API)   |
                           |  + background workers       |
                           |  + Go: notification, chat,  |
                           |        kyc, email           |
                           +--+-------+-------+-------+--+
                              |       |       |       |
                +-------------+  +----+---+   |   +---+---------+
                v                v        v   |   v             v
          +-----------+     +---------+ +-----+--+ +-----------+
          | PostgreSQL|     |  Redis  | |Object  | | Stripe    |
          | (truth)   |     | (bus)   | |storage | | Chargily  |
          +-----------+     +---------+ |(files) | +-----------+
                                        +--------+
        Out to phones: Firebase push.  Out to inboxes: email (Sender.net).
```

Same picture in mermaid:
```mermaid
flowchart LR
    App[Flutter app] -->|HTTPS, WebSocket| Edge[Caddy edge]
    Staff[Staff browser] -->|office addresses only| Edge
    Web[Landing site] --- Edge
    Edge --> Django
    subgraph Unit["One app unit"]
        Django["Django: API, console, AI helper API"] --- Workers["Background workers"] --- Go["Go: notification, chat, kyc, email"]
    end
    Django --> PG[("PostgreSQL: truth")]
    Django --> Redis[("Redis: bus")]
    Go --> Redis
    Go --> PG
    Django --> Files[(Object storage)]
    Go --> Files
    Django --> Stripe[Stripe]
    Django --> Chargily[Chargily]
    Go --> FCM[Firebase push]
    Go --> Mail[Email: Sender.net API]
    Django --> Mail
```

## What are the Django apps and what does each own?
One line per folder in `backend/monolith/apps`. A member never sees these names; staff see them only in "Technical records".
| App | Owns |
|---|---|
| `accounts` | People: sign-up, sign-in, tokens, email codes, ban state, Google and Apple links |
| `core` | Shared plumbing: business settings, policy defaults, scheduling of events, health, languages, locks that keep money steps in order |
| `kyc` | ID verification records (Go `kyc` takes uploads; Django stores and reviews) |
| `trips` | Journeys and legs, ticket proofs, leg progress, schedule changes (the old single `Trip` is history) |
| `locations`, `routing` | Canonical places, countries, sea crossings; route and distance lookups |
| `parcels` | Delivery requests, parcel details, tracking numbers |
| `matching` | Matches, offers, counters, compatibility, listed price and recommended reward |
| `deals` | The deal: frozen terms, the status machine, cancellation, no-shows, timeline |
| `finance` | The real money system: payment orders and attempts, Stripe and Chargily, refunds, ledger, payouts, guest payment links, scheduled jobs, exports |
| `handover` | Pickup and delivery codes, attempts, lockouts, rotation |
| `disputes`, `problems` | Disputes with evidence and settlement; lighter problem reports (no-show, damage, delay) |
| `ratings`, `boosts` | Two-sided blind reviews and moderation; the sender's extra reward (there is no paid boost) |
| `discovery` | Home discovery: public profiles, route alerts, "I can carry this" |
| `notifications` | Bell notices, push, email queue, delivery rules |
| `chat` | One-to-one messages between sender and traveler of a match |
| `admin_panel` | Staff identity, the five roles, the console pages, audit log, two-step sign-in, invitations |
| `admin_helper` | AI helper keys, call log, prepared changes, read API and this skill |
| `payments`, `wallet`, `verification` | Old modules (mock payments, DZD wallet, match-based codes). Not routed; history only (money.md) |
## How does one delivery go, from request to payout?
Short answer: sender posts and offers, traveler accepts, sender pays, parcel is handed over with a code, delivered with a second code, then 48 hours of protection, then the traveler is paid. Details: deliveries.md, money.md.
```
 SENDER                    WASSLA                       TRAVELER
 ------                    ------                       --------
 post request  ---------> request awaiting_deposit
 pay deposit   ---------> request open  <----- publishes journey
                                              (needs ID + ticket ok)
 offer reward  ---------> match + offer pending
                          (fee added on top)  <--- accept / counter
                          deal payment_required
 pay balance   ---------> deal funded  (exact places unlock)
 set recipient ---------> deal pickup_ready
 give parcel +             picked_up -> in_transit  <-- confirm pickup
  pickup code                                          with pickup code
                          delivery code buffered 30 min
                          delivery_ready  (code to recipient by email)
 RECIPIENT gives code ---> traveler enters it
                          delivery_confirmed
                          protection_window (48 h; disputes allowed)
                          completed   -> payout eligible
                          payout sent / paid  ----------> traveler paid
```
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A["Traveler publishes journey: ID and ticket approved"] --> B["Sender posts request: awaiting_deposit then open"]
    B --> C["Sender offers, traveler accepts: deal payment_required"]
    C --> D["Sender pays balance: funded, exact places unlock"]
    D --> E["Recipient set: pickup_ready"]
    E --> F["Pickup code: picked_up then in_transit"]
    F --> G["30 min later delivery code emailed: delivery_ready"]
    G --> H["Traveler enters code: delivery_confirmed"]
    H --> I["protection_window 48 h"] --> J["completed: payout eligible then paid"]
```
Request status path: `awaiting_deposit`, `open`, `matched`, `in_transit`, `delivered`, `completed` (side exits `cancelled`, `expired`). A deal is created straight in `payment_required`.
## What is the deal status machine?
Every status change happens in one place on the server (never in the app). Each change is idempotent: a replayed webhook or a double click does nothing the second time. Deadlines come from values frozen on the deal at funding, so later Settings changes do not affect a running deal.
```
 payment_required --paid--> funded --recipient set--> pickup_ready
   |  +--> expired   (1 h payment grace over, space released)
   +-----> cancelled (cancelled before paying)
 funded or pickup_ready (before pickup):
   party cancels      --> refunded (full refund)
                       or partially_refunded (late sender cancel)
   traveler no-show   --> refunded (staff record it)
   sender no-show     --> status stays (staff record it)
 pickup_ready --pickup code--> picked_up --at once--> in_transit
 in_transit --30 min buffer--> delivery_ready --code--> delivery_confirmed
 delivery_confirmed --at once--> protection_window
 protection_window --48 h, no dispute--> completed
 picked_up, in_transit, delivery_ready, delivery_confirmed,
 protection_window --sender or traveler opens dispute--> disputed
 disputed --full_traveler_payout--> completed
          --full_sender_refund----> refunded
          --partial_split---------> partially_refunded
```
```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> payment_required
    payment_required --> funded: money confirmed
    payment_required --> expired: payment grace over
    payment_required --> cancelled: cancelled before paying
    funded --> pickup_ready: recipient set
    funded --> refunded: cancel or traveler no-show
    funded --> partially_refunded: late sender cancel
    pickup_ready --> refunded: cancel or traveler no-show
    pickup_ready --> partially_refunded: late sender cancel
    pickup_ready --> picked_up: pickup code
    picked_up --> in_transit: at once
    in_transit --> delivery_ready: 30 min buffer over
    delivery_ready --> delivery_confirmed: delivery code
    delivery_confirmed --> protection_window: at once
    protection_window --> completed: 48 h with no dispute
    picked_up --> disputed: party dispute
    in_transit --> disputed: party dispute
    delivery_ready --> disputed: party dispute
    delivery_confirmed --> disputed: party dispute
    protection_window --> disputed: party dispute
    disputed --> completed: full_traveler_payout
    disputed --> refunded: full_sender_refund
    disputed --> partially_refunded: partial_split
```
- Not in the app: professional transporters, courier handoff, Kaba/ProductRequest; sea legs are off by default.
- Cancelling after funding ends in `refunded` (full refund) or `partially_refunded` (late sender cancellation: the traveler keeps compensation). It ends in `cancelled` only when nothing had been collected.
- A failed payment does not move the deal. The payment attempt is marked failed, the sender is emailed, and the deal stays `payment_required` so the sender can retry until the grace ends.
- `payment_failed` and `offer_accepted` are in the status list, but no code moves a deal into them today.
- Terminal states: `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`, `refunded`, `partially_refunded`.
- Who can open a dispute: only the sender or the traveler, in the app, while the parcel is carried (`picked_up`, `in_transit`, `delivery_ready`) or after delivery until the stored protection end (`delivery_confirmed`, `protection_window`). One live dispute per deal; after one is resolved a party cannot open another ("This delivery has already had a dispute resolved. Contact support if something is still wrong.").
- Refusals: before pickup "This parcel has not been picked up yet. Cancel the delivery instead."; after protection "The payment protection window for this delivery has closed."
- Staff cannot open a dispute today.
- `disputed` remembers the status it came from. Resolving is the only way to `completed` without a confirmed delivery (a dispute in carriage decided for the traveler).
## What is the money flow?
Short answer: the sender pays the reward plus Wassla's fee (25% seeded, rounded up to a cent). Money is held until delivery plus 48 hours with no dispute, then the traveler gets the reward and Wassla keeps the fee. Example: reward 20.00 EUR, fee 5.00 EUR, sender pays 25.00 EUR, traveler gets 20.00 EUR (details: money.md, pricing.md).
```
 SENDER pays 25.00 EUR  (reward 20.00 + fee 5.00)
   |  posting deposit paid first is credited, not charged twice
   v
 [payment order -> attempt -> Stripe (EUR) or Chargily (DZD shown)]
   |  provider webhook confirms  --> ledger "customer payment",
   v                                 "deal funding"; deal funded
 HELD (escrow) ---- 48 h protection after delivery_confirmed ----+
   |            \                                                |
   |             \-- cancel before pickup: refund (full, or      |
   |                 sender late-cancel: traveler gets 10%,      |
   |                 max 15.00 EUR, rest back to sender)         |
   |             \-- dispute open: payout frozen; resolution:    |
   |                 refund / payout / partial split             |
   v                                                             |
 clean end: payout eligible <------------------------------------+
   |  release check (no dispute, fully paid, no hold, method ok)
   v
 PAYOUT to traveler: 20.00 EUR (+ any boost)
   EUR: Stripe transfer (automatic is off by default)
   DZD: finance sends by hand, records receipt, then attests
 Wassla keeps 5.00 EUR fee
```
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A["Sender pays reward plus fee"] --> B["Payment order and attempt"]
    B -->|Stripe EUR or Chargily DZD| C{"Provider confirms by webhook"}
    C -->|yes| D["Ledger entries, deal funded, money held"]
    C -->|no| E["Attempt failed, deal stays payment_required; expired after grace"]
    D -->|cancel before pickup| F["Refund: full, or traveler 10 percent capped"]
    D -->|delivery confirmed| G["protection_window 48 h"]
    G -->|dispute opened| H["Payout frozen, staff resolve"]
    H --> I["Refund, payout or partial split"]
    G -->|no dispute| J["Payout eligible after release check"]
    J --> K["Payout: Stripe transfer or manual DZD"]
```
Payout status arrows (the code's allowed moves):
```
 not_eligible --> eligible | blocked | frozen | cancelled
 eligible     --> scheduled | blocked | frozen | cancelled
 scheduled    --> processing | eligible | blocked | frozen | cancelled
 blocked      --> eligible | scheduled | frozen | cancelled
 processing   --> sent | paid | failed | frozen
 sent         --> paid | failed | frozen
 paid         --> failed   (a bank return after payment)
 failed       --> scheduled | processing | eligible | blocked
                  | frozen | cancelled
 frozen       --> not_eligible | eligible | blocked | paid | failed
                  | cancelled
 cancelled    --> (nothing; final)
```
- Usual payout path: `not_eligible` until protection ends, then `eligible` (or `blocked` if setup is missing), then `scheduled`, `processing`, `sent`, `paid`. A dispute moves it to `frozen`; its resolution makes it `eligible` again (traveler gets a share) or `cancelled` (traveler gets nothing). Order and refund statuses: money.md.
- The ledger is append-only: nothing is edited, mistakes are fixed by a new linked "correction" row.
- The app never shows payment or payout success by itself; the provider's signed webhook and Wassla's records decide.
- A payout that is already `paid` cannot be frozen; a settlement then cannot take the money back automatically (a staff recovery matter).
## How do journeys, legs and matching work?
Short answer: a traveler builds a journey from legs (flight, drive; sea is off by default) and publishes it. A sender sees compatible travelers, makes the first offer, and the traveler answers. Boost adds reward but never makes an incompatible pair match (details: trips-and-matching.md).
```
 TRAVELER                                   SENDER
 journey draft                              request open
   + legs (FLIGHT / DRIVE)                    (deadline, weight, route)
   + ID approved, ticket proof approved             |
   |  publish                                       |
 journey active  <----- compatibility check ----->  Find Travelers
   |   (route, dates, weight, deadline; a snapshot  |
   |    is stored on the match)                     v
   |                                    offer (sender proposes first)
   v                                              |
 match pending: offer pending --> declined / withdrawn
   |                                / expired (6 h default)
   |   counter: old offer countered, new offer pending (other side)
   +-- accept --> offer accepted, match accepted, deal payment_required
 later: journey in_progress -> completed (or cancelled, expired)
```
```mermaid
flowchart LR
    J["Journey draft"] -->|ID and tickets approved| JA["Journey active"] --> C{"Compatible with open request?"}
    C -->|yes| O["Sender offers: offer pending"]
    O -->|counter| CO["Old offer countered, new offer pending"] --> O
    O -->|decline, withdraw or no answer| DE["offer declined, withdrawn or expired"]
    O -->|accept| AC["offer accepted, match accepted"] --> D["deal payment_required"]
```
- A counter-offer marks the current offer `countered` and creates a new `pending` offer from the other side, with a fresh answer deadline.
- Journey `in_progress`, `completed` and `expired` are worked out when the journey is read (pickup done, legs over, nothing left to sell); the stored status changes only on publish (`active`) and cancel (`cancelled`). A paused journey cannot be found or offered on.
- Status words. Journey: `draft`, `pending_verification` (listed, never used), `active`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`. Match: `pending`, `accepted`, `in_transit`, `delivered`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`. Offer: `pending`, `countered`, `accepted`, `declined`, `withdrawn`, `expired`.
- Home discovery (public profiles, route alerts, "I can carry this") never creates a match or moves money.
## How does verification work?
Short answer: travelers need an approved ID before publishing; every flight or ferry leg needs an approved ticket proof; recipients and guest payers need no account. Staff decide in the console Verification inbox. Details: accounts-and-verification.md.
```
 TRAVELER app                 GO kyc service          STAFF (Trust role)
 Identity check ---photos---> stores in private   
   (front, back, selfie)      bucket, tells Django
                                    |
                             submission pending ---> Verification > ID tab
                                                      Approve / new upload
                                                      / Reject (+ reason)
 <---- email + live notice ----- approved | rejected (can resubmit)
 Ticket per flight (or sea) leg -> proof pending --> Tickets tab
                                 approved | rejected
 Publish journey allowed only when ID approved and tickets approved
 Payout identity check: separate review (accounts-and-verification.md)
```
```mermaid
flowchart LR
    T["Traveler uploads ID photos"] --> G["Go kyc stores files"] --> K["pending"] --> S{"Staff review"}
    S -->|approve| A["approved: ID verified"] --> P["Journey can be published"]
    S -->|reject or new upload| R["rejected: resubmit"]
    TP["Ticket proof pending"] --> S2{"Staff review"} -->|approve| TA["approved"] --> P
    S2 -->|reject or new upload| TR["rejected"]
```
- ID statuses: `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`, `expired`. Proof statuses: `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`. Viewing a photo needs the evidence right and is audited.
## What is the problem and dispute flow?
Short answer: a problem report is a light note on a deal; a dispute is the only thing that can freeze a payout and change who gets the money. Details: problems-and-disputes.md.
```
 PROBLEM REPORT (sender or traveler, deal funded up to delivery_ready)
   damaged, mismatch, sender/traveler no-show --> starts escalated
   any other kind ----------------------------> starts open
   open or escalated --staff--> acknowledged --staff--> closed
   open or escalated --staff--------------------------> closed
 NO-SHOW (funded or pickup_ready, before pickup): staff record who.
   traveler no-show = full sender refund (default); sender: no money

 DISPUTE (sender or traveler, from pickup to end of 48 h protection)
   open <--> awaiting_evidence <--> under_review   (staff move it,
     |  evidence: text, photo, video; payout frozen  any direction)
     v  (from any of the three)
   resolved (Finance or Super Admin):
     full_sender_refund   -> deal refunded
     full_traveler_payout -> deal completed
     partial_split        -> deal partially_refunded
```
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    PR["Problem report"] -->|"damaged, mismatch, no-show"| ES["escalated"]
    PR -->|"other kinds"| OP["open"]
    OP --> AK["acknowledged"] --> CL["closed"]
    ES --> AK
    OP --> CL
    ES --> CL
    D["Dispute: open"] <--> AE["awaiting_evidence"] <--> UR["under_review"]
    D <--> UR
    D --> RS["resolved"]
    AE --> RS
    UR --> RS
    RS -->|full_sender_refund| RF["deal refunded"]
    RS -->|full_traveler_payout| PO["deal completed"]
    RS -->|partial_split| SP["deal partially_refunded"]
```
- Problem reports can be filed only while the deal is `funded`, `pickup_ready`, `picked_up`, `in_transit` or `delivery_ready`.
- A dispute's three review states can be set by staff with the manage right in any order. `resolved` is reached only through a resolution, which moves the money. A resolved dispute cannot be reopened.
- A `closed` dispute status still exists in the data model, but nothing sets it: the uncalled close step was removed in L18A, so every dispute ends `resolved`.
- Who may act: Ops, Trust and Super Admin can change a dispute's review state; Finance and Super Admin can resolve; Support can only view (admin-console.md).
- Problem kinds and dispute categories: see problems-and-disputes.md and overview-and-glossary.md.
## How do the staff console and the AI helper work together?
Short answer: staff use the console in a browser (two-step sign-in, five fixed roles, everything audited). An admin can also create a key and paste it into their own AI assistant. The assistant reads as that admin and can only prepare changes; the admin confirms every change by hand in the console. Details: settings-and-helper.md, admin-console.md.
```
 ADMIN                 AI ASSISTANT              WASSLA HELPER API
 two-step code
 create key  ------key shown once----> (read key wslr_ 8 h,
                                         change key wslw_ 30 min)
                       guide/briefing/queues/
                       records/search  ------>  read only, role-limited,
                       <-- no files, emails, codes, bank data --
                       POST action  --------->  change key: 2 done at once
                                                 (note, acknowledge);
                                                 19 are PREPARED ("waiting")
 console page <---- confirm link ---------------
 read server summary
 Confirm / Reject (own reason)
        |  server re-checks: two-step, not expired, helper on,
        v  permission now, record unchanged
 runs the console's own function as the admin, audited "via ai_helper"
 (or "No longer applied" if anything changed; expires after 24 h default)
```
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A["Admin signs in with two-step code"] --> B["Creates read or change key, shown once"] --> C["Pastes key into AI assistant"]
    C --> D["Assistant reads: guide, briefing, queues, records, search"]
    D --> E["Assistant POSTs action: status waiting plus confirm link"]
    E --> F["Admin opens link in console, reads summary"]
    F --> G{"Confirm or Reject"}
    G -->|confirm| H["Server re-checks rights and record, runs action, audits via ai_helper"]
    G -->|reject or 24 h pass| I["Nothing changes"]
```
- Console pages by area: Overview, AI helper, Users, Verification, Reviews, Requests, Journeys, Deals, Disputes, Problem reports, Payments, Refunds, Payouts, Ledger, Finance dashboard, Manual DZD, Exports, Staff, Settings, System (jobs, email, geography), Audit log. Roles: `ops`, `support`, `finance`, `trust_verification`, `super_admin`.
## Where does data live, and what is private?
| Data | Where | Who can see it |
|---|---|---|
| People, requests, journeys, deals, money records, ledger, audit log, settings | PostgreSQL (cents in EUR) | App users see their own; staff by role |
| ID photos, ticket proofs, parcel photos, dispute evidence, payout evidence | Separate private object-storage buckets (parcel, proof, dispute, KYC, payout) | Opened through short-lived signed links; staff need the evidence right; each open is audited |
| Live events, push and email queues | Redis (also saved in the database as obligations) | Internal only |
| Passwords, handover codes, helper keys | Passwords and codes stored as hashes; helper keys as a fingerprint plus last four characters. The delivery code goes to the recipient by email; the traveler can never read it | Nobody; key shown once |
| Secrets (database, Redis, backup, provider keys) | Mounted files on the server, not in the code | Operators |
- Exact addresses stay hidden until the deal is `funded`. Chat text is not readable by staff in the console (messages-and-notices.md).
- Backups: a daily encrypted database backup to an offsite restic repository (timer at 02:15 UTC). How many daily, weekly and monthly copies are kept is owner configuration, not in the code: the backup script refuses to run until the operator sets the three counts (`BACKUP_KEEP_DAILY`, `BACKUP_KEEP_WEEKLY`, `BACKUP_KEEP_MONTHLY`), and the example file only says "REPLACE_WITH_COUNT".
## Which scheduled jobs run, and what do they do?
Jobs are durable rows in the database (status `pending`, `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`), so a restart or a Redis flush loses nothing. One finance worker checks them every 30 seconds; one reservation releaser polls every 60 seconds. Staff see failures in System > Background jobs. These are the job kinds:
- Money: `deposit_expiry_refund` (refund deposit when a request expires unmatched), `payment_grace_release` (free the space and expire the deal if unpaid), `attempt_expiry` (close an old checkout), `provider_reconcile` and `provider_event_process` (re-check payments, apply stored webhooks), `refund_reconcile` (check refund results).
- Payout: `payout_release_check` (at protection end: `eligible`, `blocked` or `frozen`), `payout_execute`, `payout_reconcile`, `payout_account_refresh`, `payout_sweep` (automatic EUR payout steps, execution off by default).
- Deal lifecycle: `delivery_code_release` (after the 30 min buffer, emails the recipient), `protection_expiry` (ends the 48 h and completes the deal), `rating_reveal`.
- Messages: `outbound_message`, `notification_dispatch` (send queued email and notices).
- Marketplace: `discovery_route_alerts`, `leg_reminder` (nudge traveler to report progress), `schedule_change_settle`, `offer_response_reminder` and `offer_expiry` (unanswered offer, 6 h default).
- AI helper: `admin_helper_call_prune` (call log kept 30 days), `admin_helper_action_expiry` (prepared change unconfirmed, 24 h default).
- Profile photos: `profile_photo_object_delete` (deletes a stored photo once no member's photo names it; retried until storage confirms).
- Daily host maintenance (03:15 UTC) only cleans login tokens, checks the event outbox for lost deliveries and runs approved retention commands. It never starts the finance or lifecycle workers.
## How is Wassla hosted, and what are the environments?
- Design target: one Ubuntu server (4 vCPU, 8 GB RAM, 160 GB SSD) running Docker Compose. Services: `postgres`, `redis`, `app`, `caddy`, plus one-shot `maintenance` and `backup` jobs.
- The `app` container is deliberately a single unit: Django web (3 workers with 2 threads each in the shipped server example; the launcher's own default is 2 x 2), Django's ID-upload service, a reservation releaser, a finance worker, and the Go notification, chat, kyc and email programs, plus a small internal web server for the static landing site. If any child stops, the whole unit fails loudly instead of running half-alive. There is exactly one copy so jobs are never run twice.
- Network: only ports 80 and 443 are public, through Caddy. The database and Redis sit on a private network with no outside route. `/admin` and `/api/admin` answer 403 unless the visitor comes from the owner's approved addresses. Internal metrics routes answer 404.
- Settings profiles in code: `prod` (strict, refuses unsafe values), `dev`, `test_local`, `local_preview`. A mock payment provider is for tests only and forbidden in production.
- Deploys use a full reviewed Git SHA as release id and image tag; the previous image is kept for rollback. Nothing merges to `main` or deploys without the owner's approval.
- Whether a production server is live today is not stated in the repository files. The infra README says the folder "does not provision, deploy, change DNS or activate providers". So never say the app is live; say the files describe the planned setup and the owner knows the current state.
- Updates have a short maintenance window (not zero downtime), and rolling back an image does not undo database changes.
## Not covered here
- Whether production is live, the real domain, hosting costs, backup retention numbers and recovery targets (owner decisions, not in the repository).
- Per-endpoint API lists, database columns, legal entity and licensing questions.


==== NOTES: knowledge/deliveries.md ====

# The life of a delivery

Covers: how a parcel request is created, proposed, accepted, paid, picked up, handed over, completed or cancelled, with every status, time window, code rule and who sees what.
Related files: pricing.md, money.md, problems-and-disputes.md, trips-and-matching.md, messages-and-notices.md, admin-console.md, settings-and-helper.md

Words used here: the sender posts a parcel request. The traveler carries it on a journey. A "deal" is created when a proposal is accepted. The recipient is the person who receives the parcel; they need no account.

## What are the steps of a delivery, start to finish?
1. Sender posts a parcel request (status `open`, or `awaiting_deposit` first if a posting deposit is required).
2. Sender proposes a price to a traveler (the sender always proposes first). Traveler accepts, declines, or counters.
3. When an offer is accepted a deal is created (`payment_required`) and the traveler's space is reserved for a payment grace period.
4. Sender pays. Deal becomes `funded`. Exact addresses are revealed to the traveler. Sender gets a pickup code.
5. Sender records the recipient (name and email). Deal becomes `pickup_ready`.
6. At pickup the traveler types the sender's pickup code. Deal becomes `in_transit`. A 30-minute safety wait starts.
7. After 30 minutes the delivery code is released: the recipient is emailed it, the sender can see it. Deal becomes `delivery_ready`.
8. At handover the recipient tells the traveler the code and the traveler types it. Deal becomes `delivery_confirmed`, then `protection_window` (48 hours).
9. If nobody disputes, the protection window ends and the deal becomes `completed`; the traveler's payout becomes eligible (see money.md).

## How does a sender create a parcel request? What is required?
Every request needs all of these (the server refuses otherwise):
- Pickup place and delivery place: an active locality or airport from the catalogue. They must differ. A place that cannot be matched is refused: "This place is temporarily unavailable for matching."
- Ready window start and end (start before end), and a deadline (at or after the window end, and in the future). Errors: "Ready window end must be after its start.", "Deadline must be at or after the ready window end.", "Deadline must be in the future."
- Weight: 0.01 to 100 kg.
- Declared value: 0 to 100,000,000 EUR cents.
- Sender's proposed reward for the traveler: 1 to 100,000,000 EUR cents. It is only the sender's intent, not a price; the agreed price is on the offer.
- Title (max 160 characters), description (max 2000), category (documents, small_box, electronics, clothing, other).
- One photo of the item (required). It is uploaded first, then used by the create call. JPEG, PNG or WebP only, max 10 MiB.
- Five declarations, all must be ticked: description accurate, item legal, no prohibited goods, declared value accurate, customs responsibilities understood. Refusal: "This safety declaration must be confirmed."
Optional:
- Length, width, height: 0.01 to 500 cm each, all three or none. Refusal: "Enter length, width and height together, or leave all three empty."
- Handling notes (max 2000), fragile flag.
- Preferred pickup/drop-off point (a saved location of the sender, inside the chosen place).
- Boost (extra reward, see pricing.md).
- Target traveler: makes the request private to one traveler (a sender cannot target themselves). A private request is not listed, and only the sender and that traveler can open it ("This targeted request is private.").
- Posting deposit amount (otherwise the server recommends one).
Not allowed: extra unknown fields ("This field is not accepted by the V1 delivery contract.").

## Which items are prohibited?
- The server has no list of prohibited items and does not check contents. The control is the sender's five declarations ("no prohibited goods", "item is legal", "customs responsibilities understood" and the others above). Item categories are only the five above.
- In the app, the posting form has a "See what can't be sent" link. It opens a sheet that only repeats "It contains no prohibited goods"; it shows no list.
- The public website has a draft "Prohibited items & acceptable use" page (marked "Review required before launch"). Never send: weapons, explosives, ammunition or items meant to harm people; illegal drugs, controlled substances or counterfeit goods; live animals, human remains, biological or hazardous materials; stolen goods, cash equivalents, or items meant to evade customs or sanctions; anything banned by the law of the departure, transit or arrival country, or by the airline, carrier or airport.

## Is there a minimum reward? What if the sender picks too little?
Yes. The reward may be above or below the recommended price, but never below the platform minimum for that route and weight. If lower, the create call is refused (HTTP 400) with code `price_below_minimum` ("The reward is below the minimum for this request."), the `minimum_reward_eur_cents` and the `recommended_reward_eur_cents`. The app shows "Offer must be at least {amount}". The minimum and recommendation are calculated in pricing.md. The app shows a price quote before posting.

## What are the parcel request statuses?
| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `awaiting_deposit` | Created but the posting deposit is not paid. Not visible to travelers, cannot be proposed on. |
| `open` | Published. Travelers can see it and the sender can propose. |
| `matched` | An offer was accepted; waiting for payment or pickup. |
| `in_transit` | Parcel picked up. |
| `delivered` | Delivery confirmed (protection window). |
| `completed` | Deal completed. |
| `cancelled` | Cancelled by the sender, or the deal was cancelled/refunded after funding. |
| `expired` | Nobody took it before the deadline (see below). |
The status shown is worked out from the latest deal: no deal = the request's own status; deal `payment_required`, `funded`, `pickup_ready`, `payment_failed` or `disputed` before pickup = `matched`; `picked_up`, `in_transit`, `delivery_ready` (or pickup confirmed) = `in_transit`; `delivery_confirmed`, `protection_window` (or delivery confirmed) = `delivered`; `completed` = `completed`; `cancelled`, `expired`, `refunded`, `partially_refunded` = `cancelled`. Exception: a deal that was cancelled or expired BEFORE funding (and before pickup) sends the request back to `open`. A request already stored as `cancelled` or `expired` stays so.

## Is a posting deposit needed?
It depends on the payment setting "timing mode" (`payments.timing_mode`, no console form). The seeded default is `posting_deposit`: the request starts as `awaiting_deposit` and becomes `open` only after a confirmed payment (the app never decides that itself). The other mode, `after_acceptance`, needs no deposit (app: "No deposit needed"). Recommended deposit: 10% of the recommended sender total, kept between EUR 3.00 and EUR 7.00; the sender may choose from EUR 3.00 up to the whole amount owed. The console Settings card "Commission, deposit and floors" has "Posting deposit (%)", "Minimum posting deposit (€)", "Maximum posting deposit (€)" (admins can change them). The deposit is credited against the deal balance when an offer is accepted. If the request ends unmatched or is cancelled, the deposit is refunded. See money.md and pricing.md.

## When does a request expire?
A request is listed to travelers only while the deadline and the ready-window end are in the future, it is `open`, has no active deal and is not targeted at one traveler.
- Expiry is done by the "Deposit expiry refund" background job. It is scheduled when the posting deposit is paid and the request is published, to run at the request's deadline plus the deposit expiry grace (seeded 0 seconds). It marks a still-`open` request `expired`, refunds the deposit in full (refund reason "Request expired unmatched") and ends its Boost.
- A request that found a traveler is not expired; its deposit is credited, not refunded.
- In the `after_acceptance` mode (no deposit) no such job is scheduled, and the code has no other job that marks a request `expired`. Such a request just stops being listed once its deadline passes.

## Can the sender cancel a parcel request?
- Only the sender, only while `open` or `awaiting_deposit`, and only if the request has no deal.
- Pending proposals on it are withdrawn and the negotiations cancelled. The deposit (if paid) is refunded in full and the order cancelled. Any unpaid Boost ends.
- Errors: "Only the sender can cancel." (403); "A request with a Deal must use the Deal cancellation workflow." (409); "Cannot cancel a parcel in status '<status>'." (409).

## What is "I can carry this"?
A traveler tells a sender that one of the traveler's journeys could carry that request. The server first checks the traveler's journey really fits (the same check as Find Travelers). It creates no offer, no price, no reservation; the sender gets one notification and still proposes first.
- Limit: 10 signals per traveler per rolling 24 hours (setting: Marketplace features, "I can carry this signals per traveler per day", 1 to 100).
- Repeating a signal for the same request and journey returns the existing one.
- Refusals: "This request is no longer open." (404), "Choose one of your own journeys." (404), "This journey cannot carry this request." (409), "You have reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow." (429), "Browsing requests is not available right now." (listings switched off).
- Also, when a request opens, fitting travelers (max 30, each at most 5 such alerts per day, settings in Marketplace features) are told once. That is not an offer either.

## How does a proposal work? Who proposes first?
- The sender proposes first, to a specific traveler's journey, with a reward in EUR cents. The traveler cannot start a negotiation.
- The other side (not the proposer) can accept, decline or counter. Counter creates a new offer with a new price; the old one becomes `countered`. The proposer can withdraw while it is pending.
- Only one pending offer per negotiation. A negotiation ("match") is `pending` until an offer is accepted, declined, withdrawn or expires.
- Checks at proposing: request is `open`; journey is active or in progress and not paused; the traveler is not the sender; a targeted request only accepts its target traveler; the journey must truly be compatible (route, timing, capacity). Boost cannot bypass compatibility. Price below minimum is refused (`reward_below_minimum`, with the minimum).
- Offer statuses: `pending`, `countered`, `accepted`, `declined`, `withdrawn`, `expired`.
- Common refusals: "Only the request sender may propose first.", "A sender cannot propose to their own journey.", "The delivery request is not open.", "The journey is not active.", "This private delivery request targets a different traveler.", "The proposer cannot accept their own offer.", "The offer is no longer pending."

## What is the proposal deadline?
- Each proposal and counter-proposal has a response deadline: the time it was sent plus 6 hours, or the pickup time of that match if that is sooner.
- One reminder goes to the person who must answer, 1 hour before the deadline (not sent if that moment is not after the offer was sent).
- From the deadline on, the offer cannot be accepted, countered, declined or withdrawn: refusal `offer_expired` ("The response deadline of this offer has passed."), even before the background job runs. The job then sets it `expired` and ends the negotiation like a decline; the request stays open so the sender can propose to someone else. Expiry reserves nothing, charges nothing and creates no reliability mark.
- Settings (console Settings, "Marketplace features"): "Give proposals a response deadline" (on by default), "Hours to answer a proposal" (default 6, 1 to 168), "Remind the person who must answer, hours before the deadline" (default 1; 0 = none; must be smaller than the hours). Applies to proposals sent from then on; an existing offer keeps its deadline. With the switch off new proposals have no deadline.

## What happens when an offer is accepted?
- Either party except the proposer accepts. The server rechecks compatibility, capacity (weight, volume, parcel count on every leg) and that the request is still open.
- If a Boost is included, the acceptor must confirm the total; if it changed they get `offer_economics_changed` and must review again.
- A deal is created with status `payment_required`. Prices, commission and Boost are frozen on the deal. Space on the traveler's legs is reserved for the payment grace period (seeded 3600 seconds = 1 hour, stored setting `reservation.payment_grace_seconds`, shown read-only in Settings; no console form). The balance to pay is created (any posting deposit is credited off it).
- Offer becomes `accepted`, negotiation `accepted`, request `matched`. Competing pending offers on the same request are expired.
- Accepting the same offer again returns the existing deal.
- Other refusals: `request_already_matched`, `journey_not_active`, `capacity_exceeded`, `incompatible_candidate`.

## What are all the deal statuses and what do they mean?
| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `offer_accepted` | Exists in the list but new deals start at `payment_required`. |
| `payment_required` | Waiting for the sender's payment; space reserved. |
| `funded` | Paid in full. Waiting for the sender to record the recipient. |
| `pickup_ready` | Recipient recorded; traveler may confirm pickup. |
| `picked_up` | Pickup code accepted (passes straight on to `in_transit`). |
| `in_transit` | Traveler carries the parcel; delivery code still locked. |
| `delivery_ready` | The 30-minute buffer passed; delivery code released. |
| `delivery_confirmed` | Delivery code accepted (passes straight to `protection_window`). |
| `protection_window` | 48 hours after delivery. Payout cannot be released; a dispute is possible. |
| `completed` | Protection ended cleanly, or a dispute was decided for the traveler. |
| `cancelled` | Cancelled before funding, or after funding with nothing collected. |
| `expired` | Not paid within the grace period; reservation released. |
| `payment_failed` | In the list but never set by any code: after a failed payment the deal stays `payment_required` and the sender retries. |
| `disputed` | A dispute is open; payout frozen. |
| `refunded` | Sender fully refunded (cancellation, no-show or dispute). |
| `partially_refunded` | Sender refunded part; traveler got compensation or a share. |
Only one active deal can exist per request at a time. For lists the server groups deals: active (anything still moving), completed (delivery confirmed, protection, completed), cancelled (cancelled, expired, refunded, partially refunded).

## What happens if the sender does not pay in time?
The reservation lasts the payment grace period (seed 1 hour). A background job and a sweep then release it: deal `expired`, negotiation `expired`, space freed, the balance order closed, request back to `open` (its unpaid Boost comes back with it). A payment that arrives after this is recorded and refunded; it does not revive the deal.
Before paying, either party can cancel: the deal becomes `cancelled`, space freed, request back to `open`, free of charge.

## What changes at funding?
- Deal `funded`; the funded time is stored. Prices stay as frozen at acceptance.
- The lifecycle rules are copied onto the deal (30-minute buffer, 48-hour protection, review window, cancellation rules, code limits). A later settings change does not affect this deal.
- Agreed pickup time is frozen (from the match's pickup time, else the request's ready-window start, else the leg departure). It is used for the cancellation cutoff and no-show window.
- The funded arrival time is frozen (used as a payout floor; see Early arrival).
- Any Boost paid is consumed (cleared from the request).
- The sender's pickup code is created.
- Exact locations become visible to the traveler. Before funding, a traveler (and everyone but the sender) sees only the coarse public location (public label, city, region, rough coordinates). After funding the traveler of the funded deal sees the sender's exact pickup and delivery locations. The funded route (legs and times for this parcel) is shown to both parties only after funding.

## Who is the recipient and when is it set?
- Recipient: full name (max 120), email (required), phone (max 32, optional), delivery note (max 1000, optional), language.
- Only the sender can set or change it, only after funding and before pickup is confirmed. Errors: "Only the sender can set the delivery recipient.", "Recipient details are collected once the delivery is funded.", "The parcel has already been picked up; the recipient can no longer be changed here."
- Setting it moves `funded` to `pickup_ready`. Without it the traveler's pickup code is refused (`recipient_required`).
- Who sees it: sender and staff see everything. The traveler sees only "recorded" before pickup, and after pickup the name and delivery note. The traveler never sees the email or phone. Nobody else sees anything. Changes are logged on the timeline without the new values.

## How does pickup work (the pickup code)?
- The sender gets an 8-character code when the deal is funded and can view it in the app any time before pickup (and rotate it for a new one). The traveler cannot view it; they must be given it in person by the sender and type it in.
- Code alphabet is letters and digits without I, L, O, U. Typing ignores case, spaces and dashes (I/L read as 1, O as 0).
