- Pickup is accepted only when the deal is `pickup_ready`. On success: pickup time stored, deal `in_transit`, capacity marked in transit, sender emailed "Pickup confirmed", the 30-minute timer starts, and the delivery code is created in a locked state.
- After pickup the deal cannot be cancelled; use a dispute.
- Code errors: `recipient_required`, `deal_not_pickup_ready`, `pickup_already_confirmed`, `code_not_available`.

## How do wrong codes, lockouts and rotation work?
Defaults (stored per deal at funding; seed values):
- Code length 8.
- 5 wrong tries lock the code for 15 minutes (900 s). After 3 such lockouts the code is locked for good; only the sender rotating it gives a usable new one.
- At most 12 attempts per rolling hour per code kind; beyond that `handover_rate_limited` ("Too many attempts. Try again later.", HTTP 429).
- A wrong code always answers "That code is not valid for this delivery." plus attempts remaining and lock time. Messages never say how close the guess was.
- Only the traveler can submit codes ("Only the traveler can submit a handover code."); only the sender can view or rotate ("Only the sender can view a handover code.").
- Rotation replaces the old code. Pickup rotation: before pickup, funded deals only. Delivery rotation: only after the 30-minute buffer, while in carriage, before delivery; it re-sends the recipient's email with the new code.
- Every submission and every time the sender views a code is logged (never the code itself).

## Why can't the traveler see the delivery code? How is it sent?
The traveler has no way to view it at all (no screen, API or message returns it to them). The delivery code is created at pickup but locked for 30 minutes (1800 s; stored per deal). During the 30 minutes nobody can see it, including the sender; revealing or rotating is refused with `delivery_code_buffer_open` ("The delivery code is inside its safety window.").
When the 30 minutes pass (a scheduled job, or the sender opening the code, whichever is first):
- Deal becomes `delivery_ready`.
- The recipient is emailed the code (in the recipient's language, with tracking number, recipient name and sender name). The code is added only when the email is built, never stored in the message.
- The sender is emailed that the code is available and can view it in the app.
- The traveler only gets a refreshed deal; they learn the code only by the recipient reading it aloud.
If the email never reaches the recipient, the sender can read the code to them or rotate it.

## How is delivery confirmed?
The traveler types the delivery code the recipient gives them, while the deal is `delivery_ready`. On success: delivery time stored, deal `delivery_confirmed` then `protection_window`, protection ends 48 hours later (172,800 s, from payout setting "protection window", stored per deal), the review window opens (14 days, 1,209,600 s), both parties are emailed "Delivery confirmed", a reminder email goes 24 hours before protection ends (server setting PROTECTION_ENDING_REMINDER_SECONDS, default 86,400), and the rating invitation is sent. Refusals: `delivery_code_buffer_open`, `delivery_already_confirmed`, `pickup_not_confirmed`.

## How does a deal auto-complete?
When the protection window ends, a scheduled job evaluates the payout. The deal becomes `completed` automatically if: there is no active dispute and the time is past `max(protection end, funded arrival time)`. Then the payout becomes eligible and both parties are emailed "protection ended". If a dispute is active, the payout is frozen and the deal stays `disputed`. If the funded arrival time is later than the protection end (an unusually early delivery), the deal is still closed out as `completed` while the payout waits for that time. Payout details: money.md. A dispute resolved for the traveler can also complete a deal without a confirmed delivery; a dispute can also end as `refunded` or `partially_refunded`.

## Who can cancel a funded deal and what does it cost?
Allowed only for the sender or the traveler, only while `funded` or `pickup_ready`, and only before pickup. The server quotes first (`GET deals/<id>/cancellation`), then performs it (`POST deals/<id>/cancel`).
| Case | Result |
|---|---|
| Traveler cancels | Sender refunded in full. No compensation. Recorded as a reliability signal. |
| Sender cancels more than 24 hours before the agreed pickup | Sender refunded in full. |
| Sender cancels 24 hours or less before the agreed pickup (exactly 24 h counts as late) | Traveler is compensated 10% of the base traveler reward (rounded up), capped at EUR 15.00; the rest goes back to the sender. Platform fee on this: 0%. |
| Sender cancels after declining the traveler's new times | Treated as a traveler cancellation: full refund, no compensation. |
Worked example: base reward EUR 20.00, sender cancels 5 hours before pickup: 10% = EUR 2.00 to the traveler; the sender gets back everything collected minus EUR 2.00. If reward was EUR 200: 10% = EUR 20.00, capped to EUR 15.00. The Boost part is not included in the 10%.
- Cutoff defaults (Deal-frozen): 86,400 s, 1000 bps, EUR 15.00 cap, 0 bps platform fee. If no agreed pickup time exists, it counts as early (no penalty).
- Result status: `refunded` (all back), `partially_refunded` (compensation paid), `cancelled` (nothing had been collected). The parcel request becomes `cancelled` (not reopened; the sender must post a new request and pay a new deposit). Space is freed, codes cancelled, both emailed "deal cancelled".
- Refusals: `cancellation_not_available_after_pickup` ("This parcel has already been picked up. Open a dispute instead."), `use_pre_funding_cancellation` ("This delivery has not been funded yet; use the standard cancel action."), `deal_not_cancellable` ("This delivery can no longer be cancelled."), `not_authorized` ("Only a party to this delivery can cancel it.").
- Cancelling again after a cancellation returns the same answer, not an error.

## What if the traveler changes the journey times? Declined schedule change?
A traveler may re-time legs of an active journey. A parcel is "affected" if on that leg, and "material" if the new arrival is after its deadline or moves more than 360 minutes (6 hours; setting "route change material shift minutes", 30 to 10,080) from the last agreed arrival, or the pickup moves by the same amount (or earlier than agreed pickup). A collected parcel is only informed. With no material parcel the change applies at once. Otherwise each material sender accepts or declines; it applies when all accept, collected or inactive; the traveler can withdraw it. A sender who declines may cancel, and that cancellation is treated as the traveler's (full refund, reason `declined_schedule_change`). Funded arrival, payout floor and agreed pickup stay as frozen.

## What are no-show reports and what does staff do?
- Reporting (parties): the person left waiting reports. A traveler reports `sender_unavailable` ("The sender did not come"); a sender reports `traveler_no_show` ("The traveler did not come"). Allowed only from the agreed pickup time until pickup is confirmed, on a funded, active deal with no no-show already recorded. Refusal `no_show_report_not_allowed` with reason: `deal_not_funded`, `deal_not_active`, `pickup_time_unknown`, `pickup_time_not_reached` (with when), `pickup_already_confirmed`, `no_show_already_recorded`. Wrong person: "Only the person who was left waiting can report this."
- Other problem types any party can report from funding until delivery: recipient unavailable, parcel mismatch, parcel damaged, trip disrupted, customs delay, other (note max 1000 characters). After delivery use a dispute. Reports of no-show, damage or mismatch go to staff at once (`escalated`); statuses: open, escalated, acknowledged, closed. A report changes no deal status or money.
- Staff: Console, Disputes > Problems list shows the reports; a no-show report has a "Record a no-show" link to Marketplace > Deals > (deal) > no-show page. Needs the "record no-show" permission. Staff choose the party (sender or traveler) and add a note (max 255).
- Traveler no-show: sender refunded in full, deal `refunded` (or `cancelled` if nothing was collected), request cancelled, space freed, reason `traveler_no_show`. Sender no-show: recorded only, no refund by default (staff may override the refund choice). Not allowed after pickup, or if a no-show was already recorded (the second call returns the first).

## What is the tracking number?
Format `WSL-` plus 8 characters from `23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ` (no 0, 1, I, L, O, U), e.g. `WSL-7K3M9QXD`. Random, created with the request, never changes, never reused, identical across all deals of that request. It only identifies a parcel and authorises nothing. People may type it in lower case, without the hyphen or without `WSL`. Shown to: the sender on their request, both parties on the deal and in emails (pickup, delivery, cancellation, rating, protection and payout emails, recipient code email, and offer reminders); a traveler sees it through a deal or proposal they are part of, not on the open request. Console titles also use it (e.g. "Record a no-show · WSL-…").

## What progress updates and reminders does the traveler give?
- On each leg of a journey the traveler reports "departed" and "arrived" (and "at the port" on sea legs, which are off by default). Order is enforced (departed before arrived; each departure needs the previous leg's arrival; error `progress_out_of_order`). Not earlier than 24 hours before the planned time (`progress_too_early`). A report can be retracted within 10 minutes unless a later report exists.
- A report is copied to the timeline of every funded parcel on that leg, labelled "reported by the traveler", and tells the senders. It is a statement only: no status change, no money.
- Reminders to the traveler (stored setting `marketplace.reminders`, default on): ask "departed?" 30 minutes after planned departure and "arrived?" 60 minutes after planned arrival, only if the leg carries a funded parcel; repeat every 180 minutes, at most 2 repeats. These have no form on the console Settings page; they change only through a full new settings version posted through the staff settings API (needs `manage_settings`).

## What is early arrival?
If the traveler reaches the destination much earlier than the funded schedule, they can report it. "Material" means at least 6 hours early (21,600 s, fixed in code). Only the traveler may report, only while carrying the parcel. The sender then confirms or declines. It changes no status, releases no code, starts no protection window and does not move the payout floor: payout still waits for the funded arrival time. Refusals include `not_materially_early`, `arrival_report_pending`, `arrival_already_confirmed`, `delivery_already_confirmed`, `dispute_active`.

## What does each person see at each step?
| Step | Sender | Traveler | Recipient |
|---|---|---|---|
| Request open | Full request, tracking number | Public card only (no address, no recipient) | Nothing |
| Offer | Offer, deadline | Offer, deadline, coarse locations | Nothing |
| Payment required | Deal, cancel option, pay | Deal, reserved | Nothing |
| Funded | Pickup code, can set recipient | Exact locations, funded route | Nothing |
| Pickup ready | Pickup code | Can enter pickup code | Nothing |
| In transit (first 30 min) | Countdown, no delivery code | Name and note of recipient | Nothing |
| Delivery ready | Delivery code (view/rotate) | Can enter code, never see it | Emailed delivery code |
| Protection window | Countdown, can dispute | Countdown, payout state | Nothing |
| Completed | Rating invite | Payout status, rating invite | Nothing |
Staff see the full stored timeline.
Each party's timeline shows only allowed fields (status, times, amounts, ids), never code material or contact details.

## Where does staff see deals and requests?
- Console menu: Marketplace > "Delivery requests" (view_requests), "Journeys" (view_journeys), "Deals" (view_deals; each deal has a detail page with its timeline and a no-show page); Disputes > "Disputes" and "Problems" (view_disputes).
- Settings > "Business settings": the "Discovery, routes, prices and proposals" card (eyebrow "Marketplace features") holds the proposal deadline switches and the "I can carry this" cap. The page also has the pricing (commission, deposit, floors), Chargily exchange rate, provider availability and boost commission forms.
- The handover buffer (30 min), protection window (48 h), payment grace (1 h), free-cancellation cutoff, late-cancellation compensation and cap, and review window are shown read-only in the "Lifecycle guardrails" table. No console form changes them; they change only through a full new settings version posted through the staff settings API (needs `manage_settings`, a written reason, audited). A change never affects a deal already funded.

## Not covered here
- Mobile screen-by-screen wording for the delivery screens (see app-walkthrough.md).
- Boost bands, payment flow, refunds, payouts, disputes and ratings in depth (see pricing.md, money.md, problems-and-disputes.md, reviews.md).


==== NOTES: knowledge/messages-and-notices.md ====

# Messages, Notifications, Email and the Public Website

Covers: in-app chat, the notification bell, phone push, live updates, transactional email (including the recipient's delivery-code email), reminders, notification switches, languages, support contact, the public website, and the Go services.
Related files: deliveries.md (deal states, delivery code buffer, protection window), money.md (payment and payout emails), problems-and-disputes.md, accounts-and-verification.md, trips-and-matching.md (proposal deadlines), admin-console.md (console pages), settings-and-helper.md (Settings).

## How does chat work and who can chat with whom?
- Chat is 1:1 text between the two people on a match: the sender and the traveler. Nobody else can read or write in it (a non-party gets "Not a party to this match." and a 403).
- A chat thread is simply the match. There is no group chat, no chat before payment.
- Chat opens only once the deal is funded (money paid). Before that the app says "Chat isn't available yet. Chat opens for this delivery once payment is confirmed." and offers a "Go to payment" action to the sender.
- The inbox ("Chat" tab) only lists threads whose deal was funded at some point. Empty inbox text: "No conversations. Chat opens once a delivery is paid for."
- Matches on the old product-request type are refused with reason `product_request_retired` (not in the app).

## When can a user send a message, and when is the thread read-only?
Sending needs all of: the user is a party, the deal exists, the deal is funded, and the deal is not in a closed state.
- Closed (read-only) deal statuses: `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`, `refunded`, `partially_refunded`.
- `disputed` is deliberately NOT closed: people can keep talking during a dispute (for example to arrange a return).
- After a deal closes the thread stays readable forever to both parties. The app shows "This conversation is closed. You can still read it, but new messages aren't possible."
- The thread list tells the app `can_send` true or false per thread so the composer is live or read-only.
- Reasons the server gives when sending is refused (field `reason`):

| reason | HTTP | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `not_a_party` | 403 | user is not the sender or traveler of this match |
| `no_accepted_offer` | 402 | no deal yet (offer not accepted) |
| `payment_pending` | 402 | deal exists but is not funded |
| `match_closed` | 402 | deal is closed (see list above) |
| `product_request_retired` | 402 | retired request type |

Staff cannot unlock chat for an unfunded deal from the chat itself; the rule follows the deal's funded state.

## What limits apply to messages? Are attachments allowed?
- Text only. No images, files, voice or attachments exist in chat (the message has only a text body).
- Maximum 2000 characters per message; leading/trailing spaces are trimmed; an empty message is rejected.
- Rate limit: 60 messages per minute per user (a setting fixed by the deployment, default `60/min`).
- Duplicate protection: the app sends a `client_message_id`. Re-sending the same id with the same text returns the existing message (no duplicate). Same id with different text gives 409 `chat_idempotency_conflict`.
- The chat screen shows a permanent warning: "Never send a pickup or delivery code in chat."
- Messages cannot be edited or deleted by users.

## What is shown or hidden in chat? What does read / unread mean?
- Each message shows text, sender and time. A message becomes "read" when the other person's app fetches it (opens the thread); only the other party's messages are marked read.
- The inbox row shows the other person's name, a coarse route ("PickupCity → DeliveryCity", never a street address), the last message, last time, and an unread count. Threads sort by latest activity; threads with no message yet sort by match date.
- Reading a thread resolves its chat notifications in the bell, so the badge drops.
- History can be fetched in pages of 50 (up to 200) or by cursor ("latest", "after_id", "before_id"); bad cursors return 400 with a plain message.
- Exact addresses are not part of chat; the chat does not reveal anything the deal has not already unlocked.

## Is chat moderated? Can staff read it? (report and block)
- No automatic moderation or word filter. Members report and block (App Store rule 1.2).
- Report: in a conversation, the member presses and holds a message they received and picks a reason: Spam or advertising, Harassment or threats, A scam or a request to pay outside Wassla, Offensive or inappropriate, Something else; an optional note (up to 500 characters); and "Also block this member". Only the recipient can report a message, once (a second report answers the first). The sender is never told who reported.
- Block: from the conversation's menu ("Block"), or with the report. Either member blocking stops, both ways: sending chat messages (403, reason `blocked`), new proposals, counter-offers and accepting a pending offer (403 `party_blocked`), and "I can carry this" (403 `party_blocked`). The two also stop finding each other's trips and requests in matching, a request cannot name the other as its traveler, and route alerts leave the other's listings out. The conversation stays readable to both. A delivery already paid goes on as agreed (codes, progress, payout are unchanged). Only the blocker can unblock: from the conversation's notice ("Unblock") or Profile > Blocked people.
- Staff read chat only through reports: console page "Reported chat messages" (Trust menu, `trust/chat-reports/`, capability `moderate_chat`: Trust and Super Admin). Only reported messages are shown, each with the 5 messages before and after it, every report with its reporter, reason and note. Decisions, each with a required internal reason (audited): Dismiss the reports (message unchanged), Remove the message (both members see "Removed by Wassla" instead, and the text also leaves the recipient's notifications; the text stays for staff), Restore the message. Every decision closes the message's open reports. A member who keeps sending abuse is banned from their person page (Super Admin).
- The work list shows open chat reports ("Reported chat messages"), urgent after 24 hours. The AI helper sees only that count; chat contents never reach the helper, and it cannot decide a report.
- Dispute evidence bundles only record that a conversation existed (message count, first and last message time), not the text.

## How does the notification bell work?
- The bell is in the app header. It shows a count of active unread items. Screen reader label: "Notifications, N unread".
- Two lists: "Notifications" (active) and "history" (resolved). Page size 30 (max 100).
- Reading and resolving are different. An item can be read but still "active" if it needs action; it moves to history only when the thing it asks for is done.
- Items resolve automatically when the underlying task is done, for example:
  - offer accepted, payment required and payment failed notices: once the deal is funded
  - new offer, offer updated, response reminder: once the offer is no longer pending (answered, expired) or no longer waits for this user
  - rating prompt: once the user rated or the review window closed
  - dispute action: once a dispute exists or the window closed
  - recipient required: once a recipient is added
  - delivery code available (bell item for the sender): once delivery is confirmed
  - chat message: once the message is read
  - leg reminder: once the traveler reports that leg step
  - schedule change proposed: once the sender answered or it is no longer pending
  - early arrival reported: once the sender confirms or declines
  - payout setup needed: once payout setup is ready
  - `deal.updated` and `offer.economics_changed`: silent refresh hints, treated as already resolved
  - everything else stays active until the user reads it.
- "Mark all as read" marks every unread row read (does not delete anything). Rows are never deleted.
- Empty state: "Nothing new. Offers, payments and delivery updates show up here."
- Every notification is a stored row per recipient (the inbox), whether or not push is on. In-app notifications keep working if push is off.

## When is a push notification sent to the phone?
- Push (Firebase, for a closed or background app) is sent per notification event to each recipient who has at least one active registered phone, if push is switched on for the platform (setting `FCM_ENABLED`, off by default) and the event type has push wording.
- Firebase is used ONLY for push to a closed app. Live in-app updates use the Go realtime services over a WebSocket, not Firebase.
- If the user has the app open (live connection), the Go push worker waits 2 seconds and skips the push when the live connection already delivered the event, to avoid a duplicate.
- Push text never contains names, routes, places, prices or codes: only generic wording plus ids. Lock screens never show sensitive data.
- The delivery code is never in a push, and the sender gets only "Delivery code available. Open Wassla when the recipient is ready." The traveler never gets that push.
- Push wording exists in English, French and Arabic, chosen from the user's saved email/communication language (blank or unknown falls back to English).
- Phones that Firebase reports as invalid are switched off (marked inactive). A maintenance command deactivates phones not seen for 180 days (default).

## Which events send a push? (category decides if the user can switch it off)
Essential (cannot be switched off): offer accepted, payment received, payment failed, refund update, pickup confirmed, delivery confirmed (all three "match.completed / handover.delivery_confirmed" wordings: "The 48-hour protection window has started."), delivery code available (sender only), identity verification result (approved / needs attention / new upload needed), ticket (flight or ferry) result, traveler progress report, trip leg reminder, problem reported, schedule change proposed / answered / applied / withdrawn, delivery cancelled, early arrival reported / confirmed / not confirmed, dispute opened ("Payout is paused while the dispute is reviewed."), dispute resolved, payout update, request cancelled.

Marketplace (user can switch off): request published, new offer, offer updated (including "Proposal expired: It was not answered in time. Nothing was reserved or charged."), proposal response reminder, "New on your route" (route alert), "A traveler can carry your parcel", "A parcel fits your trip".

Messages (user can switch off): new chat message ("You have a new message on Wassla.", no text of the message).

Events with no push (bell/live only): deal refresh hints, offer economics changed.
Chat push goes only to the other person, never back to the sender.

## What notification switches does the user have?
Screen: Profile > Notifications.
- Two switches: Messages ("New chat activity") and Marketplace activity ("Offers, matches, journeys and requests"). Both default ON.
- Essential updates ("Payment, delivery, dispute, verification and account-security updates stay on.") have no switch.
- The switches affect PUSH only. The bell inbox keeps every item either way. They are saved on the account, so they apply to all the user's phones.
- A separate phone-level permission is requested only when the user taps "Enable notifications". If the system permission is off the screen explains how to enable it; "Push is not configured in this build" shows when the app build has no push set up.
- Phones are registered per installation (an app-generated id). Signing out unregisters that phone.
- Staff can view these preferences and registered phones (platform, active, last success/failure; tokens are hidden) in the Django admin lists "Notification preferences" and "Push devices", read-only.

## Which reminders exist and when do they fire?
Settings live in the business Settings revision, section "marketplace" (admin Settings); unknown keys are refused when saving.

| reminder | default | range | what happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| Proposal response deadline | on, 6 hours (`offers.response_hours`) | 1 to 168 h | every proposal/counter expires at send time + 6 h, or at pickup time if earlier |
| Proposal reminder | 1 hour before deadline (`offers.reminder_before_hours`) | 0 (none) up to 167, must be smaller than response hours | one push/bell item to the person who must answer: "A proposal is waiting for your answer. Answer soon: it expires shortly." Skipped if that moment is not after the offer was sent |
| Expiry | at the deadline | - | offer becomes `expired`; both are told "Proposal expired"; nothing reserved, charged or counted against anyone |
| Depart reminder | 30 min after planned departure (`reminders.depart_after_minutes`) | 5 to 1440 | asks the traveler "How is your trip going?" |
| Arrive reminder | 60 min after planned arrival (`reminders.arrive_after_minutes`) | 5 to 1440 | same question for arrival |
| Repeat | every 180 min (`repeat_after_minutes`), max 2 repeats (`max_repeats`) | 15 to 1440; 0 to 5 | repeats until the traveler reports |
| Master switch | `reminders.enabled` on | | off stops leg reminders |

- Leg reminders only go to a traveler whose leg carries a funded parcel, and stop when that step was reported, the journey is not live, the times changed, or an earlier step is still unreported.
- Turning the deadline switch off only stops new deadlines; offers already having one keep it.
- Trip-fit alerts ("A parcel fits your trip"): on by default; at most 30 travelers told per request and at most 5 per traveler per 24 hours (`discovery.trip_fit_max_travelers`, `trip_fit_daily_cap`). "I can carry this" signals: 10 per traveler per 24 hours (`discovery.interest_daily_cap`).
- Route alerts: at most one notification per alert every 6 hours (fixed in code; the app says "at most one notification per alert every few hours").
- Email reminder: "Payment protection ends soon" is emailed to sender and traveler 24 hours before the 48-hour protection deadline (86400 s, environment value `PROTECTION_ENDING_REMINDER_SECONDS`; 0 turns it off). It is not sent if that moment already passed, and is dropped as stale if protection is no longer current.

## What emails does the app send, and when?
Every email is tied to a durable record (visible in Django admin "Outbound messages"). Kinds and triggers:

| email (subject) | to | when |
|---|---|---|
| Confirm your Wassla email (6-digit code) | new user | at signup; code valid 15 min (`EMAIL_VERIFY_CODE_TTL_SECONDS`), 5 attempts (`EMAIL_VERIFY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`) |
| Reset your Wassla password (code) | user | password reset request |
| Wassla security notice | user | after password reset completes |
| You have been invited to Wassla Operations (private one-time link) | invited staff | admin invites staff |
| Wassla identity verification update | traveler | staff approve, reject, or ask for new upload of ID |
| Wassla flight ticket update / Wassla ferry ticket update | traveler | staff decide on a ticket proof |
| Your Wassla payment needs attention (failed) | payer account | a payment attempt truly failed (not expiry/cancel); withdrawn if later success is proven |
| Your Wassla payment receipt / Your Wassla guest payment needs attention | guest payer (no account) | guest-link payment succeeded or failed |
| Wassla refund update | account owner (+ guest payer if they paid) | refund started or completed |
| Pickup confirmed | sender | traveler's pickup confirmed; mentions the delivery-code buffer minutes (default 30) |
| Your delivery code is now available (no code inside) | sender | buffer ended and code released: "The code is not in this email. Open the app to see it." |
| Your Wassla delivery code | parcel recipient | same moment; this is the only email that contains a code |
| Delivery confirmed | sender and traveler | recipient's code accepted; states protection end time |
| Payment protection ends soon | sender and traveler | 24 h before protection ends |
| Payment protection has ended | sender and traveler | protection closed with no dispute |
| A dispute has been opened | sender and traveler | dispute opened |
| Your dispute has been resolved | sender and traveler | resolution recorded (full sender refund, full traveler payout, partial split) |
| Payout update | traveler | payout becomes eligible, needs payout setup, is processing, sent, paid, needs attention, or the bank returned it |
| Delivery cancelled | sender and traveler | deal cancelled (reason: by sender, traveler, admin, or declined new times) |
| Leave a review | sender and traveler | at delivery confirmation (review window opens) |

- Not used by any code path today (templates exist but nothing arms them): "Payment needed for your Wassla delivery", "Your Wassla payment is processing", "Wassla payment confirmed" for account owners, and "More information is needed for your Wassla case" (evidence request). Do not promise these emails.
- Offers, chat, route alerts and similar marketplace events are NOT emailed; they are bell/push only.
- Emails are plain, branded layout with code box, fact rows (tracking number, payment reference, amount in EUR, dispute reference).

## How does the recipient get the delivery code by email?
- The recipient (the person named by the sender after funding) does not need an account. Their email comes from the recipient details the sender entered ("The delivery code is sent here. Make sure it's right.").
- Timeline: traveler confirms pickup, then a 30-minute safety buffer (1800 s default, set by lifecycle policy), then the code is released. At release the system queues the recipient email (up to 12 send attempts) and the sender's "code available" email.
- Subject "Your Wassla delivery code", heading "Your parcel is on its way", box "Your delivery code", hint "Keep this code private until the parcel is in your hands." Includes tracking number and the sender's name ("Your sender" if blank).
- Language: the recipient's own chosen language (EN/FR/AR), set when the sender adds the recipient ("This is the language we'll use for the recipient's delivery email"); blank falls back to English.
- Safety: the code is never stored in the email record, never in push, bell, logs or the traveler's view. It is sealed and opened only at the moment the email is built. The traveler can never retrieve it.
- If the recipient email address is changed or the code is no longer active, the old email is obsolete and is not sent. If no recipient exists at release time (should not happen), the error is logged and the sender still holds the code.
- Staff cannot read the code from the console; the Outbound messages list only shows "Sealed secret: Yes" and the detail page "Carries a sealed secret: Yes".

## What languages are used? How is Arabic / right-to-left handled?
- Three languages everywhere: English (en), French (fr), Arabic (ar). English is the fallback.
- App language: a per-phone setting (Profile > Language), default follows the device; applies instantly; stored on the phone only. Arabic switches the app to right-to-left.
- Email language ("what we write to you in": payments, verification, deliveries, disputes, account security): saved on the account (`preferred_language`), chosen at signup or Profile > Language. Blank = English.
- The language is frozen on each email at the moment it is queued; changing language later does not change emails already queued.
- Guest payers get emails in the language chosen on their guest link. Recipients get the language on the deal's recipient record.
- Emails in Arabic use right-to-left layout; amounts and references are wrapped so they do not flip.
- Push text is chosen from the user's saved language (en/fr/ar).
- Translations of email text live in the French and Arabic message catalogs; missing text falls back to English.

## What happens when an email fails to send?
- Each email is a durable row with status: `pending`, `dispatched` (accepted by the mail provider), `failed`, `cancelled`. Visible to staff in Django admin > Outbound messages (filter by status, kind, language; search by email, key or error). It is read-only; rows cannot be deleted or edited.
- Master switch: `EMAIL_ENABLED` (deployment setting, default off). While off, nothing is sent and rows stay pending; they can still be sent after activation. Email is required for going live with payments.
- Retries: wait 60 s, doubling each attempt, capped at 6 hours. Default max 10 attempts (12 for the recipient delivery code).
- Permanent provider refusal (Sender.net answering 400 or 422, or an SMTP 5xx code such as a bad address) fails at once with `provider_permanent`; temporary trouble (a timeout, a rate limit, a token or account problem, a provider outage) is `provider_transient` until attempts run out.
- A message that can never be built (missing secret, no longer valid) is marked failed with `email_context_or_capability_unavailable`.
- Sending is rate-limited to 60 emails per minute in total (`EMAIL_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE`, 1 to 600); Django and the Go email worker share the same limit.
- Old backlog guard: a message older than 48 hours (or created before the activation cutoff) is held (`activation_held`) and is not sent until an operator reviews it. Reviews are explicit and audited: "release" or "cancel" through a maintenance command that never sends mail itself; an audit command lists the backlog.
- Messages can be cancelled when superseded (e.g. payment failed notice cancelled after later success).
- The console System page shows an Email status (enabled, configuration complete or incomplete). The health check counts pending and failed outbound messages and the length of the email queue; push has its own status (disabled, configuration incomplete, ready, degraded).
- Delivery is "at least once": rarely a duplicate can occur after a crash. Provider acceptance is not proof the inbox received it.

## How do live (real-time) updates work?
- The app keeps a live WebSocket to the Go notification service (`/ws/notifications`) and, in an open chat, to the Go chat service (`/ws/chat`). The login token is checked on connect.
- The server (Django) saves the event first, then publishes it after the database commit; Go only relays it to the user's open connections. If Go or Redis is down, Django keeps working and a retry job re-sends the event (first retry about 30 seconds later).
- When a live event arrives the app re-reads data from the server (the server stays authoritative), then updates the screen; events are merged within about 75 ms.
- Chat history is always reloaded from Django, so missed live messages are recovered by cursor on reconnect.
- Presence: a "user is online" marker lasts 15 seconds and is refreshed while connected; ping interval is 10 seconds.

## What are the Go services in backend/services?
- `notification`: live WebSocket hub for bell/live events, plus the worker that sends Firebase push to closed apps (only if push is enabled and no live delivery happened within 2 s).
- `chat`: relays new chat messages to the two people's open chat sockets. Read-only relay; it never writes messages (Django does).
- `email`: reads the email queue and sends ordinary (non-secret) emails through Sender.net's API (or an SMTP relay in development), records delivery receipts. Gated by `EMAIL_ENABLED`. Code-bearing emails (verification, reset, invitation, recipient delivery code) are sent by Django directly so codes never go through the queue.
- `kyc`: accepts identity-document uploads (JPEG or PNG, max 8 MiB per image, rate-limited), stores them in object storage and records the submission in Django. See accounts-and-verification.md.
- Shared libraries (pkg) cover login-token checks, config, database, health checks, retries, metrics, Redis and storage.

## What does the public website contain?
Static site in `web/` (no build step). Routes: `/en/`, `/fr/`, `/ar/` (Arabic is right-to-left), plus `/terms.html`, `/privacy.html`, `/prohibited-items.html`, `/support.html`. The root page links to the three languages. The legal and support pages are English only; their language links open the localized home page.
- Home page sections: hero ("Your package already has a ride"; trust chips: travelers pass identity checks, payment held until delivery, exact addresses private until funded); journeys as legs (flight legs need approved ticket proof, drive legs need no vehicle document at launch, capacity per leg); how to send (request, match, agree, pay, hand over, protection) and how to carry (verify, journey, offers, carry, confirm, payout); protections (private until funded, posting deposit credited not double charged, sender proposes first, two codes, deliberate delay, protection period, disputes freeze payout); price (example: €30.00 reward + €7.50 Wassla fee = €37.50 paid; the fee is added on top, so the traveler gets the full €30.00; EUR is canonical and a Chargily DZD figure at checkout is a platform conversion, not a second price); trust checks; FAQ (what can I send, who can carry, when does the traveler get paid, cancellations, why the traveler cannot see the delivery code, is Wassla available now); contact link.
- Terms (draft): use of Wassla, matching and agreements, payment and 48-hour protection, handoffs and prohibited use, cancellations/disputes/ratings, contact. Privacy (draft): data used, how, sharing, retention (periods not yet stated), user rights via support, cookies (no advertising cookies). Prohibited items (draft): weapons, illegal drugs and counterfeit goods, live animals/human remains/hazardous materials, stolen goods or cash equivalents or customs evasion, anything illegal in departure/transit/arrival country or by carrier; sender responsible for customs declarations; a match is not customs clearance.
- All legal pages say they are drafts pending French, EU and Algerian legal review. The site claims no payment provider is live.
- The in-app Profile has "Terms" and "Privacy" rows that open these web pages (web base address set in the build; shows "link could not be opened" if missing).
- The sitemap and robots files name the site's address, `https://wassla.eu`.

## How do users contact support?
- Website support page lists four mailboxes (placeholders marked "must be replaced with verified support mailboxes before launch"): general support, safety and disputes, privacy requests, payment questions. Users should include name, account email, and a deal or payment reference; never a handover code ("no one at Wassla will ever ask you for one"). Not an emergency service.
- In the app there is no support button or contact row. Profile's "Help and support" section holds only Terms of service and Privacy policy. Some screens only tell the user in text to contact support: the closed dispute window ("Disputes can be opened for 48 hours after delivery. Contact support if you still need help."; no button is drawn), the email-code screen if the code never arrives, and a journey paused by staff.
- Emails: all mail comes from "Wassla <contact@wassla.eu>", which is also the reply-to and the support address. These are deployment settings (`EMAIL_FROM_ADDR`, `EMAIL_SUPPORT_ADDR`, `EMAIL_REPLY_TO`, sender name default "Wassla"), and contact@wassla.eu is the default for each; the admin invitation email tells recipients to "contact Wassla support" if unexpected.
- The server tells the app the same address: `GET /api/support` (no sign-in, limit 30 a minute) answers `{"email": <EMAIL_SUPPORT_ADDR>}`, which is contact@wassla.eu unless the deployment names another (a blank setting also means contact@wassla.eu), so it is never null. The app's "Contact support" action opens an email to it (the delivery's tracking number goes in the subject when asked from a delivery). It appears in Profile, on the dispute screen, with the problem list on a delivery, and on a journey paused by staff, and is absent everywhere until the address has loaded (or when the server cannot be reached).

## Worked examples
- Sender pays at 10:00 and the traveler confirms pickup at 14:00: pickup email to the sender at 14:00 saying the code appears in 30 minutes; at 14:30 the recipient gets "Your Wassla delivery code" by email and the sender gets "Your delivery code is now available" (no code inside). The traveler sees nothing with the code.
- Delivery confirmed at 09:00 Monday: protection ends 09:00 Wednesday; "protection ends soon" email goes at 09:00 Tuesday; "protection has ended" after Wednesday 09:00 if no dispute.
- Proposal sent 12:00 with default 6 h: expires 18:00, reminder to the answerer at 17:00. If pickup time is 15:00, expiry becomes 15:00 and the reminder 14:00.
- User turns Messages push off: new chat message still lands in the bell and the chat thread; no phone push. An offer accepted notice still pushes (essential).

## Not covered here
- Exact copy of the French and Arabic email texts (kept in the translation catalogs).
- The real support mailbox addresses and final live site domain (placeholders in the repo).
- Exact environment values in production for `FCM_ENABLED` and `EMAIL_ENABLED`.
- Retention periods for messages and notifications (privacy page says not yet defined; no automatic deletion found for chat or notifications).


==== NOTES: knowledge/money.md ====

# Money movement: payments, refunds, payouts, finance console

Covers: how senders pay, how deals get funded, refunds, how travelers are paid out (EUR and DZD), and who can do what in the finance console.
Related files: pricing.md (how the price and commission are set), deliveries.md (deal states, cancellation, delivery code), problems-and-disputes.md (dispute outcomes), admin-console.md (roles, console pages), settings-and-helper.md (Settings page), accounts-and-verification.md (identity checks)

## What currency is money held in?
- EUR is the real currency. Every amount is stored as whole euro cents (1250 = 12.50 EUR).
- DZD (dinars) only appears at the edges: Chargily charges the sender in DZD, and the DZD manual payout pays the traveler in DZD. Both are converted from EUR with the exchange rate setting.
- Conversion: `EUR cents x rate / 100`, always rounded UP to a whole dinar. Example: 12.00 EUR at rate 150 DZD per EUR = 1800 DZD. 12.34 EUR = 1851 DZD (1851.00 exactly, no rounding needed here); 10.01 EUR at 150.25 = 1504.0025 so 1505 DZD.
- The rate is a setting: Settings > "Chargily exchange rate" card, field "DZD for €1" (up to 6 decimals) with a required "Rate source or reason". Stored key `payments.chargily.eur_dzd_rate_micros`, shown read-only as "Chargily EUR→DZD rate". Seeded value 150 DZD per EUR is a placeholder; staff must set it. A rate above 100,000 DZD per EUR is refused as a typing error.
- The rate is frozen on each payment attempt when checkout starts. Changing it later changes nothing already started. The traveler's DZD payout amount is also frozen when the deal is funded (see the DZD payout section for which rate).
- Smallest Chargily charge: 75 DZD (setting `payments.chargily.min_amount_dzd`, no console form). Below it the server refuses with `amount_below_provider_minimum` and the app shows Chargily as "Below this method's minimum".

## What does a sender pay in total?
- The sender pays the traveler's reward plus the platform commission on top. Commission is 25% (`commission_rate_bps` = 2500), rounded up to the cent.
- Example: reward 8.00 EUR gives commission 2.00 EUR, sender total 10.00 EUR. Reward 8.01 EUR gives commission 2.0025 so 2.01 EUR, total 10.02 EUR.
- The traveler is paid the full reward (plus the whole Boost, if any). The commission stays with the platform.
- A Boost is extra reward the sender adds; it carries its own commission (seeded 25%, `boost.commission_rate_bps`), and the Boost amount plus its commission is added to the single balance the sender pays. See pricing.md.

## What payment obligations ("orders") exist?
Each obligation is a payment order in EUR with a purpose:
| Purpose | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `posting_deposit` | Money paid up front to publish a request |
| `deal_balance` | The deal total, after a traveler offer is accepted |
| `boost` | Not used. There is no paid boost: a Boost is folded into the deal balance. |

Order fields staff see: amount owed, credited (covered by a deposit, no new cash), paid (cash captured), refunded. Outstanding = owed - credited - paid. The database refuses over-collecting and refunding more than was captured.

Order statuses: `pending`, `partially_paid`, `paid`, `refund_pending`, `partially_refunded`, `refunded`, `cancelled`. New checkouts are only possible while `pending` or `partially_paid`.

## What is the posting deposit and how big is it?
- Default mode is `posting_deposit` (setting `payments.timing_mode`; the other value `after_acceptance` means no deposit). The sender pays the deposit to publish a request; the request stays `awaiting_deposit` until the deposit is paid, then becomes `open`.
- Recommended deposit = 10% (`percent_bps` 1000) of the recommended sender total, kept between 3.00 EUR (`min_eur_cents` 300) and 7.00 EUR (`max_eur_cents` 700). Example: total 20 EUR gives 2.00, raised to 3.00. Total 50 EUR gives 5.00. Total 100 EUR gives 10.00, capped at 7.00.
- The recommendation is based on the straight-line pickup-to-delivery distance with no detour or urgency; Boost does not change it.
- The sender may choose another amount: minimum 3.00 EUR (`chosen_min_eur_cents` 300), maximum the whole obligation (chosen reward + commission + any Boost with its commission). The 7 EUR cap is NOT a limit on a chosen deposit. Errors: `deposit_below_minimum` ("That deposit is below the minimum."), `deposit_above_obligation` ("A deposit cannot exceed the amount it is paid against.").
- A chosen deposit can only be changed while its order is pending, nothing paid, no open checkout (`deposit_checkout_in_progress`: "Finish or abandon the open checkout before changing the deposit.").
- When a traveler's offer is accepted, a paid deposit is credited 100% toward the deal balance (not an extra fee). The credit is used at most once. If the deposit equals the whole total, the deal is funded straight away.
- App text: "If nobody takes it, or you cancel before accepting an offer, you get it back in full." When the request deadline passes unmatched, a scheduled job refunds the deposit (reason "Request expired unmatched"; extra wait `expiry_grace_seconds`, seeded 0).
- Settings keys are in Settings, payments section. Quoted order amounts are frozen on the order, so later setting changes do not alter them.

## How does a sender pay (card, Chargily, guest)?
- Pay screen lists rails from the server; the app never sends an amount or currency (the server rejects that with `client_supplied_amount_rejected`).
- Stripe: pays in EUR, card (Visa, Mastercard, others). Chargily: pays in DZD, Algerian cards (CIB, Edahabia). The rail decides the currency; there is no Stripe-in-dinars.
- Tapping pay opens the provider's hosted page. Coming back from it is NOT proof of payment: the app shows "Checking your payment" and re-reads the order from the server (9 checks spread over about 1.5 minutes: 3, 3, 5, 5, 10, 10, 20, 20, 20 seconds), then offers "Check again". Only the provider's confirmation (webhook or reconciliation) marks it paid.
- Only one open checkout per order. Starting again with the same rail, amount and payer resumes the open one ("A checkout is already open"). Switching rail or amount cancels the old attempt with failure code `superseded`.
- Checkout attempt lifetime: 3600 s = 1 hour (setting `payments.checkout.attempt_ttl_seconds`, allowed 5 min to 7 days). A safety check against the provider runs 5 minutes after expiry.
- Rail availability reasons shown to sender: "Not available yet" (no credentials), "Temporarily switched off", "Not ready yet" (bad configuration), "Not taking new payments right now" (new checkouts off), "Below this method's minimum". If none: "No payment method available ... Nothing has been charged".
- Two Chargily switches: `payments.providers.chargily_enabled` removes it from the list; `payments.chargily.new_checkouts_enabled` stops new checkouts only while webhooks, reconciliation, refunds and history keep working (the incident switch). Same idea for `stripe_enabled`. A `mock` rail exists for tests only (`mock_enabled`, must be off in production).
- The shipped seed settings have both rails and new Chargily checkouts switched OFF (and automatic Stripe payouts off). The live values are on Settings > "Provider availability" (admins can change them: "Accept new Stripe payments", "Allow Chargily", "Accept new Chargily checkouts"; reason required).
- Environment safety: each payment records TEST or LIVE mode; mismatches are refused.

## What are the payment attempt statuses?
`created` (row made), `checkout_pending` (provider page open), `processing`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `expired`, `cancelled`. Open = created/checkout_pending/processing; the others are final. A truly failed attempt (not an expired or cancelled one) emails the order owner, and also the guest payer if a guest paid. App wording: "Payment didn't go through" / "Nothing was charged. You can try again or use a different method."
Unapplied payment: money captured that no order could take (for example the order was closed meanwhile). It is refunded in full automatically (refund reason "Payment could not be applied") and shown in the Payments attention queue.

## How does a guest (someone else) pay?
- The order owner creates a payment link (app: "Someone else can pay", "Create payment link"). The payer needs no account.
- One live link per order; creating a new one revokes the old. Link lifetime 3 days (259,200 s, setting `payments.guest.link_ttl_seconds`, 10 min to 30 days).
- The guest sees only amount, currency and a generic purpose. No access to delivery, chat, disputes or personal details. The guest gives an email for the receipt and refund notices (required only when Wassla's transactional email is switched on).
- Only the order owner can create or revoke a link. Only the guest-capable rails are offered. The owner sees "Someone is paying now" while a guest checkout is open; the link cannot be revoked then ("Someone is paying with this link right now, so it cannot be revoked."). App states: "Payment link ready", "Someone is paying now", "This payment link has expired", "This payment link is no longer active". Invalid link for the guest: "It may have expired, been cancelled, or already been paid."
- Staff cannot see or re-create the link; only a hash is stored. The owner cannot see it again either ("For security it can't be shown again."); they create a new one, which stops the old one.

## When is a deal "funded"?
When the deal balance order is fully covered (cash paid plus deposit credit). Then: the deal moves to funded, the ledger records the sender's money as held (traveler part and platform commission part), a Boost's traveler/platform split is recorded, and the traveler's payout record is created (amount = traveler reward plus the whole Boost). Exact locations unlock after funding (see deliveries.md). If the grace time after acceptance runs out unpaid, the deal expires (`payment_grace_seconds`, seeded 3600 s = 1 hour, no console form; see deliveries.md).
Rule: when payout profiles are on (deployment flag `PAYOUT_PROFILES_ENABLED`), the sender cannot start paying the deal balance until the traveler has a payout method that fits the chosen rail ("Payout preference required before funding."). An EUR payout method also needs Stripe funding ("This payout requires Stripe funding.").

## Who can request a refund and for what reasons?
- Only staff with capability `issue_refunds` (Finance and Super Admin) can request a refund by hand: Finance > Payments, open a succeeded payment, "Request refund". Form: "Refund amount (€)" (min 0.01), "Reason", plus the tick-box "I understand the provider may receive this refund request immediately." Users cannot request refunds themselves; refunds also start automatically from the platform.
- Reasons: Request expired unmatched (`deposit_expiry`), Sender cancelled before acceptance (`sender_cancelled`), Payment could not be applied (`unapplied_payment`), Deal cancelled before funding (`deal_cancelled`), Administrative refund (`admin`), Deal cancelled after funding (`deal_cancelled_funded`), Dispute resolution (`dispute_resolution`), Verified no-show (`no_show`).
- Only a `succeeded` payment that an order took can be refunded ("Only a succeeded attempt can be refunded.").
- Partial refunds are allowed. Total refunds on one payment can never exceed what it captured: "A refund cannot exceed the captured amount." Money already reserved for a traveler payout cannot also be refunded: "This capture is reserved for a Traveler payout and cannot also fund a refund of this size."
- Asking twice for the same payment and reason returns the existing refund (no double refund).
- Every manual action is written to the audit log.

## What are refund statuses and how do they settle?
Statuses: `pending`, `processing`, `succeeded`, `failed`.
- Stripe: the refund is sent to Stripe right away. Provider unreachable: retried with growing waits (1 min, doubling, at most 6 h apart). After 8 tries it is flagged as needing manual action. A refund the provider reports as still pending is re-checked after 5 minutes.
- Chargily has NO refund API. Its refund stays `pending` with "Manual required" until staff send the money back outside Wassla (bank transfer) and record it. Re-checked every 6 hours.
- A provider refusal does not cancel the sender's right to the money: it becomes pending with manual action required.
- Manual settlement: open the refund (Finance > Refunds, or "Refunds & disputes" when the finance dashboard flag is on), enter "Settlement reference" (required, max 128 chars), optional "Note", tick "I confirm the refund was sent outside Wassla and the reference is correct." Needs capability `settle_manual_refunds` (Finance, Super Admin). Allowed on a pending, processing or failed refund ("Only an unresolved refund can be settled by hand"). Without a reference: "A manual refund settlement requires a reference."
- Order status follows: refund in progress = `refund_pending`; some back = `partially_refunded`; all back = `refunded`.
- Sender gets refund status emails (pending / done).

## What refunds happen when a deal is cancelled or disputed?
- Before funding: any captured money is refunded; orders cancelled.
- After funding, sender cancellation: more than 24 h (86,400 s) before agreed pickup gets everything back (exactly 24 h or later counts as late). Inside that cutoff the traveler gets 10% of the base reward (`sender_late_compensation_bps` 1000, rounded up, boost not counted) capped at 15.00 EUR (1500 cents); the rest goes back to the sender; platform keeps nothing. These are stored settings shown read-only in Settings > "Lifecycle guardrails"; no console form changes them. They are copied onto each deal at funding. See deliveries.md for who can cancel.
- Dispute resolution: an admin enters a "Sender refund (€)" and optionally a "Traveler payout (€)". The three shares (sender, traveler, platform) must add up to what was collected, and money already paid out cannot be clawed back. If no traveler payout is entered, the remainder not returned is shared between traveler and platform in the original ratio (`partial_split_fee_mode` proportional). If a traveler payout is entered, the platform keeps whatever remains (a negative remainder is refused). Refunds are taken from the balance order first, then the deposit. Example: collected 10.00 EUR (reward 8, fee 2), sender refund 4.00 leaves 6.00; traveler gets 6 x 8/10 = 4.80, platform 1.20.
- Errors: `settlement_refund_out_of_range`, `settlement_payout_out_of_range`, `settlement_exceeds_paid_out`, `settlement_does_not_reconcile`.

## How does a traveler get paid? The overall path
1. Funding creates the payout (status `not_eligible`). Its rail is fixed then, from the traveler's payout preference and how the sender paid.
2. Delivery confirmed by the delivery code starts the 48-hour protection window (172,800 s, setting `payments.payout.protection_window_seconds`, 0 to 30 days).
3. A release check runs when protection ends. All must hold: delivery confirmed, protection window passed, the funded arrival floor passed (the agreed arrival time frozen at funding; payout waits for the later of this and protection end), no active dispute, balance fully paid with nothing refunded or being refunded, no finance hold, payout method usable.
4. If all pass: `eligible`. If setup is missing: `blocked`. If a dispute is active: `frozen`.
5. EUR: paid automatically (if enabled). DZD: a Finance person sends the dinars by hand.
6. The deal is marked completed once protection ends cleanly; the payout then lives on its own.
Nothing here is client-side; the app only displays server state.

## What are the payout statuses?
| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `not_eligible` | Protection window or other release condition not yet met |
| `eligible` | Released, waiting for the rail (EUR: automatic; DZD: waiting for Finance) |
| `scheduled` | EUR: reserved for execution. DZD: claimed by a Finance operator |
| `processing` | Money instruction committed; EUR with provider, DZD transfer in progress |
| `sent` | EUR: bank payout in transit. DZD: operator says the transfer is sent, settlement not confirmed |
| `paid` | Done and recorded in the ledger |
| `blocked` | Cannot progress; see block reason |
| `failed` | Attempt failed or bank returned it; traveler still owed |
| `frozen` | An active dispute holds the money |
| `cancelled` | Closed, no payout |
A paid payout can go back to `failed` only on a genuine bank return. A freeze cannot recall money already committed externally; the note records the exposure for recovery review.
Traveler-app wording: Awaiting delivery, Protection period, Awaiting release, Payout ready, On the way, Payout sent, Paid, Needs attention, On hold ("A dispute is open on this delivery, so the payout is on hold until it's resolved.").

## What are the payout block reasons?
- `payout_preference_required`: traveler has not chosen a payout method; nowhere to send.
- `payout_setup_required`: method chosen but not ready (EUR account not verified, or DZD profile not approved).
- `fx_snapshot_missing`: DZD amount cannot be fixed without a rate (rate missing at funding).
- `funding_route_unavailable`: EUR payout but the sender paid by Chargily or mixed funding (EUR needs Stripe funding).
- `provider_mode_mismatch`: payout account is in a different TEST/LIVE mode.
- `funding_mode_unknown`: old payment with unknown TEST/LIVE mode.
- `legacy_instruction_required`: old payout from before versioned instructions; not settled by this system.
- `payout_below_minimum`: EUR payout under 1.00 EUR (100 cents; deployment setting, minimum 1 cent); held as a treasury hold, retried later.
- `connected_balance_pending`: money reached the traveler's Stripe account but is not yet available to pay out; waits.
- Traveler-facing reasons: payout setup required, profile under review, profile needs attention, on hold, dispute open, attempt failed, bank payout returned, country not supported for Stripe EUR payouts.
- Finance holds (kinds: dispute, provider_dispute, fraud, refund, compliance, treasury, manual) also stop a payout; clearing a hold returns it to where it was.

## How does the EUR payout (Stripe Connect) work?
- Changing payout setup (choosing a payout method, the DZD profile and its proof, Stripe onboarding, refresh and dashboard) needs a confirmed email since L18A: otherwise `email_verification_required`, nothing saved. Reading it does not. Reason: someone who opened an account under another person's email must not leave a payout method behind for the real owner to inherit.
- When payout setup cannot be changed, the Payout methods read still shows the saved choice and history, offers no button, and names the reason; a direct change is refused with the same code: `payout_profiles_disabled` (deployment flag off), `payout_traveler_required` (sender-only, deactivated or banned account), `email_verification_required`, `stripe_connect_unavailable` (EUR onboarding off), `stripe_connect_no_countries` (EUR on, no country enabled), `payout_country_unsupported` (that country not enabled; use DZD), `payout_evidence_unavailable` (private cheque store missing or down: no DZD form, choosing DZD still works). A Stripe outage is `stripe_connect_provider_error`, nothing changed.
- Traveler sets up a Stripe connected account (Profile, Payout methods, "Set up EUR payouts"), declares the legal country of the account. Allowed countries are FR, DE, ES at most, and only those the deployment lists as enabled (empty by default). Algeria is deliberately not allowed; those travelers use DZD ("If your country is not listed, use DZD payouts.").
- Two steps: (1) platform Transfer moves EUR into the traveler's Stripe account; (2) Stripe bank payout to their IBAN. Traveler counts as paid only when the bank payout is `paid`. A failed bank payout is retried by creating another bank payout, never a new Transfer.
- Automatic execution needs ALL: deployment flags (payout profiles, Stripe Connect, Connect payouts on) AND the setting `payments.payout.auto_stripe_enabled` (seeded false). Otherwise payouts wait (checked every 6 h). No manual "Pay" button exists on this rail.
- Account readiness is re-read before sending (reused up to 15 min). Not ready: deferred, retried.
- Unknown outcome (timeout) is never resent blindly; the same idempotency key is replayed within 23 h or Finance investigates.
- Retry by staff: capability `retry_payouts`, only on a `failed` bank payout with a completed transfer, no Finance hold, nothing unknown, no live bank payout. Errors: "Only a failed bank payout can be retried.", "A Finance hold is active.", "This payout changed while the page was open."
- Bank payout stages: planned, committed, unknown, pending, in_transit, paid, failed, canceled, returned.

## How does the DZD manual payout work?
- Traveler adds: first and last name, CCP account number (1 to 20 digits), CCP key (2 digits), RIP (20 digits), and a clear photo of the full crossed cheque. Must be in the traveler's own name. Applies to future payouts only; funded payouts keep their original destination.
- Review (Finance and Super Admin; needs `review_payout_profiles`, `view_payout_sensitive` and `view_payout_evidence`): Finance > "Payout method reviews". The cheque photo must be fully uploaded ("Full crossed cheque evidence required."). Outcomes: approved; needs attention (traveler asked to correct and resubmit); rejected ("This CCP account was not accepted for payouts. Submit a different account in your own name."). Approval needs a current identity attestation ("Current attested identity required.", see below). If the account name does not match the attested identity, approval needs an explicit "accept name difference" tick, else it is recorded as needs attention.
- Showing the real CCP/RIP numbers needs `view_payout_sensitive` and is audited. Numbers are encrypted; console shows only last four digits otherwise.
- Operator steps on the payout's "Manual DZD payout" page: "Claim and prepare transfer" (reserves source funds; one operator owns it) -> "Start the transfer" (commit) -> send the dinars outside Wassla -> "Record transfer evidence" (JPEG, PNG or WebP image, at most 8 MiB, plus an attestation tick; tick "The receipt proves the transfer completed" to record settlement as well) -> payout `sent` then `paid`. Only the claiming operator can continue. "Release the claim" works only before the transfer is started. Receipts are never replaced; a correction needs a new upload. "Reveal payout details" shows the CCP data (audited).
- Needs capabilities view_payouts, retry_payouts, view_payout_sensitive, view_payout_evidence, and the deployment flag PAYOUT_DZD_EXECUTION_ENABLED (off by default: "Manual DZD execution is disabled.").
- DZD amount = frozen EUR amount x frozen rate rounded up (see first section). Example: payout 8.00 EUR at 150 = 1200 DZD. If the sender paid by Chargily, the rate is that attempt's rate; if by Stripe, the setting rate at funding.
- Console refusals: "Another operator has already claimed this payout.", "Reviewed profile and crossed cheque are required.", "Delivery, protection or hold gate prevents progression.", "Committed instructions require recovery review." (use "Hold for correction or recovery review" instead).
- Marking paid writes the ledger entry discharging the traveler payable.

## What is a payout identity check?
Before a DZD profile can be approved, a reviewer with `attest_payout_identity` (Trust / Verification, Super Admin) must be assigned, and records the traveler's legal given and family names from an approved ID verification (KYC, not expired). Name comparison is only an aid: results consistent, review (alias/spelling/script difference) or mismatch; a human always decides. Only the attesting reviewer can revoke an attestation. If the ID expires or the attestation is revoked or replaced, the profile approval stops counting for new payouts (an already funded payout may keep its original approval unless revoked).

## Which console pages exist for money and who can open them?
All are in the console's Finance menu (path under the console).
| Menu item (path) | Purpose | Capability |
|---|---|---|
| Overview (finance/dashboard) | Landing: liabilities, health, integrity | view_finance_summary + flag |
| Payouts (finance/payouts-hub) | Payout queues | view_finance_summary + flag |
| Manual DZD (finance/manual-dzd) | Dinar payouts waiting for a person | view_finance_summary + flag |
| Payout method reviews (finance/payout-reviews) | DZD payout profile queue | review_payout_profiles (always shown) |
| Refunds & disputes (finance/exceptions) | Refund and dispute exceptions | view_finance_summary + flag |
| Reconciliation (finance/reconciliation) | Integrity checks | view_finance_summary + flag |
| Exports (finance/exports) | CSV: transactions, commissions, payouts | export_finance (always shown) |
| Payments (finance/payments) | Every payment attempt; reconcile, resolve, refund | view_payment_attempts or view_payment_orders |
| Refunds (finance/refunds) | Refund list | issue_refunds or settle_manual_refunds |
| Payouts (finance/payouts) | Raw payout list and detail | view_payouts |
| Payout accounts (finance/payout-accounts) | Stripe account state | view_finance_summary |
| Ledger (finance/ledger) | Double-entry records | reconcile_finance |
"+ flag" = only when the deployment flag `FINANCE_DASHBOARD_ENABLED` is on (default off; the page then answers "not found"). When that flag is on, the raw Refunds, Payouts, Payout accounts and Ledger items leave the menu (the new pages front them); when it is off they are the way in. Background jobs live under System > Background jobs (system/jobs; view_scheduled_jobs or view_operational_incidents). Payout identity checks are a Trust page (attest_payout_identity).
Payments search: tracking number, order reference, payer email, guest email, provider payment id. "Attention" filter shows items needing action.

## Which roles may do which finance actions?
Roles: Ops, Support, Finance, Trust / Verification, Super Admin (all codes).
- Finance: `view_payment_orders`, `view_payment_attempts`, `view_provider_events`, `view_payouts`, `issue_refunds`, `settle_manual_refunds`, `settle_payouts`, `reconcile_finance`, `view_scheduled_jobs`, `export_finance`, `view_finance_summary`, `view_payout_sensitive`, `view_payout_evidence`, `review_payout_profiles`, `manage_payout_holds`, `retry_payouts`, `resolve_disputes`.
- Trust / Verification: `attest_payout_identity`, `manage_payout_holds`; no refunds or payout settlement.
- Ops, Support: no payment, refund or payout capabilities. (Ops and Trust can record a no-show, and Support can manage cancellations; those flows may start refunds automatically.)
- Super Admin: every capability.
- Exports and some actions need the staff two-step sign-in (see admin-console.md).

## What do reconciliation and the money jobs do?
- Reconcile payment (Payments, open a payment that is not succeeded or is unapplied, "Retry reconciliation", page "Retry payment reconciliation"; needs `reconcile_finance`; "Reason for reconciliation" and the tick "Queue provider/refund reconciliation. This does not mark the payment paid."): queues a check against the provider. It never marks a payment paid by itself.
- "Resolve attention item" (page "Resolve payment attention item", `reconcile_finance`): archives it as resolved, dismissed or superseded; history kept. "Unapplied money cannot be dismissed before its full refund succeeds."
- Webhooks: each provider event is stored once (unique id); duplicates are no-ops; results received, processing, applied, ignored, retryable, failed.
- Jobs (durable): deposit expiry refund, payment grace release, attempt expiry, provider reconcile, provider event process, refund reconcile, payout release check, payout execute/reconcile/account refresh/sweep, protection expiry, delivery-code release. Statuses pending, running, succeeded, failed, cancelled; default 8 tries, most payment, refund and payout jobs 32. Failed jobs of kinds deposit expiry refund, payment grace release, attempt expiry, provider reconcile, provider event process, refund reconcile and payout release check: only `reconcile_finance` (Finance, Super Admin) can retry or resolve them, with a reason. Other jobs: `manage_lifecycle` (Ops) or `reconcile_finance`.
- Finance dashboard integrity states: "Finance data reconciles", "Reconciled, with a stated limitation", "Finance data does not reconcile" (treat totals as unreliable). Modes TEST, LIVE, Legacy/unknown are reported separately.

## Is there a wallet?
No. The old wallet and withdrawal tables and the old payment intent tables are history only (view-only in the Django admin, API removed). All V1 money uses orders, attempts, refunds, ledger and payouts. Do not tell users they have a wallet balance.

## Not covered here
- Live values of settings (current rate, provider switches): the code only has the shipped defaults; the live values are on the Settings page.
- Which Stripe Connect countries are enabled in production (deployment setting).
- Provider fees (Stripe/Chargily charges to the platform) and whether sender pays any: not found in code.
- Detailed ledger account postings per event (accounts: provider clearing, sender deposit, deal funds, traveler payable, platform commission, connect funds, payout in transit).
- Export column layouts and date limits.
- Chargily bank-transfer refund timing for the sender.
- Boost payment rules in depth (see pricing.md / boosts file).


==== NOTES: knowledge/overview-and-glossary.md ====

# Wassla overview and glossary

Covers: What Wassla is, who uses it, the core product rules, one delivery told end to end with the money, a glossary of every status word an admin meets, what is not in the app, and how the technical pieces fit.
Related files: pricing.md, money.md (payments, refunds, payouts), deliveries.md (deal lifecycle, handover codes), problems-and-disputes.md, trips-and-matching.md (journeys, offers), accounts-and-verification.md, admin-console.md (console pages, roles), settings-and-helper.md, app-walkthrough.md, messages-and-notices.md

## What is Wassla?

- Wassla (older code and docs say "ShipTrip") is a peer-to-peer parcel delivery marketplace.
- A sender posts a parcel (a "delivery request"). A traveler who is already making a trip (a flight, or a drive) offers to carry it. Wassla handles matching, price agreement, payment, a handover code, a protection period and the traveler's payout.
- The aim is fast, even same-day, delivery. "Same day" is a goal, not a rule: nothing in the app promises or enforces it. The sender sets a deadline and the app checks the traveler's arrival against it.
- V1 is delivery only. The market is EU to Algeria (and within each side). Platform entity is in France.

## Who are the actors?

| Actor | Who | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Sender | A user who posts a parcel and pays | Needs an account. Email verification exists, but the code does not require a verified email before posting or paying. |
| Traveler | A user who posts a journey and carries parcels | Gets paid out. Can be asked for KYC (ID check) and flight proof. |
| Recipient | The person who receives the parcel | Needs NO account. Reached by email. Gives the delivery code to the traveler. |
| Guest payer | Someone other than the sender who pays through a shared link | No account. The link works for 3 days (259,200 s, a Settings value). The page speaks the reader's language. |
| Staff | Admins using the admin console | Five roles, below. |

Staff roles (slugs in brackets):
- Ops [`ops`]: requests, journeys, matches, deals, lifecycle, no-shows, disputes (manage, not resolve), boosts and ratings (view), provider health. Ops cannot open the users list.
- Support [`support`]: read-only on users, requests, journeys, deals, disputes, ratings, audit log; can handle cancellations.
- Finance [`finance`]: payments, refunds, payouts, reconciliation, exports, payout profiles and holds; can resolve disputes.
- Trust / Verification [`trust_verification`]: KYC, flight proofs, evidence, safety review, no-shows, payout identity, payout holds, managing disputes (not resolving), rating moderation, sensitive user data.
- Super Admin [`super_admin`]: every permission, including managing admins and settings.

## Which countries, languages and currencies?

- Countries: the app offers four launch countries for pickup, delivery and journey stops: Algeria, France, Spain and Germany (DZ, FR, ES, DE; fixed in the app's place picker). Road (drive) networks are declared for Algeria and for France/Spain/Germany together. Signup asks for no country, so nobody is blocked at signup by country.
- Flights are allowed between any places; any leg between Algeria and a non-Algerian country must be a flight. Drives are only inside one road network (Algeria; or France/Spain/Germany together).
- Languages: English (default), French, Arabic. The Flutter app supports all three, including right-to-left Arabic. Each user has a preferred language used for emails and messages. If a stored value is bad, English is used.
- Currencies: EUR is canonical. All marketplace money is stored as whole cents in EUR. Chargily (Algerian card / Edahabia payments) shows a DZD amount that the server converts from EUR. Seeded rate: 150 DZD per 1 EUR (setting `eur_dzd_rate_micros` = 150,000,000; an operator must keep it current). Minimum Chargily payment 75 DZD. Stripe is used for EUR card payments and EUR payouts.
- Old DZD rows exist from before V1 ("legacy DZD" offers and deals). They are history; new business is EUR.

## What are the main product rules?

1. EUR is the canonical currency (cents). DZD is only a display conversion at Chargily.
2. The sender proposes first. A traveler cannot start a deal by naming a price on a parcel; the sender makes an offer on a match, the traveler can accept, counter or decline.
3. Boost cannot bypass compatibility. A boost is only extra reward; it never makes an incompatible parcel/journey pair match.
4. Exact locations stay hidden until the deal is funded. Before that, parties see only coarse places and distance bands.
5. The delivery code belongs to the recipient. The traveler cannot retrieve it. After pickup the code is created "buffered" and nobody can see it for 30 minutes (1,800 s, a Settings value, frozen on the deal at funding). Then it is released and the recipient is emailed.
6. Payout protection window is 48 hours (172,800 s, Settings: Payments > Payout) after delivery is confirmed.
7. A dispute freezes the payout while it is `open`, `awaiting_evidence` or `under_review`.
8. Backend state is the truth. The app never shows payment, verification, delivery or payout success on its own.
9. Sea legs exist in code but are switched off by default (`sea_legs_enabled` = false).

## How does one delivery go, with the money?

A worked example. Sender in Paris wants a parcel carried to Algiers. A traveler flies Paris to Algiers.

1. Traveler publishes a journey (legs, dates, optional price per kg). Publishing needs an approved identity check (KYC) and an approved ticket for every flight and ferry leg, so staff review those first (Verification pages). A published journey goes straight from `draft` to `active`.
2. Sender posts a delivery request. In the default mode a posting deposit is required first: the request stays `awaiting_deposit` and is not visible until the deposit is paid. The recommended deposit is 10% of the recommended sender total, clamped between 3.00 EUR and 7.00 EUR. The sender may instead choose any amount from 3.00 EUR up to the full amount owed (seeded Settings: Payments > Posting deposit).
3. Match and offer. Sender offers a reward, say 20.00 EUR. Wassla adds its commission on top: 25% (2,500 bps) rounded up to a cent = 5.00 EUR. Sender total = 25.00 EUR. The traveler's reward stays 20.00 EUR. Offers wait for an answer for 6 hours by default (or until pickup time if sooner).
4. Traveler accepts. A deal is created directly in `payment_required`. Terms are frozen (reward, fee, boost, rates). The traveler's space is held for 1 hour by default (3,600 s, Settings: reservation payment grace); if the deal is not paid by then the space is released.
5. Sender (or a guest payer) pays the deal balance by Stripe (EUR) or Chargily (DZD shown, converted from EUR). The posting deposit already paid is credited. When money is confirmed by the provider the deal becomes `funded`. Exact locations unlock.
6. Recipient details are set; deal becomes `pickup_ready`. Pickup cannot be confirmed before that. Sender gives the parcel to the traveler, who confirms pickup with the pickup code: `picked_up`, then at once `in_transit`. The delivery code is created `buffered`.
7. Travel: `in_transit`. The traveler can report leg progress (at the port, departed, arrived).
8. After the 30-minute buffer the delivery code is released; deal is `delivery_ready`. Recipient gives the code, traveler enters it: `delivery_confirmed`, then `protection_window`.
9. Either party can open a dispute from pickup until the 48-hour protection ends (staff cannot open one). If none is open at the end, the deal is `completed` and the payout becomes eligible. Traveler gets 20.00 EUR (plus any boost); Wassla keeps the 5.00 EUR fee.
