10. Payout goes to the traveler by Stripe transfer (EUR) or a manual settlement (e.g. DZD by CCP/RIP) that finance records. Automatic Stripe payout is off by default (`auto_stripe_enabled` false).
- Cancel or dispute paths: refund to the sender (full, partial or none), see the disputes and cancellation files.
- Boost example: boost of 10.00 EUR, Boost commission 25% = 2.50 EUR (its own Settings value, seeded equal to the base 25%). Traveler gets the whole 10.00 EUR extra; sender pays 12.50 EUR extra.

## Glossary: deal statuses (`Deal.status`)

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `offer_accepted` | Exists in the status list, but a new deal is created straight in `payment_required`. |
| `payment_required` | Waiting for the sender (or guest) to pay the balance. |
| `funded` | Provider confirmed the money. Exact locations unlocked. |
| `pickup_ready` | Recipient details are on file; ready for pickup. |
| `picked_up` | Traveler confirmed pickup with the pickup code. |
| `in_transit` | Parcel is travelling. |
| `delivery_ready` | Delivery code is released; waiting for the handover. |
| `delivery_confirmed` | Recipient code accepted; parcel delivered. |
| `protection_window` | 48-hour hold after delivery; disputes allowed; payout not yet eligible. |
| `completed` | Closed normally; payout may be released. |
| `cancelled` | Deal cancelled. |
| `expired` | Not paid or not progressed in time. |
| `payment_failed` | In the status list but never set by any code: a failed payment leaves the deal in `payment_required` so the sender can retry. |
| `disputed` | A dispute is active; money frozen. |
| `refunded` / `partially_refunded` | Money went back to the sender in full or in part. |

Other statuses:
- Delivery request: `awaiting_deposit`, `open`, `matched`, `in_transit`, `delivered`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`.
- Match: `pending`, `accepted`, `in_transit`, `delivered`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`.
- Offer: `pending`, `countered`, `accepted`, `declined`, `withdrawn`, `expired`. Proposed by `sender` or `traveler`.
- Deal leg allocation (one slice of a deal per leg): `pending_payment`, `funded`, `in_transit`, `released`, `completed`, `cancelled`.
- Early arrival report on a deal: `pending_confirmation` (sender must answer), `confirmed`, `declined`. It never moves money or the delivery code.
- Boost today is a plain extra reward amount on the request (smallest non-zero Boost 1.00 EUR, Settings: boost). It has no status, timer or expiry of its own; it is frozen into the deal when an offer is accepted.
- There is no paid boost and no visibility package to buy.

## Glossary: journeys and legs

- Journey: the traveler's whole trip, made of ordered legs (at most 20). Statuses: `draft`, `pending_verification` (in the list, but current code never moves a journey into it), `active` (live), `in_progress`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`.
- Leg: one hop of a journey. Mode: `FLIGHT`, `DRIVE` or `SEA` (sea is off by default; refusal wording "sea legs are not available at the moment").
- Leg progress report: `at_port`, `departed`, `arrived`, given by the traveler or staff. It moves no money and no deal status; it is copied to the deal timeline.
- Proof (flight proof): evidence a leg is real. Status `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`; resubmitting keeps history. Reviewed in the console at Verification > flight proofs.
- Schedule change: the traveler changes times. Statuses `pending_senders`, `applied`, `withdrawn`. Each sender answers `pending`, `accepted`, `declined`. Needed when the new arrival misses the deadline (`deadline`), moves the arrival by more than 360 minutes (`shift`), or moves the pickup (`pickup`). 360 min is a Settings value (marketplace > route change).
- Legacy trip (`Trip`): older single-trip model with `draft`, `active`, `in_transit`, `delivered`, `completed`, `cancelled`. Journeys replace it.
- Leg reminders: traveler nudged 30 min after planned departure, 60 min after planned arrival, repeat every 180 min, max 2 repeats (Settings: marketplace > reminders). Only for legs carrying a funded parcel.

## Glossary: money terms

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Payment order | One amount to collect. Purpose: `posting_deposit`, `deal_balance` (`boost` exists but is not used). Status: `pending`, `partially_paid`, `paid`, `refund_pending`, `partially_refunded`, `refunded`, `cancelled`. |
| Payment attempt | One try to pay an order at a provider. `created`, `checkout_pending`, `processing`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `expired`, `cancelled`. Checkout attempt lives 3600 s (Settings: Payments > Checkout). |
| Provider | Stripe (EUR cards), Chargily (Algerian cards / Edahabia, DZD). A mock provider exists for tests only and is forbidden in production. |
| Refund | Money back. Status `pending`, `processing`, `succeeded`, `failed`. Reasons: request expired unmatched, sender cancelled before acceptance, payment could not be applied, deal cancelled before funding, administrative, deal cancelled after funding, dispute resolution, verified no-show. |
| Ledger | Append-only money journal. Entry kinds: customer payment, deposit credit, deal funding, boost allocation, refund, payout, correction. |
| Payout | What the traveler receives. Statuses below. |
| Payout method | How a traveler is paid: Stripe connected account or manual settlement. Status `setup_required`, `pending_review`, `ready`, `needs_attention`, `unavailable`, `disabled`. |
| Hold | Money locked for a deal (sender's funds in escrow until release or refund). Old wallet model: `open`, `released`, `reversed`. Also "payout hold" = finance blocks a payout manually. |
| Protection window | The 48 h after delivery confirmation before the payout can be released. |
| Reconciliation | Checking Wassla's records match what Stripe/Chargily say, and fixing differences. Runs as background jobs (provider reconcile, refund reconcile, payout reconcile). |
| Commission | Wassla fee charged on top of the reward (25% seeded, rounded up to a cent). |
| Posting deposit | Upfront payment when a request is posted; credited to the deal later; refunded if the request expires unmatched. |
| Cancellation compensation | If the sender cancels within 24 h of pickup (86,400 s), traveler may get 10% (1000 bps) capped at 15.00 EUR (seeded Settings: cancellation). |

Payout statuses (`Payout.status`):
- `not_eligible`: protection window still open.
- `frozen`: an active dispute holds the money.
- `blocked`: blocked by finance or a failed check.
- `eligible`: window passed, no dispute, books reconcile.
- `scheduled`, `processing`: queued or in flight.
- `sent`: the transfer was made but is not yet settled. For a manual (DZD) payout, finance recorded the transfer with a receipt but did not yet attest it as completed. The traveler's app also shows "On the way" while a Stripe bank payout is in transit.
- `paid`: settled. For a manual payout, finance attested the transfer as completed and the payout was written to the ledger. A genuine bank return can still move `paid` to `failed`.
- `failed`, `cancelled`.
Payout method kinds: `stripe_transfer`, `manual`, `undecided`.
Stripe disbursement (the bank leg of a Stripe payout): `planned`, `committed`, `unknown`, `pending`, `in_transit`, `paid`, `failed`, `canceled`, `returned` (returned after paid).

## Glossary: disputes, problems, handover

- Dispute statuses: `open`, `awaiting_evidence`, `under_review`, `resolved`, `closed`. The first three freeze the payout.
- Dispute categories: `not_delivered`, `damaged`, `wrong_item`, `late`, `no_show`, `payment`, `other`.
- Dispute opened by: `sender`, `traveler` or `admin`.
- Dispute resolutions: `full_sender_refund`, `full_traveler_payout`, `partial_split` (fee mode "proportional").
- Evidence kinds: `text`, `photo`, `video`. Up to 20 items and 25 MB each (26,214,400 bytes) by default (Settings: disputes).
- Problem (shipment problem): a lighter report on a deal. Kinds: `sender_unavailable`, `traveler_no_show`, `recipient_unavailable`, `parcel_mismatch`, `parcel_damaged`, `trip_disrupted`, `customs_delay`, `other`. Status: `open`, `escalated` (in front of staff), `acknowledged`, `closed`. Damaged, mismatch and both no-show kinds go to staff at once. A no-show can only be reported on a funded deal from the agreed pickup time until pickup is confirmed, and only once staff have not already recorded one.
- No-show: one party did not turn up. Staff record who (`sender` or `traveler`); there is no automatic risk scoring. A verified traveler no-show refunds the sender in full by default (staff can override).
- Handover code: pickup code (sender to traveler) and delivery code (recipient to traveler). Length 8 (Crockford base32), 5 wrong tries lock it for 900 s, up to 3 lockouts, then it locks; also at most 12 tries per hour (Settings: handover). Only the sender can ask for a new code. Statuses: `buffered`, `active`, `used`, `locked`, `superseded` (replaced by a rotation), `cancelled`. Attempt results: `succeeded`, `mismatch`, `not_available`, `locked`, `rate_limited`, `not_authorized`, `wrong_state`.
- Ratings: reviews can be left for 14 days (1,209,600 s), comment up to 1000 chars; ratings are revealed by a background job.

## Glossary: other terms

- Tracking number: a parcel id like `WSL-7K3M9QXD` ("WSL-" plus 8 characters; no 0, 1, I, L, O, U). Random, identifies a parcel, never grants access. Users may type it in any case, with or without "WSL-".
- Boost: the sender's extra reward on a request to attract a traveler. Traveler receives all of it; commission 25% is added on top. It never overrides compatibility.
- Compatibility: whether a parcel fits a journey (route, dates, weight, deadline). Stored as an immutable snapshot on the match.
- Listed price: the pre-filled reward = the largest of traveler price per kg x chargeable weight (rounded up), the traveler's minimum per parcel, and Wassla's minimum. It is a default, not a floor.
- Recommended / minimum / chosen reward: Wassla suggests; the sender may go above or below the suggestion but never below the minimum.
- Business settings revision: the versioned Settings the admin console edits. Each deal freezes the values it uses, so later changes do not affect it.
- Scheduled job (obligation): a durable background task (expiries, reminders, payout release, notifications). Statuses `pending`, `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`.
- KYC: ID check for travelers. Document types `id_card`, `passport`, `driving_license`. Statuses `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`, `expired`.
- AI helper: the admin assistant. Calls are logged and pruned; prepared changes expire.

## What is NOT in the app?

- Professional transporters and courier handoff: not in the app.
- Kaba / ProductRequest (traveler buys a product): retired in V1. The old model still sits in code but is not an active feature.
- Sea legs: coded but off by default.
- Old wallet, payments and verification modules (DZD wallet, mock payment intents, match-based handover codes) are not routed in the API; the active money system is the finance module.
- Dynamic surge pricing: none. The suggested price has only a fixed urgency add-on for tight deadlines (see the pricing file).
- Vehicle or transport proof for drive legs: none. Only flight and ferry legs need a ticket proof.
- Staff screen to read chat messages: none.

## How do the technical pieces fit?

- Repository layout: `backend/monolith` (Django), `backend/services` (Go), `mobile` (Flutter app), `web` (static landing site), `docs`, `infra`.
- Django monolith: the brain. Holds all rules, money, deals, admin console and the AI helper API. Source of truth is PostgreSQL.
- Go services: three small helpers (notifications, chat, KYC), plus an email worker. They listen to events Django publishes through Redis; if they are down, Django still works.
- Flutter app: iOS and Android, three languages. Main tabs: Home, Deliveries, Chat, Profile. Notifications live in the header bell. Travelers see My Trips and Carrying inside Deliveries.
- Landing site: static pages (home, terms, privacy, prohibited items, support) in en, fr, ar.
- Admin console: Django pages (overview, AI helper, users, verification (KYC, flight proofs), trust > reviews, requests, journeys, deals, disputes, problem reports, payments, refunds, payouts, ledger, finance dashboard, payouts hub, manual DZD, exceptions, exports, staff, settings, system (jobs, email, geography), audit log, two-step login).

## Not covered here

- Cancellation rules per deal state (see the deals and problems-and-disputes files).
- The full pricing formula for the Wassla minimum and distance bands (see the pricing file).


==== NOTES: knowledge/pricing.md ====

# Pricing and fees

Covers: how a delivery's price is built end to end (traveler reward, Wassla minimum, traveler's listed price, Wassla fee, boost, deposit, EUR to DZD display) and which of these settings an admin can change.
Related files: money.md, deliveries.md, trips-and-matching.md, settings-and-helper.md, problems-and-disputes.md

All money is stored in EUR cents. The numbers below are the defaults of the seeded settings; an admin can change some of them (see the last sections). Every offer and deal copies the numbers it used, so a later settings change never changes a price already agreed.

## What are the main price terms?
- Traveler reward: the amount the traveler earns for carrying the parcel. The sender proposes it first.
- Wassla fee (commission): added ON TOP of the reward. It is not taken out of the traveler's reward. The app says: "The traveler receives this full amount. The Wassla fee is added on top, not taken out of it."
- Sender total: reward + Wassla fee. This is what the sender pays.
- Wassla minimum: the lowest reward allowed for a given parcel and route. Enforced by the server.
- Recommended reward: a suggestion, never enforced.
- Listed price: the traveler's own price on a route (optional), shown pre-filled to the sender.
- Boost: extra reward the sender adds, goes whole to the traveler, with its own fee on top.

## How is the Wassla fee calculated?
- Fee = reward x 25% (2,500 basis points), rounded UP to the next cent. Sender total = reward + fee.
- The 25% is the "commission" setting (Settings > "Commission, deposit and floors" > "Wassla commission (%)"). Changing it affects future offers only.
- Example: reward 13.33 EUR (1333 cents): fee = 333.25 -> 334 cents. Sender pays 16.67 EUR.
- Example: reward 30.00 EUR: fee 7.50, sender pays 37.50.

## How is the Wassla minimum (lowest allowed reward) calculated?
Server-side, per parcel and per matched route:
1. Chargeable weight = the larger of actual weight and volumetric weight. Volumetric weight = length x width x height (cm) / 5,000. If no dimensions were entered, volumetric weight is 0. Then rounded UP to the next 0.5 kg step.
2. Distance base price by the matched distance:
   | Distance up to | Base |
   |---|---|
   | 100 km | 4.00 EUR |
   | 300 km | 6.00 EUR |
   | 750 km | 9.00 EUR |
   | 1,500 km | 12.00 EUR |
   | 3,000 km | 16.00 EUR |
   | 5,000 km | 20.00 EUR |
   | above 5,000 km | 24.00 EUR |
3. Weight part = chargeable kg x 2.50 EUR per kg (rounded up to the cent).
4. Calculated = base + weight part. Minimum = the larger of that and the global floor of 7.00 EUR.
- The detour and urgency amounts below are NOT part of the minimum; they only raise the recommendation.
- Worked example: 3 kg parcel, 30x20x15 cm (volumetric 1.8 kg, so 3.0 kg chargeable), 200 km: base 6.00 + 3 x 2.50 = 13.50 EUR. Minimum = 13.50 EUR.
- Worked example: 1 kg parcel, 50 km: 4.00 + 2.50 = 6.50, below the 7.00 floor, so minimum = 7.00 EUR.
- Worked example: 5 kg parcel measuring 60x40x40 cm: volumetric 19.2 kg, so chargeable 19.5 kg.
- Limits on a parcel: weight 0.01 to 100 kg; each side 0.01 to 500 cm; dimensions are all-or-none (length, width, height together or none).

## How is the recommended reward calculated?
- Recommended = minimum x 120% (rounded up to the cent) + detour amount + urgency amount, then rounded UP to the next 0.50 EUR, and never below the minimum.
- Detour amount: 0.25 EUR per km of extra distance the traveler must travel (rounded up). 0 at posting time.
- Urgency amount: depends on slack between the traveler's estimated arrival and the sender's deadline: 3.00 EUR if 1,440 minutes (24 h) or less; 1.50 EUR if 4,320 minutes (3 days) or less; otherwise 0.
- Worked example (continuing the 13.50 EUR minimum): 13.50 x 1.2 = 16.20 -> rounded up to 16.50 EUR recommended. Fee 25% = 4.13 (rounded up from 4.125). Sender total 20.63 EUR.

## What price does the sender see when posting a request, and what is enforced?
- Before posting, the app asks the server for a quote: minimum, recommended and the chosen reward, each with "Traveler receives", "Wassla fee" and "Total sender cost". The app does no arithmetic itself.
- At posting time no journey exists yet. The estimate uses the straight-line distance between the pickup and delivery places and no detour. So the posting minimum is a lower bound; a real matched route can price higher.
- Urgency at posting time is measured from the end of the sender's ready window to the deadline. So a request whose deadline is 24 hours or less after its ready window ends gets the 3.00 EUR urgency amount in its recommendation (1.50 EUR for 3 days or less). It never changes the minimum.
- If a place has no coordinates the distance counts as 0, so the minimum falls to the lowest distance band (or the 7.00 EUR floor).
- The reward field is pre-filled with the recommended reward if empty. The sender can go above or below the recommendation.
- Refusal: a reward below the minimum is refused with code `price_below_minimum` ("The reward is below the minimum for this request."). The app shows "Offer must be at least {amount}".
- The reward the sender posts is only their intention (field `sender_proposed_reward_eur_cents`). The agreed reward is the one on the accepted offer.
- If the pricing settings cannot be read, posting prices fail with a 503 (service unavailable).

## What is the traveler's listed price (price per kg and minimum per parcel)?
- A traveler may set, on a route (journey), a price per kg and a minimum per parcel. Both or neither ("Set both a price per kg and a minimum per parcel, or neither."). It is optional; default is no price ("No price set. Senders propose their own amount.").
- The listed price for a given parcel = the largest of: (price per kg x chargeable weight, rounded up to the cent), the traveler's minimum per parcel, and the Wassla minimum for that match. The Wassla fee is then added on top, so the sender sees price plus fee.
- The listed result carries a "basis" saying which term won: `per_kg`, `traveler_minimum` or `wassla_minimum` (ties go to the traveler's terms first).
- Worked example: traveler lists 8.00 EUR/kg, minimum 15.00 EUR. Parcel 3 kg: 24.00 EUR (basis per_kg). Fee 6.00, sender total 30.00. A 1 kg parcel: 8.00 < 15.00, so 15.00 EUR (basis traveler_minimum), unless Wassla minimum is higher.
- It is a default, not a floor and not a ceiling. The sender sees it pre-filled ("Their price for this parcel", "Offer {amount}") and can tap "Offer a different amount". Any amount at or above the Wassla minimum is accepted. Below the listed price the app notes: "That is below their price, so they may decline or counter."
- Find Travelers can sort by "Lowest price" (listed price; unpriced journeys keep the normal order).
- The price can be changed while the journey is draft, pending verification, active or in progress. Otherwise: code `journey_price_not_editable`. A change applies only to offers made afterwards; existing offers and deals keep their amounts. Each change is logged.
- Limits (admin setting, Marketplace features): largest price per kg 100.00 EUR (min 1.00, max 1,000.00 on the form); largest minimum per parcel 500.00 EUR (max 10,000.00). Refusal code `journey_price_invalid` with reason `price_per_kg_above_maximum`, `min_price_above_maximum` or `price_incomplete` ("The price per kg is above the largest Wassla allows.").
- If the feature is switched off, a new price is refused with `traveler_pricing_disabled` ("Setting a price on a route is not available right now."), no listed price is shown, and clearing or re-sending an existing price still works.
- Only the journey owner can set it.

## How does an offer, a counter-offer and acceptance affect price?
- The sender proposes first. A counter-offer may come from the other party (the one who did not propose the current offer). Each offer freezes reward, fee and sender total.
- The server re-prices the real matched sub-route and refuses a reward under the minimum: code `reward_below_minimum` ("Traveler reward must be at least {minimum} EUR cents for this match.").
- A pending offer shows the totals including the sender's current boost ("provisional"). If the sender changes the boost while an offer is open, the traveler is notified and acceptance refuses stale numbers with `offer_economics_changed` ("This offer's amounts changed. Review them before accepting."). The acceptor must confirm the total they were shown.
- Offers have a response deadline (setting, default on): 6 hours to answer, never after the pickup time; one reminder 1 hour before. Unanswered, it expires with nothing reserved or charged.
- At acceptance the numbers freeze into the deal. After that nobody can change them.

## What is a boost, and how is it priced?
- A boost is extra reward the sender adds on a request. The traveler receives 100% of it. Wassla's fee on the boost is charged on top to the sender, at its own rate (default 25%, separate from the delivery commission), rounded up to the cent.
- Smallest boost 1.00 EUR, largest 1,000,000.00 EUR (100,000,000 cents). 0 means no boost. Refusals: `boost_amount_below_minimum`, `boost_amount_above_maximum` ("That boost is below the minimum." / "above the maximum.").
- Worked example: reward 24.00 EUR, boost 5.00 EUR. Boost fee = 1.25. Traveler gets 24.00 + 5.00 = 29.00. Sender pays 30.00 (reward + fee) + 5.00 + 1.25 = 36.25.
- Boost is not a timer or package. Its ranking effect lasts until the request's deadline, while the request is open for matching. There is no paid boost and no visibility package to buy.
- Editable only while the request is awaiting deposit or open, by the sender, before its deadline. After an offer is accepted: "This request's boost is no longer editable." (`boost_request_not_active`); after deadline `boost_request_expired`. If boosts are switched off: `boost_disabled` ("Boosts are not available at the moment.").
- Lowering a boost is refused if the posting deposit already paid would exceed the new total: `boost_below_prepaid_deposit`.
- In the app, a boost is added after publishing (presets +5 EUR, +10 EUR, custom). The server also accepts one at posting.
- Boost history (set, raised, lowered, removed, frozen, consumed, released) is recorded.

## How does a boost change ranking, and why can it not bypass compatibility?
- Compatibility (route, capacity, timing, verification, safety) is decided first. Ranking runs only on candidates that already passed; it refuses to rank an incompatible one. A boost cannot make an incompatible request compatible. The app says: "Only travelers who already match your parcel ever see it."
- Ranking weight = boost divided by 5.00 EUR, rounded up, minimum 1, maximum 30. Each weight point adds 25 score points, capped at 200 points total (reached at weight 8, i.e. any boost above 35.00 EUR; 35.00 EUR exactly is weight 7 = 175 points). The base score is about 0 to 1000.
- Boost never touches KYC, capacity, timing or safety.

## How do the total the sender pays and the traveler receives work with a boost?
- Traveler receives = reward + boost. (field `traveler_total_minor`)
- Wassla receives = reward fee + boost fee. (`platform_total_minor`)
- Sender pays = reward + reward fee + boost + boost fee. (`sender_total_with_boost_minor`) This is the amount of the deal's payment.
- Cancellation compensation uses only the base reward, not the boost.

## How does the posting deposit work?
- Default mode: sender pays a deposit up front to publish a request (status "Deposit needed" until paid). The setting `timing_mode` can be switched to pay-after-acceptance (then "No deposit needed").
- Recommended deposit = 10% of the recommended sender total (half-up rounding), kept between 3.00 and 7.00 EUR. It is based on the recommendation, not the sender's chosen reward and not the boost.
- The sender may choose their own amount: minimum 3.00 EUR, maximum = the whole sender total they will owe (chosen reward, fee, boost, boost fee). Refusals `deposit_below_minimum` ("That deposit is below the minimum."), `deposit_above_obligation` ("A deposit cannot exceed the amount it is paid against."). App presets: Minimum, Recommended, Pay in full, Custom.
- It is not an extra fee: when a traveler accepts, it is credited towards the sender total (credit = the smaller of deposit paid and amount owed). Remaining = sender total minus deposit.
- Worked example: recommended sender total 20.63 EUR -> 10% = 2.06 -> clamped up to 3.00 EUR.
- A deposit can be changed only while its order is untouched (pending, nothing paid, no open checkout): else "This deposit can no longer be changed." or `deposit_checkout_in_progress`.
- If the sender cancels the request, the deposit comes back ("Any deposit you paid comes back to you").
- If reward or boost is raised later after paying in full, extra balance can be due.
- Guest payer: someone else can pay an amount through a share link that only allows paying that amount (no access to the delivery). Link lifetime: 3 days (259,200 s, setting). Checkout attempt lifetime 1 hour (3,600 s, setting).

## How does the DZD (Chargily) amount work?
- EUR is always the real amount. Stripe charges in EUR. Chargily charges in Algerian dinars (DZD) using a server-calculated conversion; the app shows "Rate: EUR 1 = {rate} DA" and "You'll be charged". The app never calculates it.
- DZD = EUR cents x rate / 100, rounded UP to a whole dinar (no fractions). The rate is a setting with 6 decimals. Example at a rate of 150 DZD per EUR: 30.00 EUR = 4,500 DZD; 30.01 EUR = 4,501.5 -> 4,502 DZD.
- The rate comes only from Wassla settings (admin types it, with a source or reason); there is no automatic rate feed. The seeded 150 DZD is a placeholder an operator must set before real use. Maximum accepted 100,000 DZD per EUR.
- A new rate applies only to Chargily attempts started afterwards. Each attempt keeps its own frozen rate and DZD amount; refunds use that frozen rate (full refund returns the exact dinars charged).
- Minimum Chargily charge 75 DZD; below that: "The converted amount is below the provider's minimum charge." (`amount_below_provider_minimum`).
- Default state: the shipped seed settings have Stripe, Chargily and new Chargily checkouts switched OFF until an admin enables them. The live values are on Settings > "Provider availability" (admins can change them: "Accept new Stripe payments", "Allow Chargily", "Accept new Chargily checkouts").
- DZD payouts to travelers also use a rate frozen at payout creation (rounded up whole dinars).

## What does each person see, and where?
- Sender, posting: minimum, recommended, chosen reward, "Traveler receives", "Wassla fee", "Total sender cost", deposit guidance, boost breakdown (boost, boost fee, "You pay for the Boost").
- Sender, proposing to a traveler: listed price, "Use their price", "Use suggested" (recommended), total with boost.
- Traveler, on an offer: "You receive" / "Total you receive"; includes the sender's boost ("Includes the sender's boost of {amount}. The total is locked in when you accept").
- Deal money: "You pay", "Wassla fee", "Deposit already paid", "Remaining to pay", "Refund to you", "Traveler compensation".
- Staff: the console deal and export pages show traveler total, platform total, and sender total with boost.

## Which price settings can an admin change, and where?
All in the admin console, Settings page. Each save creates a new audited settings version (needs a reason), affecting future offers/commitments only.
| Setting | Default | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Wassla commission | 25% | "Commission, deposit and floors" |
| Posting deposit % | 10% | same |
| Min / max posting deposit (recommendation band) | 3.00 / 7.00 EUR | same |
| Minimum Traveler reward (global floor) | 7.00 EUR | same |
| Weight rate per kg | 2.50 EUR | same |
| Recommendation multiplier | 120% (form allows 100 to 500) | same |
| Boost commission | 25% | "Boost commission" card (field "Wassla commission on boosts (%)", 0 to 100) |
| Chargily exchange rate | 150 DZD per EUR (placeholder) | "Chargily exchange rate" card (field "DZD for €1", plus "Rate source or reason") |
| Provider switches (Stripe, Chargily, new Chargily checkouts) | off in pre-launch seed | "Provider availability" |
| Let travelers set a price | on | "Discovery, routes, prices and proposals" (eyebrow "Marketplace features") |
| Largest price per kg / minimum per parcel | 100.00 / 500.00 EUR | same |
| Hours to answer a proposal / reminder before | 6 h / 1 h | same |
| Allow sea legs | off | same |
Stored in the settings but with no console form (some are shown read-only in the "Every guardrail in operator units" table): distance bands, urgency amounts, volumetric divisor (5,000), weight step (0.5 kg), rounding step (0.50 EUR), detour rate (0.25 EUR/km), smallest boost (1.00 EUR), largest boost (1,000,000.00 EUR), boost ranking step (5.00 EUR) and cap (30), boost points (25 per weight, 200 max), smallest sender-chosen deposit (3.00 EUR), Chargily minimum (75 DZD), payment timing mode. The only way to change these is a new settings version posted in full through the staff settings API (needs `manage_settings`, a written reason, and is audited). The Django admin page for settings versions is read-only.
- Distance bands cannot be made finer than the published privacy distance bands (activation is refused: `pricing_bands_reduce_location_privacy`).
- Fixed in code (not settings): ceiling rounding of fees, EUR as the canonical currency, deposit credit rule, boost goes whole to the traveler, "sender proposes first".
- If the active settings cannot be read, pricing fails closed (503), never guessing.

## Related price rules in other areas (summary)
- Late sender cancellation (inside 24 h of pickup): traveler compensation = 10% of the base reward (rounded up), at most 15.00 EUR; the rest is refunded. Platform takes nothing from it.
- Payout protection 48 hours after delivery; a dispute freezes payout. The traveler's payout equals reward + boost.

## Not covered here
- The current live (production) values of every setting; only seeded defaults were confirmed.
- Exact refund and dispute split amounts (see cancellations and disputes files).
- How VAT, Stripe or Chargily provider fees, or customs are handled: not found in the pricing code.
- Whether any declared-value cap affects price: declared value is required and must be marked accurate, but no price effect was found.


==== NOTES: knowledge/problems-and-disputes.md ====

# Problems, no-shows, disputes, route changes and cancellations

Covers: what happens when a delivery goes wrong: problem reports, "did not come" reports and the staff no-show decision, disputes and their money settlement, traveler time changes, and cancellation consequences.
Related files: money.md (payout protection, how payouts are released, refunds), pricing.md (commission, reward, boost), deliveries.md (statuses, pickup and delivery codes), trips-and-matching.md (journeys, schedule changes), admin-console.md (roles, capabilities), settings-and-helper.md (settings page, how the helper prepares actions)

## What is the difference between a problem report, a no-show report and a dispute?
- A problem report is a statement. It is recorded, the other person is told, and the serious kinds go to staff. It never changes the delivery and never moves money.
- A "did not come" report is a problem report of a special kind, for a missed pickup. Only staff can turn it into a decision (the no-show action).
- A dispute is the only thing that can stop a payout and the only thing that can move money after pickup. Only staff decide it.
- Before pickup there is no dispute: the answer is cancelling or a no-show decision. After pickup there is no cancelling: the answer is a dispute.

## Which problem kinds exist, and which go straight to staff?
| Kind (label in app) | Goes to staff at once? |
|---|---|
| `sender_unavailable` (The sender did not come) | Yes |
| `traveler_no_show` (The traveler did not come) | Yes |
| `parcel_damaged` (The parcel is damaged) | Yes |
| `parcel_mismatch` (The parcel is not what was described) | Yes |
| `recipient_unavailable` (The recipient is not available) | No |
| `trip_disrupted` (The trip is cancelled or delayed) | No |
| `customs_delay` (Held up at customs) | No |
| `other` (Something else) | No |
- "Goes to staff" means status `escalated` from the start. The others start as `open`: the other person is told, but staff are not asked to act. Staff can still open, acknowledge and close an `open` report from the Problems page.

## Who can report a problem, and when?
- Only the sender or the traveler of that delivery. Anyone else gets "No such delivery." (404, `not_a_party`).
- General kinds: while the delivery is `funded`, `pickup_ready`, `picked_up`, `in_transit` or `delivery_ready`. Not before funding, not after delivery.
- Refusal wording: "Problems can be reported from funding until delivery. After delivery, open a dispute instead." (`problem_report_not_allowed`).
- Note: optional, up to 1,000 characters (`problem_note_too_long`: "A note can be 1000 characters at most."). Both people and staff read the note.
- One live report per person, per delivery, per kind. Sending the same one again returns the existing report instead of making a second one.
- The other person gets a live notification ("A problem was reported on a delivery").
- App: the delivery screen shows a "Problems reported" list and a "Report a problem" button. The sheet says: "The other person is told. Wassla staff look at damage and mismatches right away. Reporting does not cancel or refund anything."

## When can someone report "the other person did not come"?
- Only the person left waiting: the traveler can report `sender_unavailable`, the sender can report `traveler_no_show`. Anyone else gets 403 "Only the person who was left waiting can report this." (`problem_kind_not_allowed_for_role`).
- The window opens at the agreed pickup time and stays open until pickup is confirmed. The delivery must be funded and in `funded` or `pickup_ready`.
- If not allowed now the answer is "This cannot be reported right now." (`no_show_report_not_allowed`) with one reason:
  - `pickup_already_confirmed`: pickup was confirmed.
  - `deal_not_funded`: not paid yet.
  - `deal_not_active`: not in funded or pickup_ready.
  - `no_show_already_recorded`: staff already decided.
  - `pickup_time_unknown`: no agreed pickup time.
  - `pickup_time_not_reached`: too early; the app also gets the time it opens.
  - `already_reported`: this person already has a live report (sending it again just returns that report).
- App wording when refused: "This can't be reported right now. The pickup time may not have come yet, or the parcel may already be collected."
- The app never works out the window itself: the server says whether the button shows.
- The other person sees a banner: "The traveler says you did not come" / "The sender says you did not come", with a "Reply in chat" button, and "Nothing is cancelled or refunded yet."

## What do the problem statuses mean?
| Status | Meaning | What users see |
|---|---|---|
| `open` | Recorded, other person told, not in front of staff | "Sent" |
| `escalated` | In front of staff, nobody has looked yet | "With Wassla staff" |
| `acknowledged` | A staff member looked and is on it | "Being looked at" |
| `closed` | Finished | "Closed" |
- Staff reasons (for acknowledging or closing) are internal and are never shown to members. Members see only the new state.
- Acknowledging and closing send only a silent screen refresh, no push.

## How does staff handle a problem report? (console)
- Console page: Disputes, then "Problems" (path `disputes/problems/`). Detail page shows the report, the delivery, and the audit trail.
- Seeing the queue needs `view_disputes`. Acknowledging or closing needs `manage_disputes` (staff roles with it: ops, trust, super admin; support and finance only view).
- The queue shows escalated first, then open, acknowledged, closed; oldest first inside each. The "attention" filter shows only `escalated`. It can be searched by tracking number or participant email.
- Acknowledge: a reason is required (up to 500 characters; the form refuses longer). Allowed on `open` or `escalated`. Acknowledging an already acknowledged report changes nothing. A closed report gives "This report is already closed."
- Close: a reason is required (up to 500 characters). Message: "The problem is closed. The delivery is unchanged." A closed report gives `problem_already_closed`.
- Both are audited and add an entry to the delivery timeline.
- A staff role without `manage_disputes` is refused: "Your staff role cannot handle problem reports."
- A no-show report on the detail page carries a "Record a no-show" link (needs `record_no_show`) with the party pre-selected.

## How does staff record a no-show, and what happens to the money?
- Console page: the delivery's "Record a no-show" page (`marketplace/deals/<id>/no-show/`). Capability: `record_no_show` (ops, trust, super admin).
- Only on a funded delivery whose pickup was never confirmed (`funded` or `pickup_ready`) with no no-show already recorded. Otherwise the page says it cannot take a decision.
- The form needs "Who did not come" (sender or traveler) and a required note (up to 255 characters), stored on the timeline and audit log.
- Traveler did not come: the sender is refunded in full (everything collected, including boost and any deposit credit), the delivery is cancelled, the reserved space is released, live handover codes are cancelled, the match and the request are cancelled. Delivery status becomes `refunded` (or `cancelled` if nothing was collected). The sender must create a new request and pay a new deposit to ship again. Message: "The no-show is recorded and the sender was refunded in full."
- Sender did not come: only the no-show is recorded. No refund, no cancellation. Message: "The no-show is recorded." The delivery keeps its status (`funded` or `pickup_ready`); nothing in the code then blocks a later pickup or a normal cancellation (with the usual early/late rules). Both kinds count on the person's console page as "No-shows recorded".
- Open no-show reports on that delivery (both kinds) are closed with the reason "No-show recorded by staff: <party>." and point at the audit entry.
- Recording the same no-show twice changes nothing. A no-show after pickup is refused: "A no-show cannot be recorded once pickup was confirmed."
- The staff API (`POST /api/admin/deals/<id>/no-show`) also accepts a `refund_sender` override; the console page has no such option.
- No automatic scoring decides who did not come. It is a manual staff decision.

## Who can open a dispute, and when?
- Only the sender or the traveler of the delivery (staff cannot open one from any console page or staff API).
- Window: from the moment the parcel is picked up (`picked_up`, `in_transit`, `delivery_ready`) until the protection deadline after delivery confirmation. The default protection window is 48 hours. It is a stored business setting, shown read-only as "Payout protection window" in the "Lifecycle guardrails" table of the console Business settings page; no console form changes it. Each delivery keeps the deadline it got at delivery confirmation.
- Before pickup: "This parcel has not been picked up yet. Cancel the delivery instead." (`dispute_not_available`).
- After the protection deadline: "The payment protection window for this delivery has closed." (`dispute_window_closed`). The app says: "Disputes can be opened for 48 hours after delivery. Contact support if you still need help."
- If a dispute was already resolved on that delivery, a party cannot open another: "This delivery has already had a dispute resolved. Contact support if something is still wrong." (`dispute_already_resolved`).
- Only one active dispute per delivery. Opening again returns the existing one.
- Needs a category and a description (up to 4,000 characters). Missing description: "Describe what went wrong so the dispute can be reviewed."
- Categories (app label): `not_delivered` (Never arrived), `damaged` (Arrived damaged), `wrong_item` (Wrong item), `late` (Arrived too late), `no_show` (The other person didn't show up), `payment` (Something is wrong with the money), `other` (Something else).
- What opening does at once: the traveler's payout is frozen, a snapshot of evidence references is captured (timeline, offers, terms, payments, handover facts, chat and journey references, no-show record; never the delivery code or recipient contact details), the delivery status becomes `disputed`, protection reminders are cancelled, and both people get an email and live notification.
- Staff cannot open a dispute: the code has no staff path (an uncalled one that skipped the checks was removed in L18A), so a dispute is always opened by the sender or traveler, before protection ends. Whether staff should be able to open one is an open decision for the owner.
- App text: "Opening a dispute puts the traveler's payout on hold while we look into it."

## What do the dispute statuses mean?
| Status | Meaning | Party sees | Payout |
|---|---|---|---|
| `open` | Just opened | "Open" | Frozen |
| `awaiting_evidence` | Staff wait for evidence | "Waiting for evidence" | Frozen |
| `under_review` | Staff are deciding | "Being reviewed" | Frozen |
| `resolved` | Decided, money moved | "Resolved" | Per decision |
| `closed` | Filed away, no money decision | "Closed" | Stays as left |
- The first three are "active": evidence is accepted and the payout stays frozen.
- Staff can move a dispute only between open, awaiting evidence and under review. `resolved` and `closed` cannot be reopened ("This dispute is already finished and cannot be reopened.", `dispute_already_finished`). Moving to the same status changes nothing.
- There is no way to close a dispute without resolving it (an uncalled close step was removed in L18A), so every dispute ends as `resolved`. The `closed` status still exists in the data model, but nothing sets it.

## What evidence can be added?
- Who: either party, and staff with the evidence capability. Only while the dispute is active ("This dispute is no longer accepting evidence.").
- Kinds: text statement (up to 4,000 characters), photo, video.
- Limits (stored business settings; the item count shows read-only as "Dispute evidence limit" in the console Business settings "Lifecycle guardrails" table, with no form to change it): 20 items, 25 MiB (26,214,400 bytes) per file, types JPEG, PNG, WebP, MP4, MOV (QuickTime). Files are also checked against their real content.
- Evidence cannot be edited or removed. Files open only through a short-lived link (5 minutes).
- Staff view of evidence files needs `view_evidence` and is audited.
- Refusals: "That file type is not accepted as dispute evidence.", "That file is larger than the evidence size limit.", "This dispute already holds the maximum number of evidence items."

## How does staff handle a dispute? (console and capabilities)
- Console: Disputes list (filter by status, "attention" shows active ones, search by reference, tracking number or email). The list shows money at stake: the collected total, or the sender total before a decision.
- Dispute detail page: review-state form, resolution form with Preview then Confirm, evidence, timeline, notes.
- `view_disputes`: see (ops, support, finance, trust, super admin).
- `manage_disputes`: change review state (ops, trust, super admin).
- `resolve_disputes`: decide and move money (finance, super admin). Ops and trust cannot resolve.
- Changing the review state has an optional note (up to 4,000 characters). Staff notes stay internal and are removed from what parties see.
- Staff API: `GET /api/admin/disputes`, `POST .../status`, `POST .../resolve`.

## What resolutions exist and how are the amounts worked out?
Three outcomes. All amounts are in EUR cents; every cent collected is refunded, paid to the traveler, or kept by Wassla, and the three always add up to the collected total.
- `full_sender_refund` ("Refunded to the sender"): sender gets everything back, traveler 0, Wassla 0.
- `full_traveler_payout` ("Paid to the traveler"): no refund. Traveler gets their reward and Wassla keeps its commission, split by the fee mode below.
- `partial_split` ("Split between both sides"): staff enter the sender refund (required, must be more than 0 and less than the total: "A partial split must return some, but not all, of the money."). The traveler payout is optional.
  - If the payout is given, Wassla keeps what is left. A payout bigger than what is left is refused.
  - If not given, the rest is shared by the fee mode.
- Fee mode `partial_split_fee_mode` (frozen on each delivery at funding): `proportional` (default) shares the rest in the ratio the traveler reward and commission were agreed; the traveler takes the rounded-down share, Wassla gets the leftover cent. `platform_waives`: the traveler is made whole first (up to their reward), Wassla absorbs the shortfall.
- Money already paid to the traveler (or committed to a transfer) cannot be taken back. The refund is limited to what the platform still holds ("The sender refund must be between zero and the amount the platform still holds.").
- Worked example (default 25 percent commission on top, no boost): traveler reward 20.00, commission 5.00, sender paid 25.00.
  - Full sender refund: 25.00 / 0 / 0.
  - Full traveler payout: 0 / 20.00 / 5.00.
  - Partial, refund 10.00, no payout entered: remaining 15.00, traveler 15 x 20 / 25 = 12.00, Wassla 3.00.
  - Partial, refund 10.00, payout 10.00: 10.00 / 10.00 / 5.00.
- Delivery status after: refund 0 gives `completed`; refund equal to everything collected gives `refunded`; otherwise `partially_refunded`.
- Live handover codes are cancelled. Both people get an email and live notification. The staff note is shown to parties as "Note from Wassla".

## What is the settlement planner preview?
- Before resolving, the console asks the same planner that does the real thing what the amounts would be. Nothing is written. It shows: collected from the sender, back to the sender, paid to the traveler, kept by Wassla.
- The real decision re-plans under a fresh lock, so the preview is a guide. The console requires an explicit confirmation ("Confirm the financial consequence before resolving.").
- Resolving twice does nothing the second time: the first decision stands.
- Staff fields are entered in euros (up to 2 decimals); they are converted to cents.

## What freezes the payout, and what happens to it afterwards?
- Opening a dispute freezes the payout (status `frozen`). A payout still in its 48-hour protection wait, or ready, is pulled back. Nothing is paid while the dispute is active.
- A payout already paid cannot be frozen; the dispute is marked "payout already settled". A transfer already accepted by the payment provider cannot be recalled either; the record notes the exposure for recovery review.
- Resolving sets the payout to the decided traveler amount. A traveler amount of 0 cancels it. If the protection window is still running, the payout waits as not yet eligible; otherwise it becomes eligible (or blocked if it has a block reason).
- The unused close step would not unfreeze anything (no screen calls it, see above).

## What is "resolve" versus "set status"?
- Set status (`dispute.set_status`) only moves the review step between open, awaiting evidence and under review. No money moves.
- Resolve (`dispute.resolve`) is the decision: moves money, finishes the dispute, cannot be undone.

## What happens when a traveler changes times on a live trip?
- Only times can change (not stops or places). Allowed on a trip that is `active` or `in_progress`.
- Rules: a leg already reported arrived cannot change; a leg reported departed keeps its departure; a new departure must be in the future; a change with no difference is refused ("These are the times already in force."); only one waiting change at a time ("New times are already waiting for senders. Withdraw them first.").
- Every parcel on a changed leg is "affected". It is "material" (the sender must answer) when:
  - the new arrival is after the request's deadline and later than agreed, or
  - the arrival moves more than the margin from the arrival the sender last agreed, or
  - (parcel not collected yet) the pickup departure moves more than the margin, or moves earlier than the agreed pickup time.
- The margin is `material_shift_minutes`, default 360 minutes (6 hours), allowed 30 minutes to 7 days. It is a stored marketplace setting (`route_change`) with no form on the console Business settings page.
- If no parcel is material, the new times apply at once and senders are told. Otherwise the change waits. It applies when every material sender has accepted (or their parcel was collected or is no longer active).
- A sender whose parcel is already collected is informed only, never asked. They can report a problem or open a dispute.
- The traveler can withdraw a waiting change. It is also dropped by itself ("no longer valid") if the trip moves on. A waiting change is re-checked every 30 minutes.
- The sender can accept or decline, and change their answer while it waits.
- The funded arrival, the agreed pickup time and the payout floor stay as frozen at funding.

## What if the sender declines the new times? (declined change = traveler cancellation)
- Declining cancels nothing by itself. The sender can then cancel through the normal cancel flow.
- While the declined change is still waiting, that cancellation counts as the traveler's: full refund, no compensation, recorded with the reason `declined_schedule_change`. If the traveler withdraws the change, the ordinary rules apply again.
- App: "Counted as the traveler's cancellation, because they changed the times."

## What are the cancellation consequences?
- Before payment (`payment_required`): free, nothing to refund. New deals start directly in `payment_required`; the current code never puts a deal in `offer_accepted`. A deal left in `offer_accepted` from old data is refused with "This delivery can no longer be cancelled."
- After funding and before pickup (`funded`, `pickup_ready`): the quote is computed by the server (`GET /api/deals/<id>/cancellation`) and the app only shows it.
  - Traveler cancels: sender refunded in full, no compensation.
  - Sender cancels early (more than 24 hours before the agreed pickup): full refund. The cutoff is a stored business setting, shown read-only as "Sender free-cancellation cutoff" in the Business settings "Lifecycle guardrails" table; default 86,400 seconds (24 hours). Exactly 24 hours before or later counts as late.
  - Sender cancels late (inside the cutoff): the traveler gets 10 percent of their base reward, rounded up, capped at 15.00 EUR; everything else goes to the sender; Wassla keeps nothing. Both are stored business settings shown read-only in the same table ("Late-cancellation compensation", default 10 percent; "Late-cancellation compensation cap", default 15.00 EUR); no console form changes them. The boost bonus is not counted. No agreed pickup time means treated as early.
  - Example: reward 20.00, collected 25.00, cancelled late: compensation 2.00, sender refunded 23.00. Reward 200.00: 10 percent is 20.00, capped to 15.00.
  - The values are frozen on the delivery at funding, so a later change in settings does not alter a quote.
- After pickup: no cancellation. "This parcel has already been picked up. Open a dispute instead." (`cancellation_not_available_after_pickup`).
- Other refusals: `use_pre_funding_cancellation` ("This delivery has not been funded yet; use the standard cancel action."), `deal_not_cancellable` ("This delivery can no longer be cancelled."), `not_authorized` ("Only a party to this delivery can cancel it.").
- After a funded cancellation: status `refunded` (full refund), `partially_refunded` (compensation paid) or `cancelled` (nothing collected). Space is released, live codes cancelled, match and request cancelled (the sender must post a new request and pay a new deposit). Both people get an email.
- A cancellation in progress while pickup is confirmed: whoever commits first wins; the other is refused.

## What can the AI helper do in this area?
| Action | Mode | Needs | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `note.add` | Direct (runs at once) | `view_deals` for a delivery, `view_disputes` for a dispute, a person-page capability for a person | Adds an internal note, up to 2,000 characters. Notes cannot be edited or deleted. Staff only. |
| `problem.acknowledge` | Direct | `manage_disputes` | Acknowledges a report that is `open` or `escalated`; needs a reason (max 500). Both people then see that staff are on it. |
| `dispute.set_status` | Prepared: admin confirms on the console | `manage_disputes` | Moves an active dispute to open, awaiting evidence or under review. |
| `dispute.resolve` | Prepared | `resolve_disputes` | Resolution `full_sender_refund`, `full_traveler_payout` or `partial_split` (with `sender_refund_eur_cents`, optional `traveler_payout_eur_cents`). Shows the planner preview; if amounts change by the time of confirmation it says "This no longer applies". |
| `deal.record_no_show` | Prepared | `record_no_show` | Records `party` sender or traveler; the reason fills the note (max 255). |
- The helper never closes a problem report; staff close it on the console.
- The helper never cancels a delivery, opens a dispute or answers for members.
- Refusals use the console's own rules (for example "This report is not waiting for staff", "This dispute is already finished.").

## Is a traveler's cancellation or no-show held against them?
- Nothing scores it. Ranking uses a fixed neutral reputation and no reliability signal.
- What is kept: who cancelled each delivery and any no-show decision. The person's console page shows "No-shows recorded" and "Cancelled by this person" counts.

## Not covered here
- Exact DZD amounts shown for refunds (DZD conversion is server-calculated at payment; see money.md).


==== NOTES: knowledge/reviews.md ====

# Ratings and Reviews

Covers: who can rate whom and when, the blind review window, what profiles and trips show, reporting a review, and how staff moderate reported reviews (console page and helper actions).
Related files: deliveries.md (delivery states, protection window, completion), trips-and-matching.md (Find Travelers, public profiles, listings), problems-and-disputes.md, admin-console.md (roles and permissions, audit log), settings-and-helper.md (settings page, how prepared helper actions are confirmed)

"Rating" and "review" mean the same thing here: a score from 1 to 5 plus optional tags and an optional comment, written about the other person on one delivery.

## Who can rate whom, and how many ratings per delivery?
- Only the two parties of a delivery (a "deal"): the sender rates the traveler, the traveler rates the sender.
- The role, the rater and the person rated are read from the delivery. Nobody can rate on someone else's behalf. A guest who only paid for the order, the parcel's recipient and unrelated users cannot rate (error `not_authorized`, "Only the sender and the traveler of this delivery may rate it.").
- Exactly one rating per side per delivery. Two ratings at most per delivery.
- A rating can never be edited or deleted after it is sent. Staff moderation does not edit it either (see below).
- You cannot rate yourself.

## When can a delivery be rated?
- Only after delivery has been confirmed. The delivery must be in one of these statuses: `delivery_confirmed`, `protection_window`, `completed`, `disputed`, `partially_refunded`. A disputed or partly refunded delivery can still be rated on purpose, so bad deliveries are not left out.
- Not before: any earlier status gives `rating_not_available` "This delivery cannot be rated yet."
- The review window opens when delivery is confirmed and lasts 14 days (1,209,600 seconds). After it closes: `rating_window_closed` "The review window for this delivery has closed." Nobody can add a rating then.
- The window length is a stored business setting (default 14 days in the shipped settings; allowed 1 hour to 365 days). The console Business settings page shows it read-only as "Rating review window" in the "Lifecycle guardrails" table; no console form changes it. Each delivery freezes its own window when delivery is confirmed, and each rating freezes the window end when it is sent. A later settings change does not move deadlines already promised.
- Old deliveries without a stored deadline use their own frozen policy value, default 14 days.
- Sending twice: `rating_already_submitted` "You have already rated this delivery." The app treats this as success and shows "Thanks for the rating".

## What is in a rating? Score, tags, comment limits
- Score: whole number 1 to 5. Anything else gives `rating_score_invalid`.
- Tags (optional): only these eight are accepted: communication, punctual, careful_handling, clear_instructions, friendly, late, poor_communication, damaged. Duplicates are removed. An unknown tag is refused with `rating_tag_invalid` "That rating tag is not offered." The list is a business setting, not fixed in the app; the app takes it from the server.
- Comment (optional): up to 1,000 characters by default (a stored business setting with no console form; the server cuts longer text). Absolute outer limit 4,000.
- The app shows tags with underscores as spaces.

## What does the rater see in the app?
- On the delivery screen, a "Leave a rating" action appears while the viewer can still rate. Prompt: "Rate the traveler" (sender) or "Rate the sender" (traveler). Home shows "Rate this delivery" as an attention item.
- The rate screen is titled "How did it go?" and says: "Neither of you sees the other's rating until you've both left one, or the window closes."
- After sending: "Rating saved". If the other side has not rated: "Your rating is saved. You'll see theirs once they've rated you too." If the window is closed: "The rating window has closed."
- If the other side already rated, the delivery screen says "Hidden until you both rate".

## What is the blind window? When does the other person see my rating?
- Ratings are blind. The other party cannot read a rating until either (a) both sides have rated, or (b) the review window of that rating has passed.
- Both-sides case: the moment the second rating is saved, both become visible to each other.
- The rater can always read their own rating. The delivery's rating state only says whether the other side "has submitted", never what they wrote. The delivery timeline event for a rating never carries the score.
- Visibility is computed from this rule, not from a stored flag. A background job (`rating_reveal`) stamps `revealed_at` when the window closes, but a late job cannot keep a rating hidden, and an early job cannot show one early.
- Staff can read all ratings of a delivery.
- Rating states on a delivery: `available` (you can rate), `submitted_waiting` (you rated, yours not yet visible), `revealed` (yours is visible), `expired` (window over, you did not rate), `unavailable` (not ratable yet).

## What is "countable"? (the one rule behind every average and count)
A rating is countable when BOTH are true:
1. It is revealed (both sides rated, or its window has passed), and
2. Staff have not removed it.
Every public average, rating count and review list uses only countable ratings. Nothing else is counted anywhere public (cards, Find Travelers, ranking, trip cards, profile, the reviews lists).
- A rating whose comment staff hid is still countable: only the text goes, score and tags still count.
- A rating still inside the blind window (other side not yet rated) is not countable yet.
- Ratings of non-completed outcomes are not filtered by status: a rating on a disputed delivery counts like any other once revealed.

## How is the average calculated, and when does a profile show "New"?
- Average = plain mean of the countable scores, rounded to one decimal (for example scores 5, 4, 4 give 4.3). The count is the number of countable ratings.
- With no countable rating the card shows state `new` with no average and count 0. The app shows the word "New" on traveler cards, Find Travelers results and request candidate cards.
- The member's own rating: the server answers `GET /api/users/me/ratings/summary` with `{state, average, count}`, the same numbers as their public profile card (countable ratings, both sides), so a removed rating is left out. Own profile screen: the "Rating" fact shows the server's summary (no arithmetic on the phone): "No ratings yet" instead of "New", else the average with a star. It is the same figure as the public card, so a removed rating is left out.
- As traveler vs whole person: a trip card, trip reviews and Find Travelers use only ratings written BY SENDERS about the traveler ("as a traveler"). A public profile card uses the whole person: ratings from both sides (as traveler and as sender).
- Example: a person with 3 countable sender ratings 5, 5, 4 shows 4.7 (3 reviews) on a trip card. If their profile also has one traveler-written rating of 2, the profile card shows (5+5+4+2)/4 = 4.0 (4 reviews).

## What else does a profile or trip card show (other trust signals)?
- Public profile card (spec "4.2"): first name only, `identity_verified` (an approved ID check that has not expired), member since (year-month), rating (average and count, or "New"), `completed_deliveries`, usual routes (cities only).
- Completed deliveries = deliveries with status `completed`. For a trip card it counts only deals the person carried as traveler; for a profile card, deals as traveler plus as sender. Journeys published, offers sent or matches do not count, only completed deals.
- Trip card traveler block: first name, identity verified (always true on a listed trip, because only travelers with a current ID check are listed), rating as traveler, completed deliveries.
- Reviews never show the author's name, any user id, the delivery or its tracking number. Each review shows: score, tags, comment (or "Comment removed by Wassla"), "From a sender" or "From a traveler", the month written (year-month) and whether the viewer reported it.

## Who can read a person's public reviews?
Two endpoints, 20 reviews per page by default, at most 50 per page, offset up to 1,000, newest first.
1. Person reviews (profile, both sides): readable only when marketplace "Show public profiles on Home" is on AND the person has a visible profile (chose to be discoverable, not hidden by staff, account active and not banned). Otherwise the app shows "Reviews are not available" ("Reviews are switched off for now. Please check back later.").
2. Trip reviews (sender-written ratings of the trip's traveler). Allowed in this order:
   - the trip's owner, or anyone who shares a match with the owner: always;
   - a sender coming from Find Travelers who names their own OPEN delivery request, when the matching engine finds that trip a compatible candidate: allowed even if the listings switch is off. Any failure of this path answers exactly like an unknown trip (`trip_not_found`, "This trip is not available.") so it reveals nothing;
   - otherwise the public path: only if the listings switch is on and the trip is listed now.
3. If the listings switch is off and no other door applies, trip reviews show "Reviews are not available".
4. A visitor without an account reads both lists on the public paths only: person reviews as in 1; trip reviews only while the listings switch is on and the trip is listed now. A visitor naming a request gets `trip_not_found`, like an unknown trip. "Whether the viewer reported it" is always empty for a visitor.
- Gone: "This is not available any more" / "The person or trip is no longer listed, so its reviews cannot be shown."
- No reviews yet: "No reviews yet. A review shows here once a delivery has been rated and the review is open to both sides."
- A member's own received ratings are always readable on Profile, "Ratings". The person reviews list above follows the profiles switch even for the owner.
- The listings switch ("Show trips and parcel requests on Home") and the profiles switch ("Show public profiles on Home") are in Settings, section "Marketplace features". Both default to on. They apply at the moment of each request; they change nothing already written.
- Note: a trip is "listed now" only while it passes listing rules (current ID check, not banned, has a future departure with capacity left, every needed ticket approved, sea legs only if the sea switch is on and crossings active).

## Where do users see their own received ratings?
- Profile, "Ratings" (My ratings): every revealed rating the person received, newest first, up to 100. Not-yet-revealed ratings are absent.
- Summary on top: average with count, from the ratings not removed by staff.
- A hidden comment: the rated person still reads their own rating as written, plus a note "Wassla hid the comment from others. The score still counts."
- A removed rating is still listed to them, marked "Removed by Wassla, not counted", and is not in their summary. They cannot report it.
- Empty: "No ratings yet. Ratings appear after a delivery is completed."
- Reporting is offered from the three-dots menu ("Report") on each review that is not removed and not already reported by them.

## Who can report a review, and how?
- Any member who can read the review where the app shows it, or the person it is about.
- Reasons (exactly five): Offensive, Contains personal information, Not about this delivery, Untrue, Other. App wording: "Offensive or abusive", "Shares personal information", "Not about the delivery", "Not true", "Something else". Codes: `offensive`, `personal_information`, `unrelated`, `untrue`, `other`.
- Optional note, up to 500 characters. Staff read it; nobody else does.
- Once per person per review. A second report answers with the first one's state (200), nothing new is stored.
- What the reporter sees: "received" (staff not yet looked), then "reviewed" (staff decided). App text: "You reported this. We have received it." then "You reported this. We have reviewed it." They never learn the outcome.
- The author is never told who reported. Staff see the reporter's name and email.
- Sheet text: "Tell us what is wrong. We will look at it, and the person is not told that you reported it." After sending: "Thanks, we will review it".
- Refusals:
  - own review: `own_review` 409 "You cannot report your own review."
  - review does not exist, is still blind, was removed, or the viewer cannot read it: `review_not_found` 404 "This review is not available." (the same answer for all, so the route cannot be used to probe ratings)
  - the rated person reporting a review staff already removed: `review_removed` 409 "Wassla already removed this review."
  - reason missing or not one of five: 400 "Choose a reason."
  - settings unavailable: `reports_unavailable` 503 "Reporting is unavailable."
- A sender who reached a traveler's review from Find Travelers through their own open request can report it even when public listings are off (the app sends the trip and the request together).

## Where do staff handle reported reviews?
- Console page "Reported reviews" (menu group "Verification", URL path trust/reviews/). Needs the permission `moderate_ratings` ("Can moderate ratings"); roles that have it: Trust and Super admin. Both the list and each review page need it; staff without it get no page.
- Work list queue "reviews" (Reported reviews): same permission; due today, urgent after 72 hours of waiting (nothing is blocked while it waits).
- Queue columns: Review (stars, score/5, comment excerpt), About (rated person, rated by the sender or traveler), Tracking number, Reports (count and reasons, most frequent first, like "Untrue x2"), Last report (age), State.
- Order: open reports first, longest waiting at the top, then decided ones. Filters: "Open reports", "Decided". Search: tracking number, participant email, comment text. Empty: "No review is waiting" / "No reported reviews found".
- State cell: "N open reports", "Comment hidden", "Rating removed", or "Reports dismissed".
- Review page (Review, parcel WSL-...): the review as written (score, tags, comment, who wrote it, who it is about, "Visible to members" yes/not yet), the standing decision with who decided, when, and the reason; every report with reporter, reason, note, status and decision; the audit history (only for staff with `view_audit_log`); a link "Open the delivery" (needs `view_deals`). A person's own page also has a "Ratings" tab (needs `view_ratings` or `moderate_ratings`) showing received average, count and how many were removed. `view_ratings` is held by Ops, Support and Super admin.

## What decisions can staff make on a review?
Four decisions; each needs a written reason (required, up to 500 characters) that goes in the audit log and stays internal, never shown to members.

| Decision (button) | What happens | Audit action |
|---|---|---|
| Dismiss the reports | Review unchanged. Open reports become `dismissed`. | `rating.reports_dismissed` |
| Hide the comment | Text replaced by "Comment removed by Wassla". Score and tags still count. Open reports become `actioned`. | `rating.comment_hidden` |
| Remove the rating | Leaves every public average, count and list. The rated person still sees it marked "Removed by Wassla, not counted". Open reports become `actioned`. | `rating.removed` |
| Lift the decision | Removes the standing hide or removal; the review is shown and counted as written again. Open reports become `dismissed`. | `rating.moderation_lifted` |

- At most one standing decision per review (hidden comment or removed). Hiding after remove (or the reverse) replaces the earlier decision (it switches state); the audit log keeps the history.
- Every decision closes all of the review's open reports at once, so reporters' "received" becomes "reviewed". A report filed during the decision stays open for the next one.
- The rating row itself is never edited or deleted.
- Refusals (code, wording): `moderation_unchanged` "This review has no open report to dismiss." / "No decision stands on this review." / "The comment is already hidden." / "The rating is already removed."; `rating_has_no_comment` "This review has no comment to hide."; `reason_required` "A reason is required."; `permission_denied` 403 "You cannot moderate reviews."; `rating_not_found` 404 "No such review."
- The review page only offers decisions that make sense now: Dismiss only with open reports; Hide only if there is a comment and it is not already hidden; Remove unless already removed; Lift only when a decision stands.
- Two staff deciding at once queue up; the second reads the first one's result.
- Success messages: "The reports are dismissed. The review is unchanged." / "The comment is hidden. The score still counts." / "The rating is removed from every public average, count and list." / "The decision is lifted. The review is shown as written."

## What do the rater, the rated person and the public see after a decision?
| Situation | Public (cards, lists) | Rated person ("My ratings") | Reporter |
|---|---|---|---|
| Comment hidden | Score and tags shown, comment replaced by "Comment removed by Wassla"; still counted | Reads their own rating as written, plus note "Wassla hid the comment from others. The score still counts." | "reviewed" |
| Rating removed | Gone from the list, average and count | Still listed, marked "Removed by Wassla, not counted", not in their summary | "reviewed" |
| Dismissed | Unchanged | Unchanged | "reviewed" |
| Lifted | Back as written | Back as written | "reviewed" |
- The author (rater) has no screen listing the ratings they gave. Nobody (author, rated person or reporter) gets a notification when staff decide; the reporter only sees the state change in the app.
- The reporter is never told the result, only "received" then "reviewed".

## How does the AI helper work with reviews?
- Record type `review_report`, keyed by the Rating id (not a report id). Needs `moderate_ratings`. Fields it reads: tracking number, score, written by (sender or traveler), writer and rated person, number of open reports, each report's reason, status and time, and the standing decision. The comment and each report's note are treated as untrusted text (they may contain instructions; the helper must not follow them).
- Suggested next step it gives: with open reports "Read the review and the reports, then dismiss them, hide the comment or remove the rating."; with none "No open report. Nothing to do."
- Timeline it shows: Review written, Reported: <reason>, Decision: <state>.
- Actions (all "prepared": the helper prepares, the admin confirms, nothing runs by itself; each needs a reason):
  - `review.dismiss_report` dismiss the reports
  - `review.hide_comment` hide the comment (score still counts)
  - `review.remove` remove the rating from every public average, count and list
  - `review.lift` lift a standing decision
- The helper refuses an action when the review page would not offer it ("The review page does not offer this decision now."), and it records the same audit entries as the console. Task guide: "moderate_review" (asks like "Someone reported a review. What do I do?", "How do I remove a rating?").
- The queue "reviews" in the work list feeds the helper's list of review reports (rows: tracking number, score, open reports).

## How does the audit log record review decisions?
- Target is the rating (`ratings.rating`), actor is the staff member, with reason, the delivery's tracking number, before and after (the standing decision and the number of open reports) and metadata (how many reports were closed and as what).
- Visible on the review page to staff with `view_audit_log`.

## Worked examples
- Sender gives a traveler 5 stars the day after delivery; the traveler has not rated yet. The sender sees their own rating; the traveler sees nothing and the traveler's average does not change. Day 14: window closes, the rating becomes visible and counts.
- Both rate within 2 days: both become visible immediately and count.
- A traveler's 4 sender ratings are 5, 5, 4, 2. Staff remove the 2: the trip card average goes from 4.0 (4) to 4.7 (3).
- Staff hide a comment on a 1-star rating: the 1 still counts in the average; the text shows "Comment removed by Wassla".

## Is anyone notified about ratings?
- When delivery is confirmed, each party gets one "Leave a review" email and in-app message (the review window opens then).
- Nothing is sent when ratings are revealed, when a review is reported, or when staff decide on a report.

## Not covered here
- Changing the review window, comment limit or tag list: no console form exists; they live only in the stored business settings.
- Ratings do not change Find Travelers ranking; see trips-and-matching.md for ranking.


==== NOTES: knowledge/settings-and-helper.md ====

# Business settings and the AI helper

Covers: every business setting an admin can see or change (names, meanings, defaults, limits, who may change them, how a change is saved) and how the admin AI helper works (keys, what it reads, the 21 actions, confirmation, expiry, errors).
Related files: money.md, pricing.md, deliveries.md, problems-and-disputes.md, trips-and-matching.md, admin-console.md, reviews.md

Important for the assistant: the helper has NO endpoint that reads business settings. Values below are the defaults seeded by the migrations. An admin may have changed them since. For a live value say "the default is X; check Settings, then Business settings on the console".

## Who can see and change business settings?
- Console page: Settings, then Business settings. Page name in the task list: "Change the business settings".
- Seeing the page needs the permission `view_settings`. Changing anything needs `manage_settings`.
- Only the Super Admin role holds these two permissions. Ops, Support, Finance and Trust / Verification do not.
- Without `manage_settings` the forms are shown greyed out, and a save is refused: "Business settings management permission is required."
- The AI helper can never change settings (not even the AI helper switch). Settings is on its "never" list.
- Sea crossings (Geography catalogue) also need `manage_settings`.

## How is a settings change saved, activated and audited?
- Settings are versions. Each save creates a NEW version (version number = latest + 1) and activates it at once. The old active one becomes retired.
- Exactly one version is active at any time. A retired version can never be activated again.
- A saved version can never be edited or deleted. Values are frozen at creation.
- Each console form copies the active version, changes only its own keys, validates the whole result, then saves. Every form needs a reason (max 500 characters) and a confirm tick.
- Validation runs all the strict readers (matching, payments, lifecycle, marketplace, AI helper). A bad value is refused and nothing is saved.
- The staff API refuses any setting name containing secret, password, token, private_key or webhook ("Provider credentials and secrets are environment-only."). The console forms only write fixed names.
- Activation also refuses pricing distance bands finer than the public distance privacy ladder (100, 300, 750, 1500, 3000, 5000 km maxima, then open). Finer bands would leak location.
- Audit: one audit entry "settings.version_created" with who, reason, before and after values.
- New values apply from the next request. They bind only FUTURE things. A deal, offer or payment already made keeps the numbers it was frozen with (handover, protection window, cancellation terms, review window are copied onto a deal when it is funded).
- A staff API also exists: list the last 50 versions (`view_settings`) and create a version (`manage_settings`). Create takes a full policy object, a commission in basis points, a pricing version label, "activate" and a reason (max 500). Sent without "activate", the version is saved as a draft, and no route activates a draft later.
- A missing or unreadable active version makes money and lifecycle actions refuse (fail closed, a 503-style "settings unavailable" answer).

## Which forms does the Settings page have?
| Form (section on the page) | What it saves |
|---|---|
| Commission, deposit and floors | Commission %, posting deposit %, deposit min, deposit max, minimum traveler reward, weight rate per kg, recommendation multiplier |
| Chargily exchange rate | DZD for 1 EUR |
| Provider availability | Stripe on/off, Chargily on/off, Chargily new checkouts on/off |
| Discovery, routes, prices and proposals | The marketplace switches (below) |
| Admins' own AI assistants | The AI helper switch and its four numbers |
| Boost commission | Wassla commission on boosts |
The page also shows read-only "guardrails" (delivery-code buffer, payout protection window, payment grace, cancellation cutoff and compensation, rating window, dispute evidence limit, checkout and guest-link lifetimes).
